Day 2, 13 November 20264 entries

13 Nov Repo setupCloning the jam repo locallyAndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy

Question

How do we get the most recent remote main of orion-eighty-one/orion-81-space-ctf-epic-jam into the local folder D:\orion-81-space-cft-epic-jam while getting GitHub MCP working?

Decision

Clone main over HTTPS with plain git into the existing empty folder. GitHub MCP isn't needed for the clone, so it stays a separate setup task.

Outcome

  • Cloned main at fded4f5 (merge of PR #2 "Make .gitignore cover the Unreal project in its subfolder", Andrius, 2026-11-13 19:49 UTC).
  • Local HEAD matches origin/main.
  • Repo contents: .gitattributes (Git LFS for .uasset, .umap, art, audio, fonts), .gitignore (covers the Unreal project in orion_81_space_ctf/), README.md.
  • Git 2.56.1 and Git LFS 3.8.1 are installed. The GitHub CLI (gh) is not installed, and no GitHub MCP server is connected in this Claude session yet.

Still open

  • The local folder is spelled cft but the repo is ctf. Keep the folder name or rename it before the Unreal project paths get baked into tooling?
  • GitHub MCP: which setup (remote GitHub MCP server via OAuth, or local server with a personal access token), and who on the team needs it?

Coach tip (topic: Git setup for a 3-person Unreal team)

Add lockable to the .uasset and .umap LFS lines in .gitattributes, and lock maps with git lfs lock before editing them. Binary assets can't be merged, so if Andy, Andrius and Zilvinas all touch the CTF map on Saturday, two of them will lose their work.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: session default
  • Used for: cloning the repo, checking it against the remote, writing this devlog note
13 Nov ToolingGitHub MCP for Claude CodeAndy

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy (go-ahead). Claude chose the setup details below as defaults, so the team still needs to review them.

Question

Should we set up GitHub MCP so Claude can work with the jam repo's PRs, issues and CI directly?

Decision

Yes. Use GitHub's official remote MCP server, configured once in a shared .mcp.json at the repo root.

How it's set up

  • No GitHub connector exists in the claude.ai registry, so the server is configured in Claude Code itself.
  • Server: https://api.githubcopilot.com/mcp/ over HTTP. Auth is a personal access token sent as Bearer ${GITHUB_PAT}.
  • The token is read from each person's GITHUB_PAT environment variable, so .mcp.json holds no secret and can be committed for all three of us.
  • Only the repos, issues, pull_requests and actions toolsets are enabled, which keeps Claude's context small.
  • .env and .env.* were added to .gitignore as a safeguard.
  • Claude Code 2.1.318 reads the config (claude mcp get github). It shows as pending approval until someone trusts it.

Verification (2026-11-13, Andy's machine)

  • Connected. GitHub MCP listed all three PRs (#1, #2, #3), all merged by Andrius. #3 adds the empty Unreal project skeleton.
  • Listing branches failed with 403 "Resource not accessible by personal access token", which means the token lacks the Contents permission.
  • Lessons from setup: the first attempt failed because GITHUB_PAT wasn't set. The second got a 401 because only the github_pat_ prefix had been saved. After either fix, fully restart the app.

Still open

  • Add Contents (read and write) to Andy's token, and include it from the start for Andrius's and Zilvinas's tokens.
  • Done (Andy's decision): .mcp.json and the .gitignore change are on branch chore/github-mcp-config as PR #4 (https://github.com/orion-eighty-one/orion-81-space-ctf-epic-jam/pull/4). The PR was opened through GitHub MCP, which confirms the token's Pull requests write access works. GitHub user AndroidBeacshire merged it into main (9c7bcfc) at 21:51 UTC.
13 Nov Branchingfeat/v0 for game v0.0.0AndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy

Question

Where do we build v0.0.0 of the game?

Decision

On a new branch, feat/v0, cut from the latest main.

Outcome

  • feat/v0 was created at 9c7bcfc, the PR #4 merge. It includes the UE 5.8 project skeleton and the shared GitHub MCP config.
  • Pushed to GitHub (Andy's decision) and tracking origin/feat/v0. Andrius and Zilvinas can now branch from it.
  • Local main was fast-forwarded to match origin/main.
  • The local .uproject has an uncommitted change: EngineAssociation went from "5.8" to a machine-specific engine ID. Unreal writes this when the project is linked to a locally registered engine build. Don't commit it, because it would break the project for the other two machines.

Still open

  • What's in scope for v0.0.0? Suggested minimum: one ship flying in 6-DoF, one map with obstacles, two flags, capture and score, and the M key map. AI teams come in v0.1.

Coach tip (topic: branching on a 3-day jam)

Keep feat/v0 short-lived. Each person works on a small branch off it (flight, map, flags) and merges back at least twice a day. Long-lived branches with Unreal binary assets can't be merged, so whoever merges last ends up redoing work.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: session default
  • Used for: fetching, checking new commits and the local .uproject change, creating the branch, this devlog entry
  • Done (Andy's decision): pulled PR #3 into the local clone with a fast-forward to 1854020. The C++ project orion_81_space_ctf targets UE 5.8 and has no LFS assets yet.

Coach tip (topic: AI tooling during a jam)

Give the PAT access to orion-81-space-ctf-epic-jam only and set it to expire on 2026-11-17. An agent with org-wide write access is more risk than a 3-day jam needs, and the expiry means nobody has to remember to revoke it.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: session default

13 Nov PortOrion81 prototype into the jam repo, CTF removedAndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy (port the prototype and drop unwanted features). Claude chose what counted as "unwanted" from the jam brief, so the team needs to review the list below.

Question

Take the features built in the local prototype (Documents/Unreal Projects/Orion81, never in git) and move them into the jam project orion_81_space_ctf/, dropping anything we no longer want.

Decision: kept (ported, renamed to the orion_81_space_ctf module)

  • 6DOF fighter: flight assist, boost, catapult launch, shields over hull, collision damage. Controls are generated at runtime, so no editor setup is needed.
  • Weapons: kinetic, energy (was "plasma", renamed to match the brief) and homing missiles. One pooled projectile manager handles every shot.
  • 12 vs 12 single player against bots, with football-style roles: defenders, patrol and attackers.
  • Respawn in the home capital ship's launch tube, then relaunch.
  • Canvas HUD, kill feed, and the M tactical map showing both capital ships and every fighter.
  • Procedural space backdrop: stars, sun, planet and moon, plus the asteroid field.
  • Scripts/setup_space_scene.py: builds M_SpaceSurface, M_SpaceShield and the SpaceArena map.

Decision: shaken out

  • Capture the flag, entirely. That covers the flag actor, flag pad, captures-to-win, flag HUD and markers, and bot flag logic. The brief's win condition is destroying the enemy capital ship.
  • Ball-shaped asteroids. The prototype used spheres for 65% of its rocks. The brief says "never ball-shaped", so they're all squashed, rotated boxes until real rock meshes arrive.
  • Prototype binary assets. The map and materials referenced the old /Script/Orion81 module. Rather than carry redirects, they're regenerated by the setup script in the new project.
  • Editor-only junk: .vs, .sln, Binaries, Intermediate, DerivedDataCache, Saved and editor layouts.

Decision: added so the loop still closes without CTF

  • Capital ship damage (jam scope): a 20,000 HP health bar plus 4 glowing weak points (bridge, port reactor, starboard reactor, engine core). Any fighter hit on the hull reduces health. Weak-point hits count ×3. Each weak point pulses faster as it weakens, then explodes and costs 8% extra health. At 0 HP the capital ship blows up in a 4-second chain of explosions and goes dark.
  • Win condition: destroy the enemy capital ship → VICTORY / DEFEAT screen → the level restarts after 12 s. Fallback: after 15 minutes, the side with more capital ship health wins.
  • Bots: attackers fly strike runs at the nearest enemy weak point, then break off and come round again. Defenders guard a 300 m bubble around their own capital ship.
  • HUD: both capital ships' health bars and weak-point counts, an "under attack" warning, weak-point brackets on the enemy capital ship, and win/defeat text.
  • Dev cheat: OrionDamageCarrier <team> <fraction> in the console, for testing the win screen fast.

Still open (team)

  1. Capital ships are still the prototype's ~260 m primitive carrier, and the brief says ~5 km. Do we scale up now, or wait for Zilvinas's model?
  2. Bomb ships (Model 1) don't exist yet. Attackers use guns and dumb-fired missiles on weak points. Bombers plus escorts is the next AI step.
  3. The "disabled at 30% hull" mechanic only existed to make flag carriers drop the flag. Keep it as a combat feature, or cut it?
  4. Damage numbers (20,000 HP, ×3 on weak points, 8% penalty) are first guesses. Tune them after one full bot match.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: high (set with /effort at the start of the session)
  • Used for: reading the prototype, porting and renaming the C++, removing CTF, writing the capital ship damage model, strike-run bots and HUD, and this devlog entry

Coach tip (topic: porting prototype code during a jam)

Port into a branch, not straight onto feat/v0. A port touches every file, so a single reviewable PR lets Andrius and Zilvinas see exactly what changed in their game before they build on it.

Day 3, 14 November 202635 entries

14 Nov Core loop verified headlessBombers + escorts, launch-lane fixesAndyOpus 5.5

Decided by: Andy (keep going on the build: closed the editor so it could compile). Claude diagnosed the bugs and chose the fixes, using the jam brief's "attack squad (bombers + escorts)". The team should review the bomber/escort split.

Question

Does the full loop (launch, fly, fight, damage and destroy the enemy capital ship, win screen) actually close in a real build?

What the first test showed

  • Headless bot match (UnrealEditor-Cmd -game -nullrhi) with red's capital ship cheated down to 10%. In 2.5 minutes no blue shot ever hit it, so the match never ended.
  • Diagnostic logs showed two causes:
    1. Attackers never committed. Any enemy within 5 km pulled them into a dogfight, and red's defenders are always there. Blue had 4 attackers to red's 5, because the player takes a blue slot.
    2. Bots hovered in front of obstacles. Avoidance pushed straight back off a hit, which cancels a head-on goal, so bots froze for up to 100 s.

Decision: fixes

  • New Bomber role. The attack squad now alternates bomber and escort (blue 2+2, red 3+2). Bombers fly strike runs and ignore fighters. Escorts intercept anything within 12 km. This is a stand-in for Model 1 (bomb ship) until it and real bombs exist.
  • Avoidance slides along obstacles instead of backing off them, and a stuck detector sends any bot below 1,500 u/s for 2.5 s on a 4-second sideways detour.
  • Launch lanes kept clear of asteroids where they enter the field (6 km radius, LaunchLaneRadius).
  • Capital ship health now logs each 10% band (LogOrionCapital). Bot diagnostics are opt-in: log LogOrionBot Verbose.
  • A weak-point kill by a dead pilot's shot now reads "RED squadron" instead of "Someone".

Verified

  • Red at 10% → destroyed by Blue-09 1 min 50 s in → BLUE TEAM WINS → level restarts → second match running, with weak points dying (BLUE BRIDGE at 56%).
  • From full health, bots alone take a capital ship to about 55% in 4 minutes, so a bots-only match runs roughly 8–10 minutes, inside the 15-minute limit.
  • Known test artifact: in headless runs the idle player ship parks in blue's launch lane and bots detour around it. With a human flying, this doesn't happen.

Still open (team)

  1. Bombs: Model 1 still needs a bomb weapon. Bombers use guns and dumb-fired missiles for now.
  2. Nothing committed yet. Done: Andy decided to commit on branch feat/capital-ship-loop and opened PR #5 into feat/v0 (25 files, assets via LFS; Andy's machine-specific EngineAssociation GUID left out).
  3. Not yet tested with a human at the controls in a real window, and not yet packaged.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: high (session default, set earlier with /effort)
  • Used for: reading logs, adding diagnostics, 4 rebuild-and-test cycles, the AI and asteroid fixes, this entry

Coach tip (topic: verifying the loop)

Turn this test into a one-line smoke script that fails unless the log says TEAM WINS, and run it before every merge into feat/v0. It found two loop-breaking bugs in 20 minutes that nobody would have spotted from a single playtest.

14 Nov Tooling and artRodinBridge for UE 5.8, and a NASA skybox with the "Orion-81 nebula"AndriusFable 5.2Opus 5.5

Who decided: Andrius (all four decisions below)

Decision 1: RodinBridge as a local editor tool only

  • Hyper3D's RodinBridge v0.2.1 has an official UE 5.8 build (Unreal_5_8_RodinBridge_v0_2_1_Precomplied.zip, DeemosTech GitHub release UE5.8). Its build ID matches our engine (5.8.4, 55116800).
  • The repo has no licence file, so the plugin stays out of git and out of the packaged game. It is an editor-only module.
  • Lesson: installed in the project's Plugins/ folder it broke the project build, because Unreal recompiles project plugins that ship source and this one does not compile cleanly. It now lives in the engine's Engine/Plugins/Marketplace/Rodin/ on Andrius's PC, where precompiled plugins are loaded and never rebuilt.
  • Its WebSocket server listens on all network interfaces (port 8080). Keep it blocked for Private and Public networks in Windows Firewall.

Decision 2: Rodin is not used for the sky

  • Rodin only outputs 3D meshes. It cannot produce a panorama or an .exr. Planned use: 2 or 3 hero asteroids, after the first packaged build.

Decision 3: skybox composed only from NASA releases, at 8K

  • Base: NASA SVS Deep Star Maps 2020 (starmap_2020_8k.exr). Nebula: PIA01322 (Hubble + Spitzer) over PIA08653 (Spitzer), both of the real Orion Nebula (M42).
  • There is no real "Orion-81 nebula". M42 is drawn at its true sky position, enlarged from about 1 degree to about 46 degrees.
  • Terms: not copyrighted, commercial use allowed, credit requested, no NASA logo or implied endorsement. Not CC0. ESA/Hubble copies (CC BY 4.0) were avoided. Sources, hashes and the credit text are in CREDITS.md.
  • 8K (8192x4096, about 23 pixels per degree) chosen over 4K for sharpness at 1080p. Cost: a 130.6 MB texture asset in Git LFS.

Decision 4: nebula sits up and to the left of the Red capital ship

  • New AOrionSpaceEnvironment properties: SkyMaterial, NebulaDirection (default pitch 14, yaw -24) and SkyBrightness (13).

What was built

  • Scripts/build_skybox.py: projects the nebula images onto the star map and writes the .exr (9 s for 8K).
  • Scripts/setup_skybox.py: imports the texture (BC6H, no mips) and builds M_SpaceSky, which maps the sky by view direction.
  • AOrionSpaceEnvironment: an 80 km sky dome behind the procedural stars, planet and sun.

Verified

  • Editor target builds. In a windowed game run, stars fill the launch-tube opening and the nebula's edge shows at the upper left.
  • Not yet verified: the full sky from open space with a human flying, bloom on the nebula core, and a packaged build.

Still open

  1. HDR glow for the nebula core. Done: Andrius chose the moderate glow from a three-way comparison (none, about 5x white, about 13x white). hdr_boost now scales colour by 1 + 4 * luminance^3, so the core peaks at 4.87x white. Rebuilt and re-imported. The core itself has not been seen in game yet, because it is outside the launch-tube opening.
  2. The 4,500 procedural cube stars now sit in front of real NASA stars. Decide whether to reduce StarCount.
  3. First packaged build. Still not done.
  4. Also fixed this morning, uncommitted: M_SpaceSurface was missing the Instanced Static Meshes usage flag, which would have broken asteroids in a packaged build.

AI usage

  • Models: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5) for the fetch, build fix, material fix and RodinBridge install; Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2) for the skybox, switched by Andrius with /model
  • Effort: session default, not changed in this session
  • Used for: licence research, the two Python scripts, the C++ sky dome, headless import and an in-game screenshot

Coach tip (topic: third-party plugins)

Put code plugins you did not write in the engine's Plugins/Marketplace folder, not the project's Plugins folder. A project plugin with source is compiled into every build and every package, so one plugin that does not compile stops the whole team.

14 Nov First packaged buildDevelopment for WindowsAndriusFable 5.2

Who decided: Andrius (package now, straight after the skybox)

Result

  • BuildCookRun (build, cook, stage, pak, archive) succeeded in 2 min 20 s: 497 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings.
  • The packaged game starts straight in SpaceArena with OrionGameMode: HUD, both capital-ship health bars, asteroids, the NASA sky and the nebula all render. Output is about 1.06 GB.

Bug found by the package, and fixed

  • The packaged game logged Failed to find object '/Game/Space/Materials/M_SpaceShield'. Fighters are spawned at run time and load the shield material by path, so the cooker never saw a reference to it and left it out. In the editor everything worked.
  • Fix: DirectoriesToAlwaysCook=(Path="/Game/Space") in Config/DefaultGame.ini. The second package cooked 498 packages and the game log has no warnings or errors.

Builds (outside the repo, in D:\Games\2026 Epic Jam\Builds)

  • 2026-11-14_1143_dev: first package. Shields are invisible in this one.
  • 2026-11-14_1147_dev: with the shield fix. Use this one.

Not yet verified

  • The full loop with a human at the controls in the packaged build: launch, fly, fight, destroy the Red capital ship, win screen.
  • Shield flashes in the packaged build (the material is now included, but nobody has been hit yet).
  • A Shipping build, and the build on a second PC.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: session default, not changed in this session
  • Used for: running the package, reading the cook and game logs, the cook-directory fix, the smoke test screenshots

Coach tip (topic: packaging)

After every package, open the packaged game's own log (<build>\Windows\orion_81_space_ctf\Saved\Logs) and search for "Failed to find". Anything loaded by path in code and missing from the cook shows up there, and nowhere in the editor.

14 Nov GitConflict check on Zilvinas's ship models, main frozen, develop addedAndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (freezing main, adding develop, the approval rule, and a free team rule instead of a paid plan)

Question: does Zilvinas's latest commit conflict with today's other commits?

No.

  • Zilvinas's three commits (7e77ae2 adds the Ships folder, 93520e7 puts .glb and .gltf in LFS, ed53b17 adds four Meshy ship models) branched from the tip of main at the time (b6915f6). Nobody else touched .gitattributes or Content/Space/Ships/ today, so no file was changed on both sides.
  • A test merge (git merge-tree) came out clean. GitHub user MrFluffyKitten merged the commits as PR #8 (71ff5c2, 13:05 UTC).
  • The .gitattributes change only removes blank lines and adds the *.glb and *.gltf rules. All four models are stored as LFS pointers, 43 to 55 MB each and about 196 MB in total.

Decision: freeze main as the stable branch and add develop as the working branch

  • develop was created from main at 71ff5c2 and is now the repo's default branch, so new PRs target it automatically.
  • PRs into main, from feature branches or from develop: only Andrius approves, and Andrius merges.
  • PRs into develop: Andy or Zilvinas approve, the way main worked until today.
  • GitHub can't enforce this. The org is on the free plan, where branch protection and rulesets on private repos return 403. The options were GitHub Team ($4 per user per month, about $12 a month for three of us), making the repo public, or a team rule. Andrius chose the free team rule, now written in README.md and .github/pull_request_template.md.

Found during the check

  • This morning's packaging fixes (DirectoriesToAlwaysCook in DefaultGame.ini, the M_SpaceShield and M_SpaceSurface material fixes, setup_space_scene.py) are still uncommitted on Andrius's PC, on the already-merged feat/nasa-skybox branch. Neither main nor develop has them, so a package built from either has invisible shields and the asteroid material problem.
  • .gitattributes doesn't track .exr, .hdr, .blend or .jpg yet. The skybox .exr source sits outside the repo, so nothing has gone into plain git, but the next such file could.
  • The ship models are raw .glb files in Content/. Unreal packages only imported .uasset assets, so they don't appear in the game until they're imported.

Still open

  1. Commit the packaging fixes on a branch cut from develop and open a PR. Done (Andrius said go): the four files are on fix/packaging-cook-and-materials as PR #10 into develop (d132998). Both materials are in LFS.
  2. Add .exr, .hdr, .blend and .jpg to Git LFS. Deferred by Andrius on 14 Nov ("not now"). Until then, run git lfs track "*.<ext>" before committing one of these files.
  3. Confirm that GitHub user MrFluffyKitten is Zilvinas.
  4. Record which Meshy plan made the models, because Meshy ties the model licence to the plan. Add the credit to CREDITS.md if the licence needs one.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, Learning output style
  • Effort: session default
  • Used for: reading the git history and running the test merge, checking the GitHub plan and permissions, creating develop, this PR and this devlog note

Coach tip (topic: branching in a three-person jam)

Start every task with git fetch origin and git switch -c feat/<name> origin/develop, and stop committing to a branch once its PR is merged. That's how the packaging fixes above got stranded: they sit on a merged branch, so no PR will ever pick them up.

14 NovFirst packaged build, and an edit-here / test-in-Unreal loopAndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy (package now; set up editing from Claude Code and testing in Unreal). Claude chose the two config fixes and the MCP wiring below, so the team should review them before merging.

Question

Can the game package for Windows today, and can we edit code from the Claude Code window and test it straight away in the Unreal Editor?

Update (Andy's decision): packaging is set aside for now. The focus is testing in the Unreal Editor and making changes from Claude Code. The packaging notes below are kept for when packaging resumes before Sunday morning.

Packaging: outcome

  • Fresh clone of main (6f72793) in Documents/Unreal Projects/orion-81-space-jam.
  • Windows Development package built: orion_81_space_ctf/Saved/Packaged/Windows/orion_81_space_ctf.exe (about 1 GB).
  • Fix 1, .uproject: AllToolsets, ModelContextProtocol and Terminal limited to the Editor target. Without this, an MCP server and about 20 toolset plugins would ship inside the game.
  • Fix 2, Config/DefaultGame.ini: added an Asset Manager rule for GameFeatureData that points at an empty folder. Without it, the cook fails with 2 errors that come from the editor-only GameFeatures plugin. (The D: clone had already found the same fix but hadn't committed it.)
  • Smoke test (smoke-packaged.ps1 from the D: clone): the packaged game boots, loads SpaceArena, applies the 10% damage cheat, and the bots fight. It did not reach a win screen. The process got a clean shutdown request after 40 s (ConsoleCtrl RequestExit), not a crash. The cause hasn't been found yet.

Editor loop: how it works

  • The editor is launched with -ModelContextProtocolStartServer, which puts UE 5.8's built-in MCP server on http://127.0.0.1:8000/mcp. Auto-start is off by default.
  • From Claude Code: edit the C++, start and stop Play-in-Editor (StartPIE/StopPIE), read the output log (GetLogEntries) and capture the viewport.
  • Live Coding still needs a keypress in the editor (Ctrl+Alt+F11). No MCP tool can trigger it. Header or class-layout changes still need editor close → build → reopen.
  • unreal-mcp was added to the repo-root .mcp.json, so a Claude Code session opened at the repo root gets the Unreal tools natively.
  • Verified: PIE started from Claude Code, and the log showed "Destroy the RED capital ship. Hit its glowing weak points."

Still open (team)

  1. Find why the packaged build exits at 40 s, then get the smoke test to PASS.
  2. Commit the two packaging fixes and the smoke script on a branch.
  3. Should the MCP server auto-start for everyone (a shared config), or stay a per-person launch flag?
  4. Epic's MCP plugin warns that data it sends to an LLM is Licensed Technology under the UE EULA. Confirm the team is comfortable with that.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: session default
  • Used for: cloning, two packaging runs and diagnosing them, the smoke test, launching the editor with MCP, driving PIE over MCP, this entry

Coach tip (topic: the edit-test loop)

Keep changes small enough for Live Coding: tune values in .cpp files, and batch header changes into one editor restart. A 10-second loop gets you 50 tuning passes on Saturday. A 3-minute restart loop gets you 5.

14 Nov TargetingLead reticle ("pip") for gunsAndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy (add a reticle that shows where to shoot, based on the shooter's projectile speed and the target's velocity, on branch targeting-upgrades). Claude chose the details below: target selection, the look, and hiding it for missiles. The team should review them in a playtest.

What was built (all in OrionHUD.cpp, with no header changes, so Live Coding reloads it)

  • A ring with a centre dot that marks where to put the crosshair. It uses the selected weapon's own ProjectileSpeed, so it moves when you switch between kinetic and energy.
  • Rounds inherit the shooter's velocity, so the lead is solved on relative velocity: the smallest t > 0 where |RelPos + RelVel·t| = speed·t. This is exact, unlike the bots' one-step estimate.
  • The pip is drawn at the guns' convergence distance, so the crosshair on the pip means the nose is on the intercept, despite the chase camera.
  • Feedback: full weapon colour when in range, faded when the rounds would expire first (t > Lifetime), white with a thicker ring when the crosshair is on it..
  • Target: the missile-lock target if there is one, otherwise the enemy closest to the nose within 40° and 1.5× weapon range. Hidden when missiles are selected, because they home.

Verified

  • Compiled with Live Coding in the running editor (Live coding succeeded).
  • The intercept formula was checked in a quick simulation (crossing, head-on, same-course and outrunning targets): a round fired at the pip ends 0 cm from the target, and an outrunning target correctly gets no pip.
  • Not yet verified: how it looks and feels in a real dogfight. Claude can't see the in-game HUD from the editor's screenshot tool.

Still open

  1. Capital ship weak points: no pip yet. They're stationary, but the shooter's own velocity still pulls shots off.
  2. Bots could use the same exact solver instead of their one-step estimate, if they miss too much at long range.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: session default
  • Used for: reading the weapon and projectile code, the intercept solver and HUD drawing, the math check, this entry

Coach tip (topic: lead reticles)

Judge the pip against tracers, not kills. If a long burst at a crossing bot passes through where the bot is going, the math is done, and every remaining miss is a flying-skill problem, not a code problem.

Playtest result (Andy): tracers hit the target with the crosshair on the pip, so the aim works. Andy decided to remove the line that tied the pip to its target. The ring and centre dot stay.

Weak points too (Andy's decision): the pip now also covers the enemy capital ship's surviving weak points. Weak points are stationary, so their lead comes only from the attacker's own velocity, which the rounds inherit. Fighters and weak points share one candidate list, and whichever is closest to the nose (within 40° and 1.5× range) gets the single pip. This replaces the "prefer the missile-lock target" rule, which picked the nearest fighter to the nose anyway. Closes still-open item 1 above.

Playtest result, weak points (Andy): strafing shots with the crosshair on the pip hit the weak points. The lead reticle is done for fighters and weak points.

Direction arrow (Andy's decision): instead of the removed line, a small arrowhead now sits just outside the target and points at its pip. It only appears when the pip is more than 45 px from the target (at 1080p), so it helps when the lead is large and stays out of the way when it isn't. It uses the pip's colour, so it fades the same way out of range. Tuning: OrionAim::ArrowMinGap, ArrowOffset, ArrowSize in OrionHUD.cpp.

Playtest result, arrow (Andy): the arrow helps when the lead is large and doesn't obstruct the view. Kept as is.

Coach tip (topic: rebasing in an Unreal jam)

Close the editor before any git command that rewrites files. On Windows, Unreal keeps loaded assets locked, and a half-finished checkout costs far more time than a 30-second restart.

14 Nov Hyper3DBought for the skybox, not used yet; how to use it for assetsAndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (from now on, Hyper3D and Rodin are for asset generation). Which asset comes first is still his call.

Correction to the entry above

The team got Hyper3D in order to generate the skybox. The skybox session ruled Rodin out because Rodin only makes meshes, then built the sky from NASA images with our own script. It never used the Hyper3D MCP tools or Hyper3D's OmniCraft sky generator. So the tool we got for the sky made nothing in the game. Andrius pointed out that Claude bypassed the Hyper3D MCP.

What Hyper3D can make (from its MCP tool descriptions, 14 Nov)

  • Rodin Gen-2.5 (rodin_generate): a textured mesh from text or 1 to 5 images. Output is GLB, FBX or OBJ: 500 to 1,000,000 triangles in Raw mode, or 1,000 to 50,000 quads.
  • Rodin Agentic (rodin_generate_agentic, beta): hard-surface models built from editable n-gons, from 1 to 5 reference images.
  • BANG (rodin_generate_bang): splits a finished Rodin model into up to 12 parts.
  • PolyGen (rodin_generate_polygen): a low-poly copy (200 to 20,000 faces) of a Rodin model, with PBR textures. It only works on Rodin models, not on Meshy ones.
  • Rigging (rodin_generate_rigging): skeletons and animations. We have no characters, so we don't need it.
  • OmniCraft (web app, not in the MCP): HDRI sky panoramas plus an .exr lighting file.
  • Every generation uses credits.

Asset inventory and proposal

AssetIn the game todayHyper3D usePriority
Asteroids (450, field radius 700 m)One instanced mesh of squashed, rotated engine cubesRodin Gen-2.5: 4 to 6 rugged rock variants, PolyGen to 2,000 to 5,000 faces, simple convex collision1
Capital shipsAbout 260 m long, about 20 engine cubes and cylinders (the brief says about 5 km)Agentic or Gen-2.5 from Zilvinas's concept, as a visual-only shell over the existing collision parts. BANG splits it into hull sections for the stretch-goal destruction.2
DebrisNone. Explosions are glowing spheres.Rodin: 3 or 4 hull chunks at 500 to 1,500 faces, thrown out on kills and weak-point blasts3
Missiles and bombsInstanced cubesRodin: one missile and one bomb, 1,000 to 2,000 faces4
SkyNASA 8K sky, works in the packageOmniCraft only as polish, after the loop5
Fighters and bombersBuilt from primitives. Zilvinas's 4 Meshy models aren't imported yet.No Rodin. Import the Meshy models; PolyGen can't reduce them.none
Weak pointsGlowing 9 m spheresKeep them: they need to read clearly in combatnone
Planet, sun, 4,500 starsEngine spheres and cubesNo Rodinnone
SoundNone in the gameHyper3D doesn't make audiogap

Still open

  1. Andrius picks the first asset to generate.
  2. Check the Hyper3D credit balance before the first batch.
  3. Ask Hyper3D whether OmniCraft counts toward the Rodin prize pool.

Unreal 5.8 docs for the import step

  • Interchange: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/interchange-framework-in-unreal-engine?application_version=5.8
  • glTF: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/the-gl-transmission-format-gltf-in-unreal-engine?application_version=5.8
  • Static meshes: https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/static-meshes?application_version=5.8

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort
  • Used for: the asset inventory (Content folder and C++ asset references), reading the Hyper3D MCP tool descriptions, the Unreal docs lookup and this proposal. No generations were run, so no credits were used.

Coach tip (topic: AI asset generation in a jam)

Before the first prompt, write each asset's game contract: size in metres, triangle budget, pivot, collision, and where it's used. Example for an asteroid: 1 m across at scale 1, pivot at the centre, at most 5,000 triangles, convex collision, drawn as an instanced mesh. A model that misses the contract costs a second generation and a second import.

14 Nov MapA derelict space station replaces the asteroid field; Rodin makes the missile and bombAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (all five decisions below, in reply to the Hyper3D proposal in the entry above)

Decisions

  1. Asteroids become a derelict space station. 450 components sit between the two capital ships, in six size types: very small, small, medium, large, huge and giant. Giant parts are the main theme. The two fighter teams fly through the parts to meet and fight, or to reach the capital ships.
  2. Capital ship: not Rodin. Zilvinas is designing it with Meshy.
  3. Debris: the station parts are the debris. No separate debris asset.
  4. Missile and bomb: generate with Rodin. Done, see below.
  5. Sky: finished. No OmniCraft pass. The NASA sky stays as it is.

Generated with Rodin (Gen-2.5 Medium, quad mesh, 2,000 faces, GLB; two generations' worth of credits)

  • Missile: https://hyper3d.ai/workspace/rodin/e4287493-266e-4d47-953e-87189d5d5ca3
  • Bomb: https://hyper3d.ai/workspace/rodin/e04d1b76-f746-4493-9184-6c1a43bac7c0
  • Not done yet: nobody has looked at the two models, and they aren't downloaded or imported.

What the code looks like today

  • AOrionSpaceEnvironment::BuildAsteroids places 450 copies of one engine cube: 2 to 40 m across, mostly small, inside a 700 m radius field flattened to 40% height. The capital ships sit 950 m from the centre on each side. The launch lanes (60 m radius) and the centre (80 m) are kept clear.
  • The rocks don't move or rotate.
  • Bots avoid obstacles with collision sweeps, so the collision shape of each station part decides how bots fly around it.
  • There is no bomb weapon. Bombers fire the same kinetic, energy and missile weapons as fighters.

14 Nov Packaged smoke test passesThe "40 s exit" was the test harness, not the gameAndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy ("fix it": get the packaged smoke test to PASS). Claude chose the fixes below.

Question

Why did the packaged build quit after 40 s (ConsoleCtrl RequestExit) instead of reaching the win screen?

What we found

  • The game was fine. Re-run unchanged in the background, the old package passed: BLUE TEAM WINS after 120 s. ConsoleCtrl RequestExit is Windows closing the game's console window. The -log flag opens that window, and it closed when the shell that launched the earlier run ended.
  • Bug 1, smoke script: the top-level .exe is only a launcher, and the real game runs from Binaries\Win64. The script killed only the launcher, so a headless game stayed running after every test.
  • Bug 2, a stale package as a result: that leftover game locked Saved/Packaged. The next package built and staged fine, but the copy into Saved/Packaged silently kept the old 11:10 .ucas/.utoc and game exe. That's why M_SpaceShield was still missing even after Andrius's /Game/Space cook fix.
  • Packaging with the editor open: UAT stops with "Unable to build while Live Coding is active". Adding -nocompileeditor skips the editor rebuild and packages fine, as long as the editor binaries are current.

Fixes

  • Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1: -log dropped (-abslog still writes the log file), and any game process running from the package folder is stopped when the test ends.
  • Fresh Development Win64 package: 0 errors, 0 warnings. The staged build was copied over Saved/Packaged (.ucas 240 → 261 MB).
  • Result: PASS after 80 s. The shield material loads, and no game processes are left over. The only warning left is the harmless editor-only GameFeatureData one.

Still open (team)

  1. Commit smoke-packaged.ps1 on its own branch off develop (currently uncommitted on targeting-upgrades).
  2. Nobody has played the packaged build with a mouse and keyboard yet. Zip Saved/Packaged/Windows and try it on a PC without Unreal installed.
  3. Bombs, and the ~5 km capital ships, are still missing from the loop.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: low
  • Used for: reading the game and UAT logs, three smoke runs, two packaging runs, the script fixes, this entry

Coach tip (topic: trusting your tests)

A test that fails for reasons outside the game is worse than no test, because it trains the team to ignore red. When a smoke test fails, first rule out the harness: re-run it alone, then check what's still running and how fresh the files are.

14 Nov Derelict stationAll 12 parts generated and imported, missile model wired inAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius

What was done

  • Rodin generated the other six station parts: habitat ring arc and solar array wing (giant), fuel tank cluster (huge), docking arm (large), radiator panel (medium), hull plate (small). That makes 14 Rodin generations today: 12 station parts, the missile and the bomb.
  • All 14 models were downloaded to D:\Games\2026 Epic Jam\RodinSource (outside the repo, 150 MB). Each is about 1.9 m on its longest side, centred, with three 2048x2048 textures.
  • New script Scripts/setup_rodin_assets.py imports all 14 headless in about 20 s. The result is 70 files and 158 MB in Content/Space/Station and Content/Space/Weapons, all in Git LFS.
  • Game code: AOrionSpaceEnvironment now has one instanced mesh per station model, and BuildStation replaces BuildAsteroids. Parts are placed biggest first, the launch lanes and the centre stay clear by each part's own radius, and big parts can't sit inside each other.
  • Missiles now show the Rodin model with a small exhaust glow behind it. The bomb is imported but not used.
  • The editor target builds.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. Nanite is on for station parts. The importer turns it on by default, and I kept it: Rodin's quad faces become two triangles each, so the 450 parts would otherwise be about 2.5 million triangles on screen. Weapons have it off.
  2. Collision is the model's own shape, so holes in rings and trusses stay open. Bots steer by collision sweeps, so they should use those gaps too.
  3. Our own material (M_RodinSurface) replaces the importer's. It has the instanced-mesh and Nanite usage flags saved, which the packaged build needs. This is the same trap as this morning's asteroid material.
  4. Texture size kept in the game: 2048 for giant, huge and large parts, 1024 for medium and small, 512 for scrap and weapons.
  5. The missile stays 1.4 m long. Its nose direction was worked out from the model's shape (thin end forward), not checked by eye.

Not done, not verified

  • Nobody has seen any of this in the game: the look of the parts, the missile's direction, the frame rate, or a packaged build. The headless test below covers placement, collision and the win loop only.
  • Nothing is committed or pushed.

14 Nov Ship models in the gameCarriers, fighters and bombersAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (pull the capital ship models from develop and the small ship models, and add them to the game before the test flight). Claude chose the details below, so the team should review them.

What was done

  • feat/derelict-station was brought up to the latest develop (9315ea1: Zilvinas's two carrier models, Andy's smoke test). The uncommitted work was snapshotted first and checked afterwards: five files byte-identical, and the devlog keeps both sides.
  • The six Meshy models were looked at before any code was written. Claude rendered top, side and front pictures from each file's vertex data. All six face the same way (nose or bow at -X, top at +Y). The same pictures settled the missile: its pointed nose is the end the code already points forward.
  • The import script is now Scripts/setup_model_assets.py and handles the ships as well as the Rodin models. The shared material is now M_ModelSurface. It imports all 19 models in 68 s.
  • Fighters and bombers: the model replaces the box-built hull. The 9 m collision box, exhaust glows, engine lights and shield bubble are unchanged. Bots with the Bomber role get the bomber model; the player and all other bots get the fighter.
  • Capital ships: the carrier model is the hull. The box-built ship is switched off, collision comes from the model's own shape, and the four weak points sit on the model's surface.
  • If a model file is missing, that ship falls back to the box-built version, so the game still runs.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. The carrier is 280 m long, about the size of the box-built ship (the brief says about 5 km). At that length the models are 68 m (Blue) and 77 m (Red) wide and about 60 m tall. The length is one constant, ModelScale, but the weak points and the launch height are measured for it.
  2. Fighters launch from 45 m below the centreline, which is about 15 m under the keel. The model has no open hangar, and the old launch grid would sit inside its solid bow. The catapult still throws them forward for 2.2 s.
  3. Weak points were measured from each model: BRIDGE on the tower 6 m below the mast tip, the two REACTORS at the widest points of the hull sides aft of the middle, ENGINE CORE on top of the hull just ahead of the engines.
  4. Team colour: the box-built ships had glowing team-colour trim. The models are red and blue by texture only, with no glow.

Tested headless (the game without a window, Red's capital ship cut to 10% health)

  • A temporary in-game probe on both carriers: the model blocks a sweep from the side, from ahead and from astern. All 12 launch runs under each hull are clear. Seven of eight weak points are the first thing hit from outside. The eighth, Blue's BRIDGE, has the thin mast directly above it; the tower is under a metre across there, so the 9 m weak point sticks out on every side. The probe was removed afterwards.
  • Full match: BLUE TEAM WINS 1 min 44 s in (1 min 51 s with the station only). Red's bots destroyed the BLUE BRIDGE weak point on the way, which shows weak points on the new hull can be hit. 31 fighter kills, 1 crash, no asset or material errors.

Not verified

  • Anything that needs eyes: how the ships look in the game, whether team colours read at a distance, the frame rate, and a packaged build.

Size warning

  • Each ship model is 600,000 to 1,000,000 triangles with 4K textures. Imported, the six ships are 463 MB. The raw .glb files already in the repo are 283 MB, and the station and missile are 150 MB.
  • develop holds 415 MB in Git LFS today. Pushing this branch as it is would take the repo to about 1 GB of LFS.
  • A lighter export from Meshy (about 100,000 triangles and 2K textures for the fighters) would cut most of that with no visible loss at game distances. Re-importing takes about a minute with the script.

Still open

  2. Decide whether Zilvinas re-exports lighter models before this branch is committed and pushed. Decided by Andrius: keep these files. The other option was a lighter export from Meshy first. GitHub's documentation gives a free organisation 10 GiB of Git LFS storage and 10 GiB of download bandwidth a month (https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/git-lfs), so about 1 GB of LFS fits. Bandwidth is the one to watch: every fresh clone downloads all of it, and when the month's bandwidth runs out, LFS is switched off for the account until the next month.

14 Nov ToolingBlender 5.2.4 and MCP for Blender; Tripo deferredAndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius (set up Blender, Tripo's DCC Bridge and a Blender MCP for Claude)
Who decided: Andrius (all three decisions below, from a three-question quiz)

Decisions

  1. Blender's jam job: fix generated models for Unreal. Before the import script runs, point each Meshy or Rodin model along one forward axis, set its pivot and its size in metres, and cut it to its triangle budget. Cutting the capital ship into hull sections stays a Saturday-night stretch goal.
  2. Tripo waits until the game is packaged. It would be a third generator, next to Rodin (bought) and Meshy (Zilvinas's), with its own account and licence. Free-plan models are CC BY 4.0, which is not on our allowed list.
  3. Blender goes on the D: drive.

What was done

  • Blender 5.2.4 LTS (built 10 Nov) installed to D:\Program Files\Blender Foundation\Blender 5.2 from the official installer (blender-5.2.4-windows-x64.msi, 366 MB). Its SHA-256 matched Blender's published hash.
  • MCP for Blender 2.2.9 (MIT licence; the project used to be called blender-mcp): the add-on is copied into Blender 5.2's add-on folder, and the server is registered in Claude's user config, pinned to @2.2.9, with DISABLE_TELEMETRY=true and PYTHONUTF8=1.
  • Setup procedure for the team: D:\Games\2026 Epic Jam\BLENDER-MCP-SETUP.md (outside the repo).

Lessons

  • The add-on's installer only looks for Blender in C:\Program Files, on PATH and in Steam. With Blender on D:, set BLENDERMCP_ADDONS_DIR to %APPDATA%\Blender Foundation\Blender\5.2\scripts\addons.
  • After copying the file, the installer crashes on a Windows console while printing an arrow that the cp1252 code page can't show. It does no harm, and PYTHONUTF8=1 avoids it.
  • The Blender Foundation\Blender\3.6 to 5.2 folders already on Andrius's PC were empty stubs made by the Fab plugin on 12 Nov, not a Blender install.
  • A Claude session loads MCP tools only when it starts, so this session tested the link directly over port 9876.

Licence verdicts for development tools (policy §7.2), checked by the planner model. They are recorded here because the jam repo has no tracker/.

ToolLicenceVerdict
Blender 5.2.4GPLAllowed as a development tool: it never ships, claims no rights in what we export, and has no clause restricting AI input. Scripts that import bpy stay out of the repo, because Blender treats scripts that use its Python API as GPL-covered when they are distributed.
MCP for Blender 2.2.9MITAllowed. Telemetry is off at both ends (the server's environment variable, and the add-on's consent setting reads false). Generator API keys are left empty.

Coach tip (topic: reading a passing test)

A pass tells you the loop closed, not that the log is clean. Read the log of every packaged run for "Error" and "Failed to find" even when the result is PASS. That is how this run turned up a startup error that had been on develop since the afternoon.

14 Nov PackagingThe GameFeatureData startup error is fixedAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (fix it now in a small PR; the other option was to leave it for later)

The problem

The packaged game logged Failed to load class /Script/GameFeatures.GameFeatureData for Primary Asset Type GameFeatureData at every start, as a handled ensure with a call stack (22 error lines). The game carried on, and the smoke test still passed.

The cause

Two changes from this afternoon, each fine on its own:

  • DefaultGame.ini gained an Asset Manager entry for GameFeatureData, because the cook fails without one.
  • The .uproject limited the toolset plugins to the editor. The GameFeatures plugin comes with them, so the packaged game no longer has that class. The entry said bIsEditorOnly=False, so the packaged game went looking for a class it does not have.

The fix

One flag: the entry is now bIsEditorOnly=True, with a comment explaining why. The cook still sees the entry; the packaged game skips it.

Verified

  • Package: 594 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings, 1.24 GB (Builds\2026-11-14_1707_dev).
  • Smoke test: RESULT: PASS after 75s, with Station field: 450 of 450 parts placed and BLUE TEAM WINS.
  • Packaged log: 0 error lines, 0 handled ensures, 0 mentions of GameFeatureData. The only warning is the test's own cheat line.

Still open

  • main has the error until this fix reaches it.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort
  • Used for: finding the cause in the packaged log, the config change, the package and smoke test, this entry

Coach tip (topic: config that exists for the editor)

When a config entry is there only to keep an editor tool or the cook happy, mark it editor-only. Otherwise the packaged game inherits a promise it can't keep, and you find out from a log line weeks later, or never.

14 Nov Fleet battleThree capital ships and 72 fighters per teamAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (72 fighters per team; three capital ships per team with the fighters divided among them; the player respawns at a random capital ship; one capital ship at the start, the others jumping in 30 s apart). Claude chose the details listed under "Choices", so the team should review them.

What changed

  • Game mode: each team has a fleet of three capital ships. The first is on station at 0:00, the second jumps in at 0:30 and the third at 1:00. Each launches its own 24 fighters when it arrives, so a team reaches 72 at 1:00. The match is now 72 v 72, up from 12 v 12.
  • Capital ship: a jump-in move (it starts 6 km behind its station and brakes onto it in 1.5 s, with a flash before and after), and its own launch-slot counter.
  • Bots: each belongs to a home ship. Defenders guard it. Attackers go for the nearest enemy capital ship still standing, so the attack moves on when one is destroyed.
  • HUD: one bar per fleet showing its health, ships left and a countdown to the next arrival. Markers, weak-point brackets and the lead reticle work on every capital ship. The map shows them all.
  • The numbers are settings on the game mode: ShipsPerTeam, FightersPerShip, ArrivalInterval, FleetSpacing.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. Win: destroy all three enemy capital ships. A ship still waiting to jump in counts as alive, so losing the only ship on station early does not end the match.
  2. Respawn: the player relaunches from a random surviving friendly ship, as asked. A bot goes back to its own ship while it survives, then to a random surviving one.
  3. Positions: the three ships sit side by side, 600 m apart, each 950 m from the centre.
  4. Launch waves: a ship's 24 fighters leave in two waves of 12, 3 s apart, because the launch grid holds 12.
  5. Time-out: the fleet with more total health wins. A ship that never arrived counts as full health.
  6. Dev cheat: OrionDamageCarrier now hits every capital ship of the team, including ones that jump in later, so the smoke test still ends quickly.
  7. A performance line in the log every 30 s (not in Shipping builds): average and worst frame time, and how many bot fighters are flying.

Tested

  • Editor build, headless, with Red cut to 10%: the second and third ships of both sides jump in 30 s apart, and BLUE TEAM WINS only after all three Red ships are destroyed (74 s).
  • Editor build, headless, no cheat, 160 s: with 134 to 139 bot fighters flying, the average frame is 4.4 to 4.7 ms and the worst 11.7 to 19.3 ms. That is game logic only, without rendering. 199 fighter kills, 3 crashes, and weak points destroyed on several ships of both sides.
  • Packaged build (Builds\2026-11-14_1805_fleet): 594 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings. Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1 reports RESULT: PASS after 75s. The packaged log has 0 error lines.

Not verified

  • Anything on screen: the frame rate with 144 ship models and their 288 engine lights, how the jump-in looks, whether the HUD bar text fits, and whether a 72 v 72 fight is readable.

Known

  • Running the editor build as a game (-game) now logs one GameFeatureData error at start. It comes from this afternoon's startup-error fix, which made that config entry editor-only. The packaged game and Play-in-Editor are clean.
  • The jam brief in the Claude org context still says 24 v 24 with one capital ship per team.

Still open

  1. Andrius: fly the packaged fleet build. Done, see the update below. The lowest stat fps figure was not written down.
  2. If the frame rate is too low, cut in this order: engine lights on distant fighters, then FightersPerShip from 24 to 16.
  3. Commit and pull request. Done, see the update below.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort
  • Used for: reading the game mode, bot, HUD and capital ship code, the design, the C++ changes, the headless and packaged tests, this entry

Coach tip (topic: scaling a battle up)

Turn the big number into a setting before you raise it, and measure the cost without rendering first. Here the game logic for 144 ships costs under 5 ms a frame, so if the frame rate drops on screen, the cause is drawing, and the fix is in lights and models, not in the bots.

Update, same day: flown and sent for review

Andrius flew the packaged fleet build (Builds\2026-11-14_1805_fleet), said it's great, and asked for pull requests into both develop and main.

  • That closes the "not verified on screen" item as far as Andrius's own judgement goes. No frame-rate figure was recorded, so the next flight should note the lowest stat fps and the GPU, or read the Perf: lines in the log.

14 NovCapital ships circle the station, jump in as a streak, and have ion thrustersAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (a jump-in animation for the capital ships, all capital ships flying and circling the centre of the debris, and ion thruster animations). Claude chose the details listed under "Choices", so the team should review them.

What changed

  • Circling: every capital ship flies one circle around the centre of the derelict station, 1,100 m out, bow along the direction of travel. All six go round at the same rate (1.5 degrees a second, a lap every four minutes, 28.8 m/s), so the two fleets stay opposite each other for the whole match.
  • Jump-in: an arriving ship comes in along its heading from 9 km back, stretched to ten times its length, and brakes onto its place in 2.4 s. There is a blue-white flash where it will appear and a bigger one as it arrives. The flash is a new effect kind that lasts 0.9 s (the old energy flash lasted 0.15 s).
  • Ion thrusters: each carrier model has four engine nozzles at the stern. Each gets a wide blue-white plume with a brighter, shorter core inside it, tinted a little toward the team colour, plus one light on the stern. The plumes shimmer, breathe slowly in brightness, and flare to about three times their length as the ship brakes out of a jump.
  • Because the ships now move: bots lead a capital ship's weak points, the player's lead reticle on weak points uses the ship's speed, the launch catapult adds the ship's own speed, and "toward the fight" for defenders and patrols now means toward the centre of the circle.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. Radius 1,100 m, up from the 950 m the ships used to sit at. The station's parts reach about 825 m from the centre at worst, so the hulls pass with over 200 m to spare.
  2. One circle, one rate, for both teams. Different rates or radii would let the fleets drift into each other or bunch up.
  3. Later ships join the formation where it has got to, 600 m ahead of and behind the first ship along the circle.
  4. Only ships that jump in get the animation. Each team's first ship is already there at 0:00, as asked earlier.
  5. A destroyed capital ship keeps circling as a dark wreck with its thrusters out.
  6. Thrusters are glowing cones, in the same style as the game's other effects (engine discs, blasts). They are not particle effects.

Tested

  • Editor build, headless, with a temporary position probe (removed afterwards, file checked against its backup): every ship stays exactly 1,100 m from the centre and advances 15 degrees every 10 s at 28.8 m/s, with the fleets opposite each other. A jump starts 9,067 m out at stretch 10.0, is 3,030 m out at stretch 5.15 after 0.8 s, and ends on its place at stretch 1.0.
  • Editor build, headless, Red cut to 10%: BLUE TEAM WINS after all three Red ships are destroyed. 65 fighter kills, 0 crashes.
  • Packaged build (Builds\2026-11-14_1831_orbit): 594 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings. Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1 reports RESULT: PASS after 85s. The packaged log has 0 error lines. Frame time was 2.6 ms average with 84 bot fighters flying (game logic only).

Not verified

  • Anything on screen: how the jump streak and flash look, whether the thruster plumes sit on the nozzles and point aft, their size and colour, and how circling capital ships feel to fly around and attack.

Still open

  1. Andrius: fly Builds\2026-11-14_1831_orbit and judge the three effects.
  2. Commit and pull request. Done, see the update below.
  3. The station code still keeps two launch lanes clear along the X axis. They no longer matter, because the ships do not sit there, but they do no harm.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort
  • Used for: checking what assumed stationary capital ships, measuring the engine nozzles on both models, the C++ changes, the probe and headless tests, the package and smoke test, this entry

Coach tip (topic: making a still thing move)

Before you move something that used to stand still, list who aims at it, who launches from it and who navigates by it. Here that was the bots' aim, the player's lead reticle, the catapult and the patrol routes. Each would have looked like a separate bug in the test flight.

14 Nov WeaponsShots now leave the new ship models' cannonsAndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy (per-model muzzle offsets in C++, not sockets on the meshes). Claude chose which part of each model counts as the guns and measured the points, so check them in a playtest.

Question

Where are the fighter weapon firing points stored, and how do we move them onto the new Meshy models?

What we found

  • The firing points were two hidden cylinders, BarrelR and BarrelL, left over from the box-built ship (OrionSpaceFighterPawn.cpp). Shots came from 3.7 m out on each side, where the old wingtip pods were, not from the new models.
  • One pair of points served all four models (blue and red fighter, blue and red bomber), and the bots too.
  • The models have no sockets.

Decision

Use a small table of muzzle points per model in C++, applied when the model is set. Sockets would let us tweak points in the editor, but they're binary .uasset edits that two people can't merge.

What was done (branch feat/fighter-muzzle-offsets, off the latest develop)

  • Measured from the vertices of the four .glb files: every model has one cannon under each wing, about 37 to 40 cm from the centreline. The barrel tips sit 11 to 20 cm ahead of the model's centre, which is 0.5 to 0.9 m ahead of the ship's centre at game scale (×4.74). The wingtip pods were left alone because they look like missiles.
  • ApplyShipModel() turns each model's tip points into ship space, so they follow any change to the model's scale or turn. The box-built ship keeps its old firing points.
  • FireSelected() fires from those points and still alternates left and right. Shots now also lean with the ship when it banks into a strafe.

Verified

  • Andy closed the editor. The editor target built in 6 s with no warnings, and the editor reopened with the MCP server.
  • PIE starts cleanly: Station field: 450 of 450 parts placed, then Destroy the RED capital ship, with no game errors in the log. The player's blue fighter shows the Meshy model.

Not verified yet

  • Playtest: do shots visibly leave the under-wing barrels? Andy is checking this in PIE now. Bombers are flown only by bots, so they can only be checked by watching one fire.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: low
  • Used for: finding the firing-point code, a Node script that reads the .glb vertices and draws top and front views, measuring the barrel tips, the C++ change, this entry

Coach tip (topic: attaching gameplay points to art)

Write measured points into the code next to the model they came from, with one line on how they were measured. When Zilvinas re-exports a ship, the comment says which numbers to redo and how, instead of someone guessing from a screenshot at 2 a.m.

Playtest result (Andy): kinetic shots leave the under-wing cannons. The points suit the kinetic weapon.

Update, same day: each weapon fires from its own mounts (Andy's decision)

  • Kinetic: the two under-wing cannons, unchanged.
  • Energy: the single gun under the nose. Its tip is about 82 cm forward on every model, which is about 3.9 m ahead of the ship's centre at game scale.
  • Missiles: the pointed pods under each wing tip, about 70 cm out, which is about 3.3 m out at game scale. They alternate left and right.
  • Measured the same way as the cannons, plus bottom views, centre-line side views and cross-sections just behind the gun tip. The cross-sections were needed because the first pass caught the hull's nose cone instead of the nose gun on two models. The Meshy models aren't perfectly symmetrical, so some nose guns sit 2 to 4 cm off the centreline.
  • Code: each model's entry in ModelMuzzles now names its mounts (WingCannons, NoseGun, WingtipPods). MuzzlePoints is one list per weapon type, and FireSelected() uses the selected weapon's list. The box-built fallback ship still fires every weapon from its old barrels. Bots use the same code.
  • Verified: the editor target built in 7 s (header change, so the editor was closed), and PIE started with no game errors. Still to check: Andy fires energy and missiles in PIE.

Playtest result (Andy): missiles leave the wingtip pods, and kinetic is unchanged. The energy weapon fired slightly to the left.

Fix, same day: energy fires from the centreline (Andy asked for the offset to be corrected)

  • Cause: on the blue fighter and both bombers, the under-nose barrel is modelled 3 to 4 cm off-centre along its whole length. At game scale that's 14 to 19 cm left of the centreline. The red fighter's gun is centred.
  • Fix: the energy mount uses y = 0 on every model and keeps each gun's measured tip position and height. Gameplay wins over the model for anything that affects aim.
  • The firing-point table is filled in when the module loads, and Live Coding keeps old values of such data, so this needed an editor restart rather than Ctrl+Alt+F11.
  • Verified: the build succeeded and PIE starts cleanly. Still to check: Andy fires energy again.
  • For Zilvinas: centre the nose guns if the ships are ever re-exported.

Playtest result (Andy): energy shots are centred. Andy is happy with all the new firing points.

Question (Andy): now that energy fires from one gun instead of two, is its damage per second halved?

No. Fire rate belongs to the weapon, not to its guns. Every trigger pull fires one projectile and waits FireInterval, however many mounts there are. The second mount only changed which side each shot left from, never how many shots there were.

  • Energy, unchanged: 12 damage every 0.22 s, about 55 per second before modifiers. That's about 87 per second against shields (×1.6) and about 25 per second against hull (×0.45).
  • Kinetic for comparison: 5 damage every 0.09 s, about 56 per second. That's about 28 per second against shields and about 69 per second against hull.
  • No decision needed. If the single nose gun should feel weaker or stronger, tune Energy.FireInterval or Energy.Damage in SetupDefaultWeapons().

Update, same day: kinetic at 6000 rounds per minute, same damage per second (Andy's decision)

  • Kinetic.FireInterval went from 0.09 s (about 667 rpm) to 0.01 s (6000 rpm). Kinetic.Damage went from 5 to about 0.56 a round (5 × 0.01 / 0.09), so damage per second stays at about 55.6: about 28 against shields and about 69 against hull. Both values are written as formulas in SetupDefaultWeapons().
  • Fire-loop fix (Claude found it): the loop only fired one round per frame. At 60 fps a frame lasts 16.7 ms, so 6000 rpm would really have been about 3,600 rpm, with about 40% less damage per second. Now each frame fires every round its time allows (at most 10, so a hitch doesn't dump a burst), and the time a frame overshoots carries into the next shot. Rounds that leave in the same frame start as far along as they'd have travelled by now, so tracers stay 3.2 m apart. Releasing the trigger still resets the cooldown. Bots use the same code.
  • Cost to watch: about 9× more kinetic rounds in flight, roughly 80 per firing ship. Each does a line trace and redraws its tracer every frame, with no cap. Check stat unit in a big dogfight. If frame time climbs, draw a tracer for only every 3rd round.
  • Weapon stats are set in the pawn's constructor, which Live Coding doesn't re-run, so this needed an editor restart.
  • Verified: the build succeeded and PIE starts with no game errors. Still to check: how it feels, and frame time.
  • Playtest result (Andy): the 6000 rpm kinetic feels good, and the frame rate is fine. The every-3rd-round tracer fallback isn't needed.

Update, same day: rebased and merged into develop (Andy's decision)

  • Andrius's PRs #24 (fleet battle: three capital ships and 72 fighters a team) and #26 (circling capital ships, jump-in, ion thrusters) had landed on develop in the meantime. Neither touches the fighter pawn files. Only the devlog conflicted, and both sides were kept.
  • After the rebase, the code commit is unchanged, and no devlog line from either side is missing.
  • Re-tested in the fleet battle, with 144 fighters, because the first frame-rate check ran with about 48. Andy: "FPS fine". The every-3rd-round tracer fallback is still not needed.
