14 NovBots spread out and fly in four styles; each capital ship has its own pathAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (bots should stop piling onto the same target, with some randomness in their decisions; they should approach in arcs and other ways instead of one straight line, rotate a little, and have behaviours, a sneaky one among them; each capital ship should have its own path and rotate a little about its axis). Claude chose the details listed under "Choices", so the team should review them.

What changed

  • Fighter targets are spread out. A bot choosing a fighter to chase scores every enemy in reach. Nearer is better, each bot already chasing that enemy makes it less attractive, and a random factor differs for every bot. No more than three bots chase one fighter. A bot stays on its pick for 3 to 7 seconds before it thinks again. The game mode keeps the count of who is chasing whom.
  • Strike runs are spread out. Each bot picks its own enemy capital ship and keeps it for 25 to 45 seconds. A nearer ship is more likely, not certain. On a strike run the bot picks one surviving weak point at random and stays on it until it is gone. Before, every bot went for the nearest ship and the nearest weak point.
  • Four flying styles. Each bot gets one for the whole match:
    • Direct: straight at it, as before.
    • Flanker: swings up to 300 m out to one side and comes in on an arc.
    • Stalker (the sneaky one): gets round behind its target, passing wide of it first if it starts in front. It holds its fire until it is within 140 m and behind. Being shot at ends its patience. On a strike run it comes in from above or below.
    • Weaver: corkscrews in on a 70 m radius, and rolls right round while it attacks.
  • Every bot banks into its turns. Before, bots kept their wings level.
  • Each capital ship has its own path. Its own size of circle: 1,100 m, 1,276 m and 1,452 m by the ship's place in its fleet. Its own tilt: the circle is tipped out of the flat plane by 4 to 15 degrees, about a different line for each ship, so the ships climb and dive as they go round, up to 330 m above and below the station's level. Its own slow roll: 5 to 7 degrees to each side, over 27 to 44 seconds.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. The style mix. Fighters: 25% direct, 30% flanker, 20% stalker, 25% weaver. Bombers: 10% direct, 35% flanker, 40% stalker, 15% weaver, because the brief has bombers sneaking in. Of the 143 bots in a match that comes to 21 direct, 50 flankers, 33 stalkers and 39 weavers.
  2. Three chasers per fighter. A bot that has just been shot may still turn on its attacker, whatever the count.
  3. Every style flies straight for the last 240 m to a fighter and the last 300 m to a weak point, so that its guns bear. The style shapes the way there.
  4. All capital ships still go round at the same rate (1.5 degrees a second). Claude first planned the same speed for all, then worked out that Blue's first ship would close 156 degrees on Red's over a 15-minute match. The same rate keeps the two fleets opposite each other with the station between them, and no two ships can meet.
  5. The size of the circle is the same for both teams (it goes by the ship's place in its fleet), so neither team's fighters have further to fly. Tilt and roll differ for all six ships.
  6. A fleet's ships are 30 degrees apart. They were 600 m apart along one circle, which was about 31 degrees.
  7. A ship on a tipped circle banks with its circle. On screen a ship leans up to about 21 degrees from level: its tilt plus its roll.

Tested

  • Target spread. The match log's 30-second performance line now also says how many bots are chasing how many fighters, and the most on any one. Editor build, headless, all 143 bots:
    • Old rule (always the nearest), measured with the same counter: 8 bots on one fighter in every sample of one run, and up to 24 in a second run.
    • New scoring without the cap: 6 to 11 on one.
    • New scoring with the cap: at most 3 on one in all 12 samples, with 52 to 80 bots chasing 29 to 45 fighters.
  • Strike spread. After 4.5 minutes all six capital ships had taken damage: Blue's were at 39%, 59% and 78% health, Red's at 42%, 59% and 70%.
  • Ship paths, with a temporary probe (removed afterwards, both files checked against their backups by hash). Each ship stays exactly on its radius at 28.8, 33.4 and 38.0 m/s. Heights range from 76 m above and below for the flattest circle to 330 m for the steepest. The closest any two capital ships came was 629 m.
  • The loop. Headless, Red cut to 10%: BLUE TEAM WINS after all three Red ships are destroyed, 104 s into the match. The time was the same with and without the cap.
  • Cost. 4.8 ms average frame with 133 to 136 bots flying (game logic only, headless). It was about 4.5 ms before this work.
  • Crashes. 7 bots flew into something in the 4.5-minute run, against 472 kills. Earlier short runs showed none against 65 kills, so arcs and corkscrews through the station may cost a few crashes. This wasn't compared properly.
  • Packaged build (Builds\2026-11-14_2035_behaviours): 594 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings. Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1 reports RESULT: PASS after 115s, and the log has 0 error lines. Blue won 109 s into the match, with at most 3 bots on one fighter in every sample. Frame time was 2.7 ms average with 137 bot fighters flying (game logic only).
  • The win takes longer. The same smoke test passed in 85 s on the build before this one, and takes 115 s now. The likely cause is the spread itself: the bots' fire now goes to three capital ships and to weak points picked at random, where before every bot hit the nearest ship. The arcs add a little too.

Not verified

  • Anything on screen. How the arcs, corkscrews and banking look. Whether the weavers' rolling is too much. Whether a stalker feels sneaky when you are its target. How tipped, rolling capital ships look, and how they are to attack.
  • A full 15-minute match. The longest run was 4.5 minutes.

Still open

  1. Andrius: fly Builds\2026-11-14_2035_behaviours and judge the bots and the ship paths.
  2. Commit and pull request, after the flight.
  3. Tuning is a few numbers. The style mix is in AOrionBotController::Setup, the cap and the distances are the OrionBot constants at the top of OrionBotController.cpp, and the six path shapes are a table in AOrionGameMode::MakeShipPath.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: reading the bot code, the design of the scoring, the styles and the paths, the C++ changes, the probes and six headless matches, the package and smoke test, this entry

Coach tip (topic: bot AI)

Make a crowd of bots differ in their decisions before you make them differ in skill. A style drawn once per bot and a shared count of who is chasing whom are cheap, and they turn a swarm into individuals. Then put one number in the log that says whether it worked. Nobody can watch 144 ships at once, but "at most 3 on one" can be read in a second.

Update, same day: sent for review

Andrius asked for the pull requests ("Open the PRs"). The other choice offered was "I'll fly it first".

  • A windowed copy of the packaged build was running on his machine from 20:38 UTC, a few minutes before he asked. Claude didn't start it. What he saw wasn't written down, so the "Not verified" list above stands as a record of what Claude could not check, not of problems found.
  • develop had moved on by one pull request (#30, Andy's devlog page builder), so the branch was rebased onto it first, by the team's rebase rules. Only this file conflicted, and both sides were kept: Andy's entry first, then this one. The code commit is unchanged, so the build that was tested is the code that was sent.
  • Both pull requests come from feat/bot-behaviours, one into develop and one into main. The one into main also carries Andy's weapon mounts (PR #28) and his devlog page builder (PR #30), because main doesn't have them yet.
  • Per the branch rule, Andy or Zilvinas approve the one into develop, and only Andrius approves and merges the one into main.
14 Nov ArtThe carrier assembled from Meshy parts, with bay doors and turning turretsZilvinasOpus 5.5

Asked by: Zilvinas
Decided by: Zilvinas

Question

Can Claude use Blender to combine the Meshy carrier parts (hull, tower, railgun, point-defence turret) into one ship, animate it, and record where every piece came from?

Decisions

  • Hull scaled 100×. Railgun on the large front platform, point-defence turrets on the six small platforms, each sized to fill its platform.
  • The bridge tower replaces the first control tower. It must be at least twice the railgun's height and symmetrical, and it goes wherever it can be tallest.
  • Bay doors at the stern, either side of the engines, open with an animation. Turrets get rotation animations.
  • Repository: Option A. Commit the source parts, the build scripts, the assembled GLB, the previews and PROVENANCE.md. Leave out the .blend files and old builds.

Outcome

  • Content/Space/Ships/Carrier_Assembled/ (PR #29): a 190 m carrier. The tower is 16.0 m tall, 2.01× the railgun's 7.94 m, on the flat spine at the ship's middle. The outlined pad was too small: only about 7 m of height would fit there.
  • Tower made symmetrical by mirroring its cleaner half. The original sat 2.2 % off-centre, with mismatched corners.
  • Bay doors: the panels can't slide (only about 0.25 m of hull around them), so each splits into two leaves that swing 90° inward. A 7 m hangar with red guide lights sits behind each.
  • Turret heads cut from their drums at the neck, each with its pivot on the yaw axis. Clips: BayDoors_Open 2 s, Railgun_Traverse 12 s, PD_Traverse 6 s.
  • Scripts/build_carrier.py rebuilds the GLB from the source parts in about 65 s, byte for byte (same SHA-256).
  • Provenance: Meshy 7.1 on the team's PRO account, text instructions only, nothing shared to the Meshy Community. Under Meshy's paid-plan terms, the team owns the models.
  • Blender 5.3 is at D:\Blender Foundation\Blender 5.3. It was not added to PATH, because that's a system setting that needs Andrius's yes.

Still open

  • Scale: the brief calls for capital ships of about 5 km. Set it on import (about 26× the 190 m build), with Nanite on. The GLB has 11.2 million triangles.
  • Not yet imported into Unreal or tested in a packaged build.

Coach tip (topic: AI-generated art in a jam)

Keep the recipe, not just the result. Source parts plus a script that rebuilds the asset byte for byte answer "how was this made?" for judges and licence checks. They also let anyone fix the ship on Saturday night without the one machine that built it.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: Medium
  • Used for: Blender scripting (assembly, symmetry, door and turret animation), checks and preview renders, PROVENANCE.md, the Meshy terms lookup, the commit and the PR
14 NovEngine trails, a 4,000 rpm gatling, Zilvinas's carrier with working turrets, and launches from its hangarsAndyAndriusZilvinasFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (engine trails for the fighters; the gatling gun at 4,000 rounds a minute with a more realistic trail; Zilvinas's new capital ship model in the game; its turrets turning and shooting at enemy ships; fighters, the player's included, leaving through the hangar doors; and resolving the conflicts with Zilvinas's PR #29 and merging with it). The 4,000 rpm figure replaces the 6,000 rpm Andy chose earlier today. Claude chose the details listed under "Choices", so the team should review them.

What changed

  • Engine trails. Each fighter leaves a tapering trail behind each of its two exhausts, 0.6 s of flight long. It is thin while the ship coasts, full at full thrust and fatter on boost. Blue's engines and trails are cold blue and Red's hot orange.
  • Gatling gun. 4,000 rounds a minute, down from 6,000. Each round does more (0.83, was 0.56), so damage per second is unchanged at about 56.
  • Tracers. One round in three is drawn, like a real belt, and every round still hits. A tracer is a streak as long as the round flies in 18 ms: a short white-hot head and a longer, thinner, redder tail. It never reaches back past the muzzle, and it thins out over its last 0.2 s. Before, every round was a 4 m bar, and at 6,000 rpm the bars overlapped into a beam.
  • The carrier. The capital ship is now the carrier Zilvinas assembled from Meshy parts, for both teams. Hull, bridge tower and the base and head of each kind of turret are separate meshes, placed from the assembled file's own figures. Weak points, the four engine nozzles and the turret muzzles were measured from the model. A team-colour beacon sits on the tip of the tower's mast.
  • Turrets. Six point-defence turrets and one railgun on each carrier. Each head turns toward its target, leads it and fires from its two barrels in turn. Point defence shoots enemy fighters within 225 m. The railgun shoots the nearest enemy capital ship within 3 km, or a fighter within 600 m when there is none. A turret only bears on what is above the deck, so a carrier's underside is the safe side to attack from. A kill reads "BLUE turret destroyed Red-10".
  • Bay doors and launches. The four leaves of the two stern bays swing 90 degrees inward on the model's hinges when a launch is asked for, and close 2.5 s after the last one. Fighters start inside a hangar and leave aft through the open doors, one per bay every 0.45 s. The player launches the same way at the start of the match and after every respawn.
  • Merge with PR #29. Zilvinas's branch conflicted with develop in the devlog only. It was merged into this work's branch with both sides kept. His branch and his pull request were not touched. When this branch lands, GitHub shows PR #29 as merged, because his commits are inside it.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. "Their trail" was read as the gatling rounds' tracers. The engine trail is the new thing in the same request.
  2. Red's engine glow changed from blue to orange, to match its trail, so a fighter's side reads from behind.
  3. One model for both teams. The two earlier Meshy carriers are no longer used by the game. Their files are still in the repository and still go into the package (about 150 MB). Deleting them is a decision for Zilvinas and Andrius.
  4. The carrier stays 280 m long. At that size a bay door is 8.7 m wide and 10.8 m tall, and a hangar 10.7 m deep, for a fighter that is 9 m long with a span of about 7 m. It fits, with little to spare. A fighter passes through its own capital ship until the catapult lets go, so a tight door can't scrape it.
  5. The doors are slabs built in code on the model's hinge places, with the model's 90-degree swing, in 0.8 s where the model's clip takes 2 s. The clips in the model file are not used: the parts are imported as static meshes, which don't carry animation.
  6. Turret heads only turn, as in the model. They don't tilt, so shots leave the barrels at the angle of the target.
  7. Turret figures. Point defence: 2.2 damage every 0.14 s, 450 m/s, 225 m. Railgun: 40 damage every 4 s, 900 m/s, 3 km. Turrets can't be destroyed, and their rounds pass through their own ship.
  8. Fighters leave aft, because the bays face the stern. They then turn toward the fight.
  9. The import script keeps an existing M_ModelSurface instead of rebuilding it. Rebuilding stopped the engine with an assertion, because the game's ship classes now load the models at start-up.

Tested

  • Fighter step (editor build, headless): BLUE TEAM WINS with Red cut to 10%. Game logic 4.9 to 5.0 ms a frame with about 136 bots (4.8 ms before the trails).
  • Carrier step (editor build, headless, with a temporary probe that was removed afterwards and checked by hash):
    • Doors go from closed to open in 0.8 s. The first two fighters leave at 0.8 s, then two more about every half second.
    • 43, 87 and 137 bot fighters flying at 30, 60 and 90 s, the same as before the hangars.
    • The fighter's box swept aft from each hangar is clear for 80 m.
    • Turrets fired 6,331 rounds in 150 s and made 28 of the 247 fighter kills (11%).
    • BLUE TEAM WINS with Red cut to 10%. Game logic 5.1 ms a frame.
  • Import: six meshes, 466 MB, all in Git LFS. The hull's collision is its simplified shape (20,054 triangles), like the earlier carriers'.
  • Packaged build (Builds\2026-11-14_2116_turrets): 624 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings. Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1 passed twice (RESULT: PASS after 120s and after 115s). The log has 0 error lines and no missing-asset lines, and it shows turret kills. Frame time was 3.1 ms average with 135 bot fighters flying (game logic only), against 2.7 ms for the build before. The first run read 4.8 ms because it started while the machine was still busy with the cook. The package is 1.42 GB, up from 1.30 GB.

Not verified

  • Anything on screen. The trails and tracers. Whether the carrier's parts sit where they should. Whether the turret heads face their targets and the tracers leave the barrel ends. Whether the door slabs fill the door openings and the hangar walls can't be seen from outside. How it looks to be launched backwards out of a hangar.
  • Whether the weak points sit on the surface: they were placed from measurements, not by eye.
  • A full 15-minute match.

Still open

  1. Andrius: fly Builds\2026-11-14_2116_turrets.
  2. Pull requests, after the flight. PR #31 (bot styles and capital ship paths) is still open, and this branch is built on top of it.
  3. Zilvinas and Andrius: delete the two earlier Meshy carriers from the repository?
  4. The game's package now carries three carriers' worth of textures. Check the package size before the jam upload.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: the merge, the trails, tracers and gun change, measuring the carrier, the import script changes, the carrier, turret, door and launch code, the probes and headless matches, the package and smoke test, this entry
  • The carrier itself: Meshy 7.1 (parts, by Zilvinas) and Claude Opus 5.5 at medium effort (assembly, in Zilvinas's session). See Content/Space/Ships/Carrier_Assembled/PROVENANCE.md.

Coach tip (topic: three requests on the last evening)

When several features arrive at once, build them in order of risk and commit each one once it is tested. Here that was the gun and trails first, then the new carrier with its turrets and doors. If the last one had broken the loop, the first would still be a build worth packaging.

14 Nov Ship specsOne table of every ship's numbers, read from the codeAndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (write up the ship specs as they are in the game now, as a table in a shared artifact for the org). Claude chose which numbers to include, read them from the code and worked out the derived ones, so the team should check the values marked ≈.

What changed

  • New artifact: https://claude.ai/artifact/6G4t1paUPmmUAjNKs7zfwf. It has tables for the three ship classes at a glance, the fighter and bomber flight model, defence, all five weapons, the carrier (size, health and weak points, turrets, hangars, movement), the fleet and match settings, time-to-kill figures, and where the ships differ from the jam brief and from develop.
  • The numbers come from branch feat/turrets-and-trails at 520309d, the newest build (10 commits ahead of origin/develop, not pushed yet). Each row can also show the setting's name in the code.
  • No code or game files changed. This entry is the only repo change.

Findings worth a look

  1. The weak points are worth 104% of a carrier's health. Four × (1,200 × 3 + the 1,600 penalty) = 20,800 against 20,000. One fighter's gatling on the weak points alone sinks a carrier in about 69 s. On plain hull it takes 4 min 48 s.
  2. Missile lock range is 220 m. That is a quarter of the missile's 900 m reach and only just past the guns' 256 m.
  3. A full rack of 6 missiles leaves a fresh fighter on 8 hull.
  4. The gatling and the energy gun both do about 55 damage a second. They differ only in shields against hull.
  5. Railguns do 10 damage a second. One needs 33 minutes to sink a carrier, longer than the 15-minute match.
  6. Bombers have the fighter's numbers and weapons. Only the model and the bot behaviour differ.

Not verified

  • Values marked ≈ (fighter span about 6.7 m, carrier beam about 58 m, mast tip about 37 m) are worked out from mount points in the code, not measured in the editor.
  • The time-to-kill figures assume every shot hits and no shield regeneration.

Still open

  1. Carrier size: 280 m against the brief's ~5 km. No decision is recorded.
  2. The spec page is private. Share it with the org from its Share menu.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: reading the ship code, the derived figures, the spec page, this entry

Coach tip (topic: ship tuning on the last night)

Tune match length with one number. Before the fleet battle, a one-carrier test ended at 1 min 51 s, and the weak points are the likely reason. If a full match on the packaged build feels too short, raise WeakPointHealth (1,200) rather than MaxHealth. That slows carrier kills without touching dogfights, and a one-line change won't put Sunday morning's package at risk.

14 Nov Flight feedbackGentler launch, white fading trails, zoom, a slimmer flying shape, weak points as hull panels, and a Blender file for ZilvinasAndriusZilvinasFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius, after flying Builds\2026-11-14_2116_turrets (a slower launch; the engine trails starting further inside the engine housing; weak points sitting on the hull and highlighted as parts of it; trails more transparent the closer the player is, but never fully; the right mouse button zooms in; a smaller fighter box so that two fighters can fly by one another, with the box for ammunition as before; the player's own trail at 30%; every fighter's trail one neutral white; a folder for Zilvinas with the assembled carrier to view in Blender). Claude chose the numbers listed under "Choices", so the team should review them.

Andrius's answers from the flight

  • Launching backwards out of a hangar: "it's ok, but the whole launch should be a bit slower".
  • Turret tracers leave the barrel ends and the heads face what they shoot: yes to both.
  • The turrets when attacked from above: "just right".
  • The engine trails read well, and Red's orange engine glow is an improvement.
  • The weak points should sit on the hull.
  • "Do the door slabs fill the bay openings, and can you see hangar walls from outside?" was answered with one "yes". The second half still needs an answer.

What changed

  • Launch. Out of the carrier's bays a fighter is now carried along with the ship and eased aft: from 7.5 m/s up to 30 m/s relative to the ship, over 3 seconds. Before, it left at 55 m/s at once while the ship flew the other way. One fighter leaves each bay every 1.2 s, where it was 0.45 s.
  • Engine trails. White for every fighter. They start 1.4 m further forward, inside the engine housing. They are see-through, and the closer a part of a trail is to the camera the dimmer it is, down to a fifth and never to nothing. The player's own trail is drawn at 30% of its width.
  • Zoom. Hold the right mouse button, or click the right stick, and the view narrows from 85 to 35 degrees. The mouse slows down by the same share.
  • Flying shape. A fighter still has its one box, 9 m by 8 m by 2.2 m, and shots hit it exactly as before. It now flies and crashes with a slimmer shape: a box 6.6 m by 3.2 m by 1.5 m against the station and the capital ships, and a row of three balls 2.2 m across against other fighters. Two fighters can pass 2.2 m apart, where they needed 8 m.
  • Weak points. On the carrier each weak point is a flat round panel, 9 m across, lying on the hull. When a ship arrives, a trace onto the hull (or the bridge tower) finds the surface and its slope, and the panel is laid flat there. The bridge's panel is on the tower's forward face.
  • For Zilvinas. D:\Games\2026 Epic Jam\For Zilvinas holds Carrier_Assembled_view.blend (313 MB, textures packed inside, lamps and a camera added), six stills of it, a README and the script that made them. It is outside the repository, because .blend files are not in Git LFS yet.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. "30%" for the player's trail is its width. Its length is unchanged.
  2. The engine glow discs keep their team colours. Only the trails went white.
  3. Fade distances. A trail is at its dimmest within 12 m of the camera and at full brightness beyond 50 m.
  4. Zoom is hold, not toggle, and 35 degrees.
  5. Ship-to-ship bumps use balls, not boxes. Three balls along the fuselage are cheap to test for every pair of 144 ships.
  6. Weak point panels are 1.4 m thick, so they stand 0.7 m proud of the hull and shots reach them before the hull.

A dead end worth recording

The first version gave every fighter a second collision body for shots. Frame time doubled, from 5.1 to 10.2 ms with 135 bots. The engine's collision queries slow down with the number of bodies that move each frame, and every shot is a query. The version that went in adds no bodies: the ship's own box stays for shots, and the slim shape is tested by hand.

Tested

  • Editor build, headless, all 143 bots: BLUE TEAM WINS with Red cut to 10%. All six carriers report "4 of 4 weak point panels fitted to the hull". 197 fighter kills in 150 s, so shots still land on fighters. Game logic 5.7 ms a frame with 137 bots, against 5.1 ms before this round.
  • Packaged build (Builds\2026-11-14_2210_feedback): 625 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings. Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1 reports RESULT: PASS after 115s. The log has 0 error lines, and every carrier fitted 4 of 4 panels. Frame time was 3.2 ms average with 138 bot fighters flying (game logic only), against 3.1 ms for the build before.

Coach tip (topic: collision shapes)

One shape for being hit and another for bumping into things is normal in games. Before giving every actor a second physics body, measure what it costs. Here it doubled the frame time, and a hand-made test for the second shape cost almost nothing.

14 Nov Capital ship sizeThe carrier goes to about 1 kmAndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius

Decision

The carrier will be about 1,000 m long, 3.6 times today's 280 m. That is a middle step toward the brief's ~5 km. It is not built yet.

Plan (also on the spec page: https://claude.ai/artifact/6G4t1paUPmmUAjNKs7zfwf#plan)

  • One scale setting moves most of the ship: CmPerModelMetre goes from 28000 / 190 to 100000 / 190. Turrets, weak points, the tower, the bays and the nozzles all follow it.
  • Sizes that are set on their own get scaled by hand: the thruster plumes and light, the mast beacon, and HullHalfLength (148 m → 500 m).
  • Orbits: at today's settings, 1 km hulls in one fleet would overlap by about 134 m. The fleets end up 2.8 km apart instead of 2.2 km.

  • Unchanged: carrier health, weak point health, turret damage and ranges, the railgun's 3 km reach, and every fighter number.

Also on the spec page

It now matches commit c14b83e: the gentler bay launch (7.5 → 30 m/s over 3 s, one fighter per bay every 1.2 s), the slim flying shape (6.6 × 3.2 × 1.5 m, three 2.2 m balls against other fighters), weak points as 9 m hull panels, and the 35° zoom.

14 Nov Bug fixFighters launched from the bays drifted into the carrier's hullAndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (reported the bug from a flight, and asked for it to be fixed before the next pull request). Claude found the cause and chose the fix, so the team should check the launch in a flight.

The bug

At the start of a match, the player's fighter kept moving along a fixed line while the carrier moved on, ended up inside the hull and got stuck.

Cause

  • The bay launch carried each fighter at the carrier centre's velocity, captured once at launch, along a fixed line. Ship collision is switched off for the 3 s launch, because the fighter starts inside a hangar.
  • The carrier circles and rolls, so its stern swings sideways. At 1 km that is about 7.5 m/s (2.6 m/s at 280 m), and the hangars are 3.6 times deeper. The hangar walls swung into the fighter, and when the launch ended and collision came back on, it was inside the hull.
  • Reproduced headless with a temporary probe: all 120 launches in 75 s ended 22.7 to 24.9 m off the bay's centreline, the player's included (23.1 m). For 22 of them, a line from the hangar to the fighter crosses the hull.

Fix (4 files, not committed yet)

  • New AOrionSpaceFighterPawn::LaunchFrom: for the 3 s launch the fighter rides the carrier. It moves along its own nose in the carrier's frame, so the carrier's speed, turning and rolling carry it out through the doors. Its world velocity is whatever that adds up to, and it keeps it when the catapult lets go.
  • AOrionCapitalShip::LaunchFighter uses it for the stern bays. The box-built ship's bow launch is unchanged.

Tested

  • Same probe after the fix: all 126 launches ended on the bay centreline (0.0 m off), 56 m out, none through the hull, the player's included.
  • The probe was removed afterwards. OrionGameMode.cpp is byte-identical to before (sha256 checked).
  • Headless match, Red cut to 10%: BLUE TEAM WINS after 146 s.
  • Package Builds\2026-11-14_2250_launchfix: BUILD SUCCESSFUL. Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1 passed twice (125 s and 130 s), with 0 error lines.
  • Frame time in the package: 4.8 to 5.7 ms with about 136 bot fighters (game logic only), against 3.4 to 4.0 ms for the 22:26 package. An editor run before and after the fix showed no difference at 60 s (5.1 against 4.8 ms). The likely reason is that no fighters are trapped in hulls any more, so more of them fight: 50 to 60 bots were chasing, against 47 to 53.

Not verified

  • How the launch looks and feels on screen.

Still open

  1. Andrius: fly Builds\2026-11-14_2250_launchfix.
  2. Commit the fix as its own commit on feat/turrets-and-trails and push it, then open the pull request.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: finding the cause, the probe, the fix, three editor builds, three headless matches, the package and two smoke tests, the spec page update, this entry

Coach tip (topic: moving launch platforms)

When something launches from a moving, turning ship, move it in the ship's frame until it's clear, not on a straight line in the world. A straight line only works while the ship flies straight, and the error grows with the ship's size. That's why this bug only showed up once the carrier went to 1 km.

14 Nov Bug fixThe player waited 14 s for a launch bay under a "SHIP DESTROYED" bannerAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (reported it with a screenshot of Builds\2026-11-14_2259_complete: "now when I launch the build I get this screen"). Claude found the cause and chose the fix, so the team should review it.

What Andrius saw

Seven seconds into the match the screen said "SHIP DESTROYED, Launching new fighter in 1". Nothing had been destroyed. The view was from the middle of the station: the screenshot shows the enemy capital ship 1,397 m away and his own 1,402 m away, and only the centre of the map is that far from both.

Cause

  • The carrier sends out one fighter per bay every 1.2 s since the flight feedback round (it was 0.45 s). The first carrier's 23 bots and the player all ask for a bay at once, so the last of them waits about 14 s.
  • The player was always the last. A pilot who doesn't get a bay asks again a tenth of a second later. The bots' tries fall into step with the bays: two bots always arrive a few milliseconds after a bay opens and take both.
  • A player without a ship has a view from the map's centre, and the screen for "no ship" always said "SHIP DESTROYED", with a countdown that sticks at 1 while waiting.
  • Measured headless before the fix: "Player launch 1, 14.1 s into the match", twice out of twice.

Fix

  • The player keeps asking the same carrier, on every frame, and that carrier's bots give way until the player is out.
  • Before the first launch the screen says "STAND BY TO LAUNCH, Bay doors opening". After a death it still says "SHIP DESTROYED", and a wait of under half a second reads "Launching: waiting for a clear bay" instead of a countdown.
  • Before the first launch the view is of the bays of the carrier that is about to launch the player, from astern and to one side.
  • The match log now says when each player launch happens.

A first fix that didn't work

Claude first only moved the player to the front of the list of launches that are due in a frame. The measurement stayed at 14.1 s: the player was usually not in the list in the frame the bays opened. Without the measurement this would have been packaged as fixed.

Why the tests missed it

The smoke test passes when a team wins, and the bots launch and fight without the player. No test looked at when the player gets a ship.

Tested

  • Editor build, headless: "Player launch 1, 0.8 s into the match" in four runs out of four. 0.8 s is the time the bay doors take to open. The bots fill at the same pace: 46 to 47 flying at 30 s, before and after.
  • After being shot down, the idle test player was relaunched (at 93.0 s and 90.9 s in two runs).
  • BLUE TEAM WINS with Red cut to 10%. Game logic 5.5 ms a frame with 132 bots, the same as before. One run read 11.7 ms while something else was using the PC, and was repeated.
  • Packaged build (Builds\2026-11-14_2325_playerfirst): 625 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings. Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1 reports RESULT: PASS after 125s. Its log says "Player launch 1, 0.8 s into the match" and a relaunch at 105.1 s, with 0 error lines and no missing-asset lines. Frame time 3.5 ms with 135 bot fighters (game logic only).

Not verified

  • Anything on screen: the new waiting view of the bays, the new wording, and the launch itself as the player sees it.

Still open

  1. Andrius: start Builds\2026-11-14_2325_playerfirst and check that the match opens on the carrier's bays and launches within a second.
  2. The fix is on branch fix/player-launches-first, committed and not pushed.
  3. Andy: the smoke script could fail when the log has no "Player launch 1" within 3 s of the match start.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: reading the screenshot, the launch queue and waiting screen code, the measurement before and after, both fixes, the headless matches, the package and smoke test, this entry

Coach tip (topic: queues)

When a shared thing gets scarcer, find out who waits longest. Slowing the bays was right for how the launch looks, and it turned the player's place in the queue from unnoticeable into 14 seconds. Put the player's wait into the log, because a test with no human in it never complains about waiting.

14 Nov Tooling questionCan Claude drive FL Studio?AndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Nobody yet. Claude laid out two routes, and Andrius chooses.

Question

Is it possible to connect Claude to FL Studio?

Answer

  • Yes, in two ways. Neither has been tried on this PC.
  • Route 1, MIDI files: Claude writes standard .mid files with a short Python script, and a person drags them into FL Studio. No connection and no install in FL Studio.
  • Route 2, live bridge: a community MCP server (MCP is the plug-in system Claude uses for tools) sends commands to FL Studio through a virtual MIDI cable (loopMIDI on Windows) to a Python script running inside FL Studio. Several exist, for example rosasynthesiz/flstudio-mcp, alansork/fl-studio-mcp and fruityloops-mcp on PyPI. Image-Line ships no official one.
  • In between: FL Studio's own Piano Roll scripting (Python). Claude writes a script and a person runs it from the piano roll's menu.

What Claude found in the game

The game has no audio at all: no sound files or sound assets in the repo, and no code that plays a sound (checked on fix/player-launches-first at 23:32 UTC).

Before route 2 could be used

Licence checks under the project's tool rules: the chosen community server's licence, and FL Studio's licence for any clause on AI input. Not done.

Still open

  1. Andrius: which route, if any, and who does it, given that packaging starts on Sunday morning.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: two web searches, checking the repo for audio, this entry.

Coach tip (topic: game audio in a jam)

Sound is feedback before it is music. Five short effects (gun, hit, explosion, launch, win sting) tell the player what just happened, and a silent game feels broken even when it works. Get those five in before anyone composes a track.

14 Nov AudioIdeas for weapon, engine, hum and flyby soundsAndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Nobody yet. Claude proposed the ideas below, and the team still needs to pick the scope and who builds it.

Question

How do we add sound: machine-gun sounds for the kinetic weapons, laser sounds for energy weapons, missile sounds, engine sounds on every ship, a dull, quiet hum near capital ships, and a whoosh when fighters fly past?

What the code means for audio

  • There's no audio in the project yet: no sound assets and no audio code.
  • Kinetic fires 100 rounds a second (6000 rpm) and the fleet battle has 144 fighters. One sound per round is impossible, so the gun has to be a loop that starts when the trigger goes down and stops, with a short tail, when it comes up.
  • Projectiles are plain structs drawn by AOrionProjectileManager, not actors. Missiles can't easily carry their own flight sound, so missile audio is a launch sound plus the explosion where EOrionBlastKind::Explosion already spawns.
  • Assets are loaded in C++ with ConstructorHelpers, so sounds can be loaded the same way.

Proposed sounds

  • Kinetic: a looping "brrrt" on each fighter, plus a tail when the trigger is released. At most 6 can play at once (Sound Concurrency, Stop Farthest Then Oldest), and the player's own gun always plays.
  • Energy: a laser zap for each shot (every 0.22 s), with pitch varied ±5% so it doesn't sound like a machine. At most 8 at once.
  • Missile: a launch thump and hiss, a lock-on tone for the player only, and an explosion on impact.
  • Engines: one loop per ship. Pitch follows speed (0.8 to 1.2) and boost adds volume. The player hears their own engine in 2D. Bot engines are 3D, fade out by about 1.5 km, and at most 10 play at once.
  • Capital ship hum: a very low, quiet loop (around 40–60 Hz with a slow wobble). Its attenuation shape is a capsule or box the size of the 5 km hull, so it fades with distance from the hull, not from the ship's centre.
  • Flyby whoosh: the player pawn watches fighters within about 150 m. When one reaches its closest point under about 60 m and the relative speed is high, a whoosh plays at that ship. Pitch and volume follow relative speed. Each ship can trigger it once every 1.5 s, with at most 3 at once.
  • Also suggested: the capital ship's death explosion and a win sting, because that's the payoff of the loop.

Where the sounds come from (pick one)

  • Generate the WAVs with a script in Scripts/. They're original, so no licences, and they can be rebuilt.
  • Build them in MetaSounds in the editor. That suits the engine and the hum, which change with speed.
  • Download CC0 packs and credit them in CREDITS.md.
  • .wav and .ogg are already tracked by Git LFS in .gitattributes.

Proposed order (build the loop before polish)

  1. Only after a full fleet-battle match reaches the win screen.
  2. Tier 1, about 1–2 h: kinetic loop, energy zap, missile launch, explosions, the player's engine, capital ship death and the win sting.
  3. Tier 2, about 1 h: bot engines with the voice cap, and the capital ship hum.
  4. Tier 3: flyby whoosh, lock tone, impact sounds. Cut the flyby whoosh first, since it needs the most new code.
  5. Packaging still starts Sunday morning, with or without Tier 3.

Docs

  • Sound Attenuation
  • Sound Concurrency reference
  • MetaSounds
  • These links now open the 5.9 pages. The 5.8 docs are our source of truth.

Still open

  • Has a full fleet-battle match reached the win screen yet? Audio waits until it has.
  • Who builds audio tonight, and which source: generated, MetaSounds or CC0?
  • Are the player's own sounds cockpit (2D) or out in the world (3D)?

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: low
  • Used for: reading the weapon, projectile and capital ship code, the sound proposal, this entry

Coach tip (topic: game audio in big battles)

Cap the number of voices before you add a single sound. Make two Sound Concurrency assets, one for weapons and one for engines, so 144 fighters can't drown out each other or your own engine. Then add "hear guns and engine" to the packaged smoke test, because audio that works in PIE can still go silent in a package if the assets aren't cooked.

Update, same evening: scope decided (Andy's decisions)

  • Win conditions accepted. The team has played several full matches to the win screen and is happy with the win conditions for now, so audio is no longer blocked.
  • Andy builds the audio tonight.
  • The player's ship sounds different from everyone else's. The player's own weapons and engine have their own sounds, played directly to the listener. Every other ship's sounds are muffled, but never silent, because sound doesn't travel in space.
  • Weapons and engines come first. Next is the capital ship hum, and the flyby whoosh is still the first thing to cut.
  • How Claude suggests building the muffling: the player's sounds go to a dry submix in 2D. External sounds go to an "External" submix with a low-pass filter (start around 2–3 kHz and about −6 dB), and their attenuation adds more low-pass with distance, so far-off fighters sound duller than near ones.
  • Still open: where the sounds come from (generated by a script, CC0 downloads or MetaSounds).

AI usage (this update)

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, effort low
  • Used for: the scope questions, the muffling recipe, this update

Coach tip (topic: weapon and engine sounds)

Cut the player's kinetic loop to a length that's a whole number of 10 ms steps (one round at 6000 rpm), with the loop point on a zero crossing. The sound then keeps time with the tracers and doesn't click when it loops. A gun that doesn't match its own tracers sounds wrong, even if nobody can say why.

Update, same evening: the sounds are generated by a script (Andy's decision)

  • Andy chose script-generated sounds over CC0 downloads and MetaSounds.
  • orion_81_space_ctf/Scripts/build_sounds.js (Node.js, no packages) makes 18 original WAVs in under a second, into Saved/GeneratedAudio/, which git ignores. Each run gives byte-identical files.
    • Player (2D, heavy, felt through the hull): kinetic loop and tail, energy shot, missile launch, engine loop, boost loop.
    • Other ships (lighter and mid-range, so they survive the muffling): kinetic loop and tail, energy shot, missile launch, engine loop.
    • Shared: small and capital explosions, capital ship hum, flyby whoosh, missile lock beep and locked tone, win sting.
  • The kinetic loops are 48 rounds of exactly 10 ms each (6000 rpm), and each round starts from silence, so the gun keeps time with the tracers. The script checks every loop seam, and none jumps more than the loop's own biggest step, so none should click.
  • orion_81_space_ctf/Scripts/import_sounds.py imports them into /Game/Audio/Generated and sets the _Loop sounds to loop. It also creates:
    • ATT_External_Ship: other ships' sounds are 3D and fade out by 1.5 km. They're always low-passed (3 kHz up close, 700 Hz at 1.5 km), so space sounds muffled but never silent. Attenuation filtering was used instead of a submix, because it's one asset and every external sound picks it up by itself.
    • Voice caps, nearest kept: guns 6, lasers 8, missiles 6, engines 10, explosions 10.
    • The player's sounds have no attenuation (2D, dry) and priority 4, so they always get a voice.
  • Verified: it ran headless in UE 5.8 with 0 errors, 18 of 18 imported. A second headless pass read back every setting on the saved assets. The new .uasset files are in Git LFS (filter: lfs), 2.4 MB in total.
  • Not verified: how anything sounds. Claude can't listen. Andy should preview the SW_* sounds in the Content Browser. To change a sound, edit its line in SOUNDS in build_sounds.js, then run both scripts again.
  • Not done: the C++ that plays the sounds (fire, release, engine pitch, explosions), and the capital ship hum's attenuation, which has to match the carrier's real size.
  • CREDITS.md now says the sounds are original and generated from code.

Update, same evening: weapons, engines and explosions are wired up (Andy asked for weapons and engines)

  • Fighter pawn (OrionSpaceFighterPawn): three audio components (gun, engine, boost) and two sound sets. The ship the local player flies uses PlayerSounds (2D, heavy). Every other ship uses ExternalSounds, which ATT_External_Ship muffles. The set is chosen every frame, so respawns and possession swap it without extra hooks.
    • Kinetic: FireSelected() records the time of each round. While rounds keep coming (less than 0.1 s apart), one loop plays. When they stop (trigger up, weapon changed, ship disabled), the loop stops and the barrel spin-down tail plays.
    • Energy: one zap per shot, pitch varied ±5%. Missiles: one launch sound each.
    • Engine: pitch goes from 0.8 at a standstill to 1.2 at full speed, a little higher while boosting, and volume follows thrust. Both glide over about 0.15 s so they don't flutter. A disabled engine sputters. The player's boost layer fades in on top. Other ships boost louder and higher on their one loop.
    • Engine voices: SCON_Engines keeps the nearest 10. A refused loop tries again every second, so the nearest ships win their voices back as they move. Bot loops start at random points so they aren't in phase.
  • New OrionAudio.h: ships more than 1.5 km from the player don't start sounds at all, so 144 fighters don't use up the caps with sounds nobody hears.
  • Projectile manager: every Explosion blast plays a sound, pitched by size (missile bright, fighter fuller, weak point deep). A capital ship's death (120 m blast) plays SW_Explosion_Capital in 2D, so everyone hears the match's big moment.
  • Sounds are loaded with ConstructorHelpers, so the cook picks them up with the classes.
  • Verified: the editor target built with no warnings. A headless match (editor build, -game -nullrhi -nosound, Red cut to 10%) reached BLUE TEAM WINS in 88 s with up to 86 bots flying. It logged no new errors and no Failed to find for any sound. The only errors were the known GameFeatureData line and Epic's editor-toolset Python plugins.

Update, same evening: round 2 of the sounds, from Andy's notes (Andy's decisions)

  • Andy's notes: the machine gun should be lower, and its timing with the fire rate is good. The spin-down should sound like a motorised barrel, not a cowbell. The laser should be less bright. The missile launch needed a better sound. The engine is good, but the boost "roar" should become a low rumble that varies in pitch and volume, because it dominated the mix. The lock beep is fine, but the locked tone is too bright and harsh. The explosions sounded like drums. The other sounds wait for later.
  • What changed. Brightness is the spectral centroid, measured on the WAVs before and after:
    • Machine gun: a darker body and knock, and the two wing cannons now alternate (the game already alternates mounts), which drops the pitch. Still exactly one round every 10 ms. Brightness 614 → 218 Hz for the player, 3,007 → 460 Hz for other ships.
    • Spin-down: a motor whine that falls with the barrels' rotation, barrel clatter slowing to a stop and a soft clunk. The three fixed ringing tones (the cowbell) are gone, which the before-and-after spectrograms show.
    • Laser: starts lower (1.3 kHz instead of 2.6 kHz), fewer overtones, a soft breath instead of the crack, and a steeper top-end cut. Brightness 786 → 472 Hz.
    • Missile launch: new. The rail clamp, a puff of ejection gas, an ignition whump, then a crackling roar that dulls as the missile pulls away. It's all noise, with no tones.
    • Boost: now a low rumble (38 and 57 Hz, wandering ±3–4 Hz) that swells and sags. About 6 dB quieter, with 96% of its energy below 150 Hz.
    • Locked tone: 660 Hz instead of 1.8 kHz, pure, with a gentle 4 Hz pulse. About 9 dB quieter.
    • Explosions: built from noise instead of a falling sine, which is how a drum is made. A crack, a fireball that darkens as it spreads and rolls with turbulence, a pressure rumble and debris crackle. A tonality score (how far the strongest narrow peak stands above its neighbours) fell from 28 dB to 12 dB, the range of noise.
  • Found and fixed along the way: every loop that mixed steady tones with noise (both engines and the capital ship hum) swelled by 2.1–2.3 dB each time it restarted, because the loop's cross-fade doubled the tones. Tones and noise now loop separately, and the engines keep exactly the level Andy approved (measured at -11.14 dB before and -11.11 dB after).
  • Each sound now gets its own random seed from its name, so changing one sound never changes another.
  • Soundboard (same link, version 2): reworked sounds are marked, each says what changed, and a Previous button plays the version it replaced. Checked locally: all 18 sounds load, all 15 previous versions play, loops toggle, there are no console errors and no sideways scrolling.
  • Not done yet: the new sounds aren't imported into Unreal. The editor is open, so Andy runs Scripts/import_sounds.py from Tools > Execute Python Script.
  • Not verified: how any of it sounds. Claude checked with measurements and spectrograms, not by ear.

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: max, at Andy's request for this round
  • Used for: the sound redesigns, an analysis script (brightness, band energy, tonality, spectrograms) to check them without listening, the soundboard's previous-version playback, this update

Coach tip (topic: tuning sounds)

Test every sound on laptop speakers as well as headphones. Laptop speakers play almost nothing below 150 Hz, and the new boost (96% below 150 Hz) and your machine gun (84%) live mostly down there. If the gun sounds thin on a laptop, raise its knock (chunkGain), not its volume. Jam judges often play on laptops.

Update, same evening: other ships' engine is now your engine, muffled (Andy's decision)

  • Andy asked for other ships' engine to sound the same as the player's, with more low-pass.
  • SW_Ext_Engine_Loop is now built from the player's engine settings (PLAYER_ENGINE in build_sounds.js, shared by both) and passed through a 12 dB-per-octave low-pass at 600 Hz (EXT_ENGINE_LOWPASS_HZ). The filter runs over the loop as a loop, so it still loops cleanly. It has its own rumble texture, so a crowd of engines doesn't sound like one sample. In game, ATT_External_Ship still muffles it further with distance.
  • Measured against the player's engine: the hum (42, 84 and 126 Hz) is within 0.3 dB, the 1.24 kHz whine is 13 dB down, and its 2.48 kHz overtone is 25 dB down. Overall level is within 0.3 dB. The other 17 sounds are byte-identical to before.
  • The soundboard (same link, version 3) has the new engine. Previous on its card plays the original other-ships engine.
  • Trade-off to know: 99% of this engine's energy is below 150 Hz, where laptop speakers play almost nothing. On a laptop, other ships' engines will be hard to hear. Raising EXT_ENGINE_LOWPASS_HZ to about 900 keeps more of the whine and makes them easier to place.
  • Still not in Unreal: run Scripts/import_sounds.py (Tools > Execute Python Script) to pick up this round.
14 Nov AudioLicence-free recordings for each sound slotAndyOpus 5.5Sonnet 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy (search the web for realistic, licence-free sounds for every slot, with five Sonnet agents, starting with the player's own sounds). Which recordings to use is still open: Andy picks on the shortlist page.

Question

Can we find real, licence-free recordings for each sound slot, to build custom sounds from instead of synthesizing everything?

How it was done

  • Five Claude Sonnet 5.5 agents searched in parallel, each taking a group of slots: machine gun and spin-down; laser and missile launch; engine, boost and capital ship hum; explosions; and flyby, lock-on tones and win sting. They only read web pages: no downloads, sign-ins or new accounts.
  • Licence rule: only CC0 and public domain, read on each sound's own page. This matches earlier team decisions, which kept CC BY 4.0 out (the sky map and Tripo). CC BY, CC BY-SA, non-commercial terms and "free with our own licence" sites (Pixabay, Mixkit, Zapsplat, Sonniss) were all excluded.
  • Claude (Opus 5.5) then re-read the licence on 19 of the candidates' own pages, including every top pick except the lock-tone ping, and checked provenance where it was doubtful.

Result

  • Shortlist page: https://claude.ai/artifact/ByvpS7p54S7VKpn4q75ZFd. It has 12 slots and 59 candidates, a top pick per slot, and checkboxes. Copy picks gives a list to paste back to Claude.
  • Top picks:
    • Machine gun: a real Garwood M134 minigun recorded 4 ft from the gun.
    • Spin-down: the end of a minigun recording where the barrels spin down.
    • Laser: a recording of a metal Springy (the classic blaster trick).
    • Missile launch: a real F-class hobby rocket, plus a heavy mechanical clamp.
    • Engine: a jet engine test from a US Government video (public domain).
    • Boost: an F-4 Phantom afterburner takeoff.
    • Capital ship hum: a transformer humming in a large room.
    • Explosion: a real firework blast, plus a debris crash.
    • Capital ship destroyed: long rolling thunder.
    • Flyby: a clean designed jet pass-by.
    • Lock-on tones: a soft sonar ping.
    • Win sting: a CC0 victory fanfare on OpenGameArt.
  • Left out on provenance: three CC0-labelled uploads (an engine room, a tesla coil and falling debris). The uploader's page says they are copies of 1930s–60s Hollywood studio effects reels, so the CC0 isn't theirs to give. Also left out: one AI-generated afterburner and several CC BY and non-commercial sounds.
  • Gaps the agents reported: no clean single gun round, no CC0 electric gun-motor spin-down, no struck guy-wire, no steady real engine run over 30 s, and no public-domain cockpit tones. Synthesis keeps covering those parts.

Still open

  • Andy picks on the page, then downloads the Freesound files while signed in (13 of the 14 default picks need a free Freesound account; Claude can't sign in) into orion_81_space_ctf/Saved/SourceAudio/, which git ignores.
  • The OpenGameArt fanfare is a direct download. Claude can fetch it once Andy agrees.
  • Two top picks are FLAC (the engine and the hum). This PC has no ffmpeg, so they need it installed, which needs Andy's OK. WAV and AIFF can be read without it.
  • Not searched: Wikimedia Commons and Internet Archive. NASA was only searched in part: one Space Shuttle launch recording on NASA's SoundCloud, not checked for download.

AI usage

  • Models: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5, max effort) planned the search, verified licences and built the page. Five Claude Sonnet 5.5 agents (claude-sonnet-5-5, default effort) did the searching.
  • Used for: web research, licence checks, the shortlist page, this entry

Coach tip (topic: sourcing audio)

When you download a source, write its page URL, licence label and the date into CREDITS.md that same minute, even for CC0, which needs no credit. If a licence is ever questioned (one explosion here has an "is this really CC0?" comment under it), the record of what the page said and when is your evidence.

Update, same evening: a source for the boost rumble (Andy's question)

  • Andy asked whether TheLittleCrow's "Rocket Launch Boost and Burning (Version B)" (https://freesound.org/people/TheLittleCrow/sounds/774270/) can be the boost rumble.
  • Claude's answer: yes.
    • Licence: "Creative Commons 0", read on the sound's page and its pack page. WAV, 96 kHz/24-bit, stereo, 25 s, 13.7 MB. It needs a Freesound sign-in to download, and no ffmpeg (it's WAV).
    • Fit, judged from Freesound's own waveform and spectrogram images: one constant level for all 25 s, with no launch hit or fade, and most of its energy in the low end, so it loops well. The author describes extra high-frequency distortion, which the low-pass for a rumble removes. Because it holds one level, the pitch and volume wander Andy asked for will come from processing.
  • Plan: resample to 48 kHz, take a steady stretch, low-pass it around 180 Hz, add slow swells and a ±4% pitch wander, loop it with a cross-fade, and keep it about as quiet as the current boost so it stays under the engine.
  • The shortlist now has it as the boost's top pick, with the F-4 afterburner as an alternate.
  • Still open: Andy downloads the WAV into orion_81_space_ctf/Saved/SourceAudio/, and decides whether it replaces the synthesized rumble or is blended with it.

Update, same evening: your engine rebuilt from the F-4 recording (Andy's decision)

  • Andy asked for the engine to use the afterburner recording, cut into sections where its pitch changes, and driven by the ship's throttle like the earlier speed-based pitch. He also said to download the chosen sounds from now on, since he had signed in to Freesound.

To keep going, Claude used Freesound's public previews of both chosen sounds, decoded to WAV in Saved/SourceAudio/ (not in git). The originals replace them once Andy signs in in Claude's browser pane.
  • Pitch map of the recording, from a spectrogram and a track of the whine's frequency:
    • Idle, 1–6 s: whine lines steady at 1,753 and 2,500 Hz.
    • Spool-up, 6.0–7.2 s: the whine rises 19% (1,757 → 2,082 Hz) before the roar buries it.
    • Full power, 11.5–18.5 s: a steady roar.
    • Afterburner, 24–28.6 s: a heavier roar.
    • After that the aircraft moves away.
  • Three seamless loops were cut at those points (idle 3.6 s, full power 7 s, afterburner 4.6 s), resampled to 48 kHz and set to the same loudness. Checked: the idle loop's whine is at 1,751 Hz, and every loop seam is clean.
  • Mixing (ENGINE_MIX in build_sounds.js, same numbers in the pawn's PlayerEngineLayers): engine RPM follows the throttle with a jet's spool lag, where 0 is idle, 1 full throttle and 1.25 boosting. Between 30% and 80% RPM the idle fades out and full power fades in, and the idle pitches up 19% like the real spool-up. Boosting crossfades into the afterburner roar.
  • Soundboard version 4 has an "Engine, recorded: throttle" card with a throttle slider and Hold to boost, running the same mix. Checked in a browser: at RPM 0 the idle is at 55%; at RPM 1 full power is at 100%; at RPM 1.25 the afterburner is at 85% and full power at 50%. The spool-down lag works.
  • C++: the player's ship gets one audio component per stage, with volume and pitch set from the curves every frame, and stages that fade out stop. Other ships keep their single muffled engine. It compiled through the game target (22 s, no warnings). The editor build and the sound import wait until the editor is closed.
  • CREDITS.md now lists both recordings. It previously said no recordings were used, which is no longer true.
  • Observation: the idle stage is thin, almost all whine with little low end. That's true to a jet at idle, but lighter than the synthesized engine. If it feels weak, a little of the old synthesized rumble can go under it.
  • Still open: Andy signs in to Freesound in Claude's browser pane so the originals can be downloaded; the editor is closed so the C++ can be built and the sounds imported; and the boost uses the recording alone or blended (Andy's call).

Update, 15 Nov just after midnight: the machine gun from the real M134 recording (Andy's decisions)

  • Andy: the recorded engine "sounds great", so it stays.   • The recording (rob762x51's "Minigun Burst Audio", a Garwood M134G with camera mics about 4 ft from the gun) was mapped from its Freesound preview, since Claude's browser still isn't signed in:
    • Spin-up: silence, then a dull motor rumble from 0.16 s, then the barrels' whine from 0.6 s.
    • Steady fire: from 1.00 s to 31.40 s, one continuous 30-second burst at about 33 rounds a second (about 2,000 rpm).
    • The ending: the last round decays by 31.48 s, then a short rundown and the whine fade to silence by 32.6 s.
  • Loop: 1.284–31.111 s, 29.8 s long. A first cut chose the loop end by matching waveforms, but checking the rounds showed the seam could stumble. The loop is now cut halfway between two rounds at each end, so the gap across the seam is one normal gap. Measured with the loop played twice: 30.0 ms across the join against a median of 30.8 ms between rounds.
  • Spin-down: the build finds where firing stops (31.46 s in the preview) and starts the tail 80 ms earlier, so it opens on the last rounds and runs 1.29 s to the recording's own silence. It's found from the audio so it still lands right on the original file. Both cuts share one gain, so the spin-down keeps its real level against the firing.
  • C++: each burst starts at a random point in the 30-second loop (Duration, because GetDuration() reports "forever" for looping sounds), and the gun fades in over 8 ms and out over 12 ms so a start or stop in the middle of a round doesn't click. The soundboard's Hold to fire does the same.
  • build_sounds.js also reads AIFF now, since the original of this recording is an AIFF. Tested by a round trip against the WAV reader: all 1,440,535 frames matched exactly.
  • Checked: the editor build succeeded (no warnings); 21 of 21 sounds imported; the player's gun reads back as a 29.83 s loop and a 1.29 s one-shot; a headless match reached BLUE TEAM WINS in 88 s with no new errors. Soundboard version 5 marks round 3, and Previous plays the synthesized gun from round 2.

Coach tip (topic: looping rhythmic recordings)

Cut a rhythmic loop between beats, never on them, and check it by playing it twice in a row, not by looking at one copy. Both seam checks tonight first gave a false alarm, and only the doubled playback showed the real 30 ms join.

Update, 15 Nov: the laser combined from two recordings (Andy's decision)

  • Andy asked for the two laser picks to be combined into one sound, with their loudest points matched and the dead zone at the start shortened. The picks are "Laser short 2.wav" by Defunct3 (a real metal Springy) and "Gun, Laser, Single Shot, Sci Fi" by Kinoton. Both are CC0, and Claude read both licences on the sounds' own pages.
  • Measured from the Freesound previews:
    • Kinoton's file opens with 230 ms of digital silence, peaks at 290 ms, and is silent again after 0.7 s.
    • The Springy has no silence, but a faint 130 ms lead-in before it peaks at 157 ms.
    • The Springy supplies the low, wobbling body; Kinoton supplies the classic falling "pew" (bright tones sweeping down).
  • How they're combined (recordedLaser in build_sounds.js): each is trimmed to start where it comes within 6 dB of its own peak, with an 8 ms run-up, and to end 45 dB below it. Each is scaled to the same level at its loudest moment, then they're lined up so those moments coincide, and mixed equally.
    • A first pass, trimming at 12 dB below the peak, still took 89 ms to reach the peak, so the trim was tightened.
  • Result: 0.72 s, loudest 27 ms in (was 157 ms and 290 ms in the originals), 30 dB down by 0.53 s. The spectrogram shows both layers starting together.
  • Note: it's brighter than the synthesized laser (27% of its energy above 2 kHz, against almost none). Andy chose these recordings after listening, so their character was kept. A gentle top cut is a one-line change if it's too bright in game.
  • Checked: 21 of 21 sounds imported; the laser reads back as a 0.72 s one-shot at player priority; a headless match reached BLUE TEAM WINS in 90 s with no new errors. Soundboard version 6: Hold to fire plays it every 0.22 s with ±5% pitch, and Previous plays the round-2 laser.
  • Other ships' lasers are still synthesized (player sounds first). CREDITS.md lists both recordings.

Update, 15 Nov: the missile launch from three stacked rocket launches (Andy's decisions)

  • Andy is happy with the combined laser. For the missile launch: use qubodup's "Rocket Launch.flac" (CC0, from a US Government video), splice its three rocket sounds together, shorten the thunderous end and trim the dead zone at the start.
  • Mapping the preview showed three separate launches, separated by digital silence: 0.5–6.5 s, 7.25–18.3 s and 19.0–26 s. Each is a big low thump, then a roar that darkens into a thunder-like rumble.
  • Claude asked how to splice them, because a missile fires every 1.2 s and three launches back to back would run about 20 s. Andy chose to stack them.
  • How (recordedMissile in build_sounds.js):
    • The three launches are found automatically from the silences between them.
    • Each is trimmed to start right at its thump and set to the same level there, and the three thumps are lined up and mixed.
    • The thunder is shortened: full level for 0.9 s after the thump, then a smooth 1.1 s fade.
    • The finished stack is trimmed once more, because one launch has a faint ignition hiss about 180 ms before its thump. Lining up the thumps had moved that hiss to the front, leaving a 175 ms dead zone; 179 ms was trimmed.
  • Result: 2.06 s, sound from 3 ms, thump at 55 ms, then the motor roar igniting at about 0.4 s at the same instant in all three launches (checked on the spectrogram), fading out by the end. Levels: thump -13 dB, roar -21 dB, last 0.3 s -45 dB.
  • Checked: 21 of 21 sounds imported; the launch reads back as a 2.06 s one-shot at player priority; a headless match reached BLUE TEAM WINS in 84 s with no new errors. Soundboard version 7: Hold to fire plays it every 1.2 s, and Previous plays the round-2 launch.
  • Note: the original is FLAC, which build_sounds.js can't read. Until it's converted to WAV, or ffmpeg is installed, the preview stays the source. Other ships' launches are still synthesized.

AI usage (this update)

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), max effort

Update, 15 Nov: missile launches vary, and the roar follows the thump sooner (Andy's decisions)

  • Andy: the stacked launch "sounds great". Two changes asked for: variance or warble so it changes slightly between launches, and a shorter delay between the opening thump and the roar.
  • Measured on the launch above 1 kHz: the thump peaks at 50 ms, there's a near-silent gap from 250 to 350 ms, and the roar ignites at about 370 ms, so the roar came 0.32 s after the thump.
  • Gap closed: closeGap keeps the thump for 0.1 s after its peak, then cuts to 20 ms before the roar ignites, with a 20 ms crossfade. The build finds the gap and the ignition from the audio. Every variant now reads 0.32 s → 0.12 s.
  • Four variants (MISSILE_VARIANTS, each from its own seed):
    • Warble: playback speed wanders ±0.8–1.6% (an average of a 3.5–6.5 Hz and a 0.6–1.4 Hz wobble), with cubic interpolation.
    • Flutter: a 4–8% loudness shimmer at 7–12 Hz.
    • A fixed pitch shift of up to ±2%.
    • A different mix of the three launches: each level varied ±15%, and the later two nudged by up to 1 ms. A first try with 4 ms nudges smeared the thumps and made two variants 3.5–4 dB louder, so the nudges were cut to 1 ms. Loudness now spans under 1 dB.
    • Checked that they really differ: the roars don't correlate between any pair (-0.05 to 0.07), while the thumps stay related in some pairs, so the punch is consistent.
  • Game: FOrionShipSounds has MissileLaunchVariants. Each player launch plays a random variant at a random pitch of ±5%; other ships' launches get the ±5% pitch too. The soundboard's Play and Hold to fire do the same. Checked: 8 presses picked B, B, D, A, B, B, C, D at pitches from 0.951 to 1.037.
  • Checked: 24 of 24 sounds imported; the editor build succeeded with no warnings; a headless match reached BLUE TEAM WINS in 86 s with no new errors, and no sound failed to load.

Coach tip (topic: sounds in a packaged build)

Give every sound a real reference from code or a Blueprint, never just a path string. The cooker packs only what something references, which is why tonight's build left out the five sounds nothing plays yet. When someone wires the hum or the win sting, set it the way the weapons are: a UPROPERTY filled by ConstructorHelpers, not LoadObject with a path at run time. A path alone is how the shield material went missing from the first package on Saturday. Then check that the build's Manifest_UFSFiles_Win64.txt lists it.
