15 NovRelease request to main; see-through weak points, team-coloured trails and a random climb for capital shipsAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (open the release pull request to main; make the orange weak point objects transparent so the hull shows; bring team colours back to the fighters' plumes; have the capital ships fly up or down by an angle that is always random). Claude chose the numbers listed under "Choices", so the team should review them.

Release to main

  • Pull request #36, develop into main: https://github.com/orion-eighty-one/orion-81-space-jam/pull/36. It carries pull requests #33, #34 and #35, 59 files, and a test merge is clean. Only Andrius approves and merges it.
  • It does not include the patch below, which was made after it was opened.

What changed in the patch

  • Weak points. On the carrier they are no longer solid orange discs. Each is a patch of see-through orange light lying on the hull, so the hull shows through. It still flashes when hit and pulses as it weakens, and shots still land on it.
  • Fighter trails. Team colours again: blue for Blue, orange for Red. They stay see-through, dimmer near the camera, and thin for the player's own ship.
  • Capital ships. Each ship's circle is tipped out of the flat plane by a random angle, up or down, about a random line, new every match. A ship climbs above the station on one half of its lap and dives below it on the other. The fixed table of six tilts is gone. The match log says each ship's circle and tilt.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. The random angle is between 5 and 20 degrees. In 400 simulated fleets the closest two 1 km hulls of one fleet came was 190 m. With the old fixed table it was 300 m. With 10 to 30 degrees some fleets came within 64 m, so 20 is the top.
  2. The line the circle is tipped about is random too, so where on its lap a ship is highest differs for every ship.
  3. The weak point light has 8% of the strength a solid glowing ball needs, and the patch stands 0.9 m proud of the hull, where the disc stood 2.5 m.
  4. "Plumes" was read as the fighters' engine trails, as in the round before. The capital ships' thruster plumes are unchanged.

Tested

  • Random climb, in two headless matches: tilts from 5.1 to 19.2 degrees in one, and 7.1 to 15.9 in the other with two of the six downward. No two ships got the same.
  • Editor build, headless, all 143 bots: BLUE TEAM WINS with Red cut to 10%. All six carriers fitted 4 of 4 weak point patches. The player launched 0.8 s into the match. Game logic 5.4 ms a frame with 133 bots, unchanged.
  • Packaged build (Builds\2026-11-15_0000_patch3): 625 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings. Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1 reports RESULT: PASS after 145s. Its log shows six different tilts, four of them downward (from 5.0 to 19.6 degrees), "Player launch 1, 0.8 s into the match", 0 error lines and no missing-asset lines. Frame time 3.6 ms with 140 bot fighters (game logic only).

Not verified

  • Anything on screen. Whether the weak points can still be found now that they are faint (the HUD's brackets still mark them). How the team-coloured trails look with the see-through material. How steep a 20-degree climb looks on a 1 km ship.

Still open

  1. Andrius: approve and merge PR #36, and fly Builds\2026-11-15_0000_patch3.
  2. This patch is on branch feat/see-through-weakpoints-team-trails-random-climb, committed and not pushed.
  3. The commit with this entry also carries the "can Claude drive FL Studio?" entry that another Claude session of Andrius's left uncommitted in the shared folder, unchanged.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: the release pull request and its description, the simulation of random tilts, the code for the weak points, trails and paths, the headless matches, the package and smoke test, this entry

Coach tip (topic: random values)

A random setting needs a safe range, and a simulation finds it faster than a playtest. Four hundred random fleets took seconds and showed that 30 degrees lets two capital ships nearly touch. Then log what was rolled, so that a bug report can be matched with the roll that caused it.

Update, same day: PR #36 merged, and the patch is sent for review

  • Andrius merged pull request #36, so main (af49de3) now has everything develop had before this patch.
  • For the patch Andrius chose "Open the PR". The other choice offered was "I'll fly it first". The pull request goes from feat/see-through-weakpoints-team-trails-random-climb into develop. This note was written before it was opened, so it has no number here.
  • Nobody has flown Builds\2026-11-15_0000_patch3 yet. All three changes are about looks, which no test judged.
  • After the patch merges into develop, main needs one more release request to get it.
15 NovHeadlights on F, flight assist on G, and a zoom that keeps the crosshair centredAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (F switches headlights on the player's ship; bot ships sometimes switch theirs on when they get close to their target and sometimes don't, and never have them on when they are not within 50 m of the target they are trying to kill; the right-mouse zoom must keep the reticle in the middle of the screen, not near the top). Claude chose the details listed under "Choices", so the team should review them.

What changed

  • Headlights. Every fighter has a spotlight in its nose and a small lamp that glows while it is on. The player switches it with F, or D-pad right on a gamepad.
  • Flight assist moved from F to G. Andrius asked for F twice after Claude pointed out that F was flight assist. It is still Y on a gamepad. The help line at the bottom of the screen now reads "RMB zoom F lights G assist".
  • Bots. A bot never has its headlights on further than 50 m from the fighter or weak point it is going for. Inside 50 m it has them on or not, decided once for each quarry. A disabled bot's lights go out.
  • Zoom. While the right mouse button is held, the camera turns to look straight at the aim point. The chase camera sits behind and above the ship, which puts the aim point 6.9 degrees above the middle of the view. That is a quarter of the way up the screen at the normal 85-degree view, and near the top edge once the view narrows to 35 degrees.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. Half of the time. A bot uses its lights on one quarry in two.
  2. The light: 80,000 candela, 300 m reach, a 10 to 22 degree cone, slightly cool white, no shadows. Without shadows a dozen lights at once stay cheap, and the light shines through things in its way.
  3. "Target" includes weak points. On a strike run the 50 m is measured to the weak point.
  4. A new ship starts with its lights off, for the player too.

Tested

  • With a temporary probe (removed afterwards, both files checked by hash), headless, 150 s:
    • Zoom off: the aim point is 6.90 degrees from the middle of the view. Zoom on: 0.00 degrees, at a 35-degree view.
    • The furthest from its quarry any bot had its lights on was 50.0 m. There were 241 switch-ons. Inside 50 m the lights were on in 52% of bot frames.
  • Editor build, headless, all 143 bots: BLUE TEAM WINS with Red cut to 10%. Between 2 and 8 bot headlights were on at each 30-second mark. The player launched 0.8 s into the match. Game logic 5.7 ms a frame with 135 bots, unchanged.
  • Packaged build (Builds\2026-11-15_0028_headlights): 625 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings. Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1 reports RESULT: PASS after 110s, with "Player launch 1, 0.8 s into the match", 0 error lines and no missing-asset lines. Its log shows 4, 0 and 9 bot headlights on at the three 30-second marks. Frame time 3.1 ms with 138 bot fighters (game logic only).

Not verified

  • Anything on screen. How bright the headlight is, whether it lights hulls and station parts usefully, how the lamp looks, and how the zoom feels now that the player's own ship drops out of the bottom of the zoomed view.
  • The cost of the lights when drawn. The headless runs measure game logic only. Lights are capped by the 50 m rule, not by a number.

Still open

  1. Andrius: fly Builds\2026-11-15_0028_headlights, press F, and hold the right mouse button.
  2. The patch is on branch feat/headlights-and-centred-zoom, committed and not pushed.
  3. main has the release (PR #36). It lacks PR #37's patch and this one.
  4. No Shipping build has been made yet.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: the headlight, key and zoom code, the bot rule, the probe and its measurements, the headless matches, the package and smoke test, this entry

Coach tip (topic: rules for bots)

Write a bot rule so that it cannot be broken, then measure it anyway. "Never on beyond 50 m" is one line that runs every frame, and the probe still earned its keep: it showed 50.0 m as the furthest, and that the "sometimes" really came out near half.

15 Nov Ten fixesBattleship salvos and cruise missiles, engine plumes that read as gas, a true 225 m/s boost, g-limited turnsAndriusFable 5.2Opus 5.5Sonnet 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (the ten fixes below, and the fan-out: Sonnet 5.5 as executors, Opus 5.5 reviewing the code). His first message asked for plumes "20% less opaque"; he sent the list again with "20% less transparent", and that is what was built. Claude chose the numbers and details listed under "Choices", so the team should review them.

The ten fixes and what was built

  1. Big guns shoot only at capital ships, like battleship cannons. The railgun no longer shoots at fighters. It fires salvos of 8 shells, 1.5 s apart, the two barrels taking turns (4 each), then reloads for 9.5 s. Each ship starts its first salvo at a random time, so the fleets are out of step. Each shell aims at a different spot along the target's hull and leads it. A shell is drawn large: a 5 m head and a 60 m tail.
  2. Six cruise missile cells on the top deck. Each has its own clock: first launch 5 to 35 s after the ship arrives, then every 24 to 36 s. Each launch picks one living enemy capital ship at random and a random point on it. The missile climbs 600 m straight up from the deck, then flies a parabola down onto the target and follows it as it moves. Each cell is marked with a hatch and a rim in the team's colour.
  3. Capital ship engine plumes. Each nozzle now has a wide faint sheath, a brighter middle layer, a white-hot core with small bright knots in it, and a flicker. A new material (M_EnginePlume) makes a cone's outline melt away and its glow fade from the nozzle to the tip.
  4. Fighter engine plumes. Every fighter engine has a sheath in the team's colour and a white-hot core, which grow with thrust, with shock diamonds on boost when the fighter is near the camera. The long engine trails stay.
  5. Gatling tracers. A tracer is now drawn along its motion as the player sees it (the round's velocity minus the player's own), not along its world velocity. It lights 20 m after the muzzle, so there is no blob on the nose, and has a small white-yellow head and a tail that tapers to an orange-red point.
  6. Laser: bolt speed 220 to 330 m/s and one shot every 0.147 s (was 0.22 s). Rockets: each has a plume and a short trail.
  7. Plumes 20% less transparent: the glow of every plume and trail layer is multiplied by 1.2.
  8. Weak point blob deleted. On the carrier model the weak points are no longer drawn. They are still there: a hit on one still counts three times and it can still be destroyed.
  9. Boost on Shift, 1.5 times faster: 225 m/s (see "What the reviews found").
  10. Turns limited by g-force: the nose turns no faster than 9 g allows at the ship's speed. The HUD's flight line now shows the g, and "LIMIT" at the limit.

How the work was split

ItemWhatExecutorReview
JAM-0001Capital ship: salvos, missile cells, plumes, weak pointsSonnet 5.5Opus 5.5: accepted, 1 should-fix, 7 notes
JAM-0002Projectile manager: missile flight, shells, fighter plumes, tracers, rocket plumesSonnet 5.5Opus 5.5: accepted, 2 should-fixes, 8 notes
JAM-0003Fighter: laser, boost, g-limited turnsSonnet 5.5Opus 5.5: changes requested, 1 blocker
JAM-0004The plume materialSonnet 5.5Checked by picture (main session)
JAM-0005, JAM-0006The fixes from the three reviewsSonnet 5.5Checked by build, pictures and matches

The main session wrote the plan, froze the interfaces between the items before any executor started (two commits), merged the four results, and did the picture checks. The plan, the items with each executor's log and each review are in D:\Games\2026 Epic Jam\tracker\.

What the reviews found

  • The fighter's speed cap never held (Opus, blocker). Thrust was added before the cap was checked, and above the cap the speed only fell at the braking rate, which is weaker than the thrust. So holding W crept from 50 m/s up to 150 m/s, and boost reached 150 m/s, not the 110 m/s in the code. With the new values the same fault would have given 220 m/s with the nose limited to 23 degrees a second. Fixed: above its ceiling a ship now always slows.
  • Bots judged their braking distance for the old speeds (Opus). Their look-ahead for obstacles now includes the distance needed to slow down, and they let go of boost 1 km from where they are going.
  • A shell's 60 m tail was drawn at full length from its first frame, back through the gun (Opus). Fixed.
  • Shells were stopped by sister ships of the firing ship's own fleet (Opus; already so before). Fixed: rounds pass through capital ships of their own team.
  • Also fixed from the reviews: work done every frame for the two rounds in three that are never drawn; the new glowing meshes are kept out of the ray-tracing scene; a salvo no longer waits on an aim spot its gun cannot reach.

What the pictures found

The game mode has a new dev tool that takes pictures of a running match from placed cameras, with no window (see "Dev tool" below). The first picture of the new capital ship plumes showed three faults that no log and no code review had caught:

  • The plume tips were green. The material faded each colour channel by a different axis of the cone, because a mask node keeps red and green unless told otherwise. Blue faded along the length and red and green did not. One line in the script.
  • The shock diamonds looked like strings of white pearls, bigger than the flame they sat in. They are now small knots inside the core.
  • The flat base of each cone showed as a bright disc on the nozzle. The material now also fades the glow in from nothing over the first 4% of the length, which hides the base.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. Boost is 225 m/s: 1.5 times the 150 m/s that boost really reached, not 1.5 times the 110 in the code (165 m/s would have felt like +10%). Boost pushes at 8,800 cm/s2 (about 9 g) as before, so cruise to top boost speed takes about 2 s.
  2. Holding W still builds speed slowly from 50 up to 150 m/s, as it did by accident. It is now written down as intended (SustainedMaxSpeed, OverCruiseThrust), so normal flight feels as before and only boost is new.
  3. 9 g. Turn limit by speed: 50 m/s 101 degrees a second (above the ship's own 90 and 80, so no change), 100 m/s 51, 150 m/s 34, 225 m/s 22 (a turn 570 m in radius). The faster you fly, the wider you turn; let go of W to turn tighter. Roll is not limited. The mouse is limited too: what it asks for beyond the limit is dropped.
  4. Capital ship weapon damage: 25 a shell (the gun's average stays 10 a second, as the railgun had) and 50 a cruise missile (about 10 a second from one ship's six cells). Three ships on 60,000 health: with no fighters at all, the capital ships alone would need about 17 minutes to finish a fleet.
  5. Laser range unchanged (lifetime divided by 1.5), because bots judge their firing distance from it. Damage per shot is unchanged, so damage per second is 1.5 times higher.
  6. Weak points keep their HUD markers. Without them nothing would show where to aim.
  7. Shock diamonds kept, small. In vacuum a real plume has none; they are there because people read them as "engine at full power".
  8. Cruise missiles cannot be shot down and pass through everything except their target ship.

Dev tool: pictures of a match

-OrionShots=20,40,60 takes a picture at those seconds and then quits. -OrionShotCam=carrier|stern|deck|wide|chase|side places the camera (left out, it is the player's view with the HUD). -OrionShotPilot=fire+boost+turn flies the player's ship by itself. Run with -game -RenderOffscreen; pictures land in Saved/Screenshots/WindowsEditor. It is compiled out of Shipping builds.

Tested

  • Build: editor target, 0 errors and 0 warnings, after each merge and each fix (8 builds).
  • Headless, editor build, all 143 bots, two matches side by side:
    • Left alone for 270 s: game logic 5.5 to 6.3 ms a frame with 133 to 140 bots flying (5.4 to 5.7 ms before this patch; one 7.3 ms window while a picture run and a build were going on beside it). The player launched 0.8 s into the match. 269 salvo shells and 282 cruise missiles were fired; the six capital ships were down to between 14,243 and 17,337 of 20,000 health. At most 3 bots on one fighter in eight of nine samples, 4 in one.
    • Red cut to 10%: BLUE TEAM WINS, all three Red ships destroyed.
    • Every ship logged "4 of 4 weak point panels fitted to the hull" and "6 of 6 missile cells seated by a trace".
  • Pictures (about 50, from seven camera places, editor build, real renderer, no window):
    • Capital ship plumes before and after: solid pale-blue cones before; soft sheath, core and knots after the three faults above were fixed.
    • Cruise missiles: seen climbing straight up from the deck on a trail in the team's colour, and from the side as arcs between the fleets, for both teams. Hatches with their rims seen on the deck.
    • The player's own view, ship flown by the dev tool: 55 m/s 7.8 g in a full turn at cruise, and 225 m/s 9.0 g LIMIT ASSIST ON BOOST in a full turn on boost.
    • Tracers: in the first picture from the cockpit they could hardly be seen (they showed only when the picture was enlarged four times). They are now 30 cm across at the head with more than twice the glow, light 10 m out, and show as bright streaks closing on the crosshair.
    • Fighter plumes seen from the side on Blue and Red fighters.
  • Packaged build (Builds\2026-11-15_0125_tenfixes): 626 packages cooked (one more than before: the plume material), 0 errors, 0 warnings. Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1 reports RESULT: PASS after 180s, with "Player launch 1, 0.8 s into the match", BLUE TEAM WINS, 0 error lines and no missing game assets. 117 salvo shells and 133 cruise missiles were fired in it. Game logic 3.2 to 3.6 ms a frame with 130 to 137 bot fighters (3.1 ms before this patch).

Not verified

  • Anything in motion. The pictures are stills. How the boost, the build-up to 150 m/s and the g-limit feel with a mouse can only be judged by flying.
  • Shells in flight and their impact blasts, and the plume and trail of fighter rockets: no picture caught one clearly.
  • Bots at the new speeds. Nothing counts bot crashes, so whether bots hit asteroids or hulls more often at 225 m/s with wider turns is not measured. The forced match still ended in a win.
  • Pace. Bots reach the enemy fleet later than before: they let go of boost 1 km from where they are going, and their look-ahead stops them boosting through the asteroid field. The forced match in the packaged build took 180 s where it took 110 s before this patch. Whether the fight now starts too slowly is for a flight to judge.
  • Balance. In the bot-only match the shells and missiles fired could account for at most 80% of the health the capital ships lost (20,825 of 26,130 if every one had hit). Fighters now decide less of a match than before.
  • Drawing cost in the packaged build. The smoke test measures game logic. The picture runs drew at 4.3 to 9.2 ms a frame in the editor build with two other matches running beside them.

Still open

  1. Andrius: fly Builds\2026-11-15_0125_tenfixes. Hold W and watch the speed build to 150; hold Shift for 225; turn at speed and watch the g; fire the gatling; look at a capital ship's stern and deck.
  2. Three numbers to confirm or change, each one constant: the 9 g limit, the 225 m/s boost, the build-up to 150 m/s with W held.
  3. Capital ship weapon damage (25 a shell, 50 a missile): keep, or lower so fighters decide more.
  4. The branch feat/capital-salvos-missiles-plumes-flight holds this patch and the headlights patch before it. Committed, not pushed, no PR yet.
  5. main has the release (PR #36). It lacks PR #37's patch, the headlights and this patch.
  6. No Shipping build has been made yet. This is the next thing to do: it is Sunday morning.
  7. Seen in the pictures and not touched: Blue's capital ships carry red markings; engine trails are flat ribbons and look like wide bands from some angles.

  • Executors: Claude Sonnet 5.5 (claude-sonnet-5-5), medium effort, six runs: JAM-0001 (151k tokens), JAM-0002 (149k), JAM-0003 (103k), JAM-0004 (99k), JAM-0005 (101k), JAM-0006 (122k).

  • About 1.25 million subagent tokens in all. The four executors ran side by side, each in its own compile-only copy of the repository (2 to 6 minutes each); the reviews ran while the pictures were being checked.

Coach tip (topic: checking what you cannot fly)

A log tells you a thing happened; only a picture tells you how it looks. Three faults in this patch (green plume tips, pearl-string diamonds, discs on the nozzles) passed the compiler, the executor's own check and a high-effort code review, and each took one look at one picture to see. Build the tool that takes the picture early: it cost about 150 lines.

Update, 15 Nov 01:23: the first launch of the audio build crashed in the renderer; the second runs (Andy asked to launch it and listen)

  • Andy asked Claude to launch Builds\2026-11-15_0112_audio with sound on, to listen before the merge.
  • First launch: the game quit about 2 s in. The log says Failed to create pipeline state ... error 8007000e (out of memory) while compiling a compute shader pipeline, then Shader compilation failures are Fatal. The audio device had already started without errors.
  • Second launch, a minute later: it runs. The match started and "Player launch 1, 0.8 s into the match" appeared, with no fatal errors and no audio warnings. The only audio note is that Windows converts the engine's 48 kHz to the output device's 44.1 kHz, which is harmless.
  • Likely cause:
    • The first run of a new build compiles every shader pipeline on the spot: 50 compile hitches, none precached.
    • The NVIDIA driver commits a lot of memory while doing that, and this PC's page file is only 2 GB (C: is 98% full). That caps Windows' memory commit limit at 33.7 GB on a 32 GB PC.
    • The second run reused the driver's shader cache from the first.
    • Not proven: nobody has tried a cold start with a build from develop. The crash is in rendering, though, not sound.
  • Risk for the submission: a judge's first launch is a cold start too. The smoke test runs without a renderer (-nullrhi), so it can't catch this.
  • To do (team):
    • Try a cold first launch of the final build on a second PC, which is still listed as not verified.
    • On Andy's PC, free space on C: or set a bigger page file. That's a Windows setting, so it's Andy's call.

AI usage (this update)

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), max effort
  • Used for: launching the build, reading the crash log, the memory check, this update

Coach tip (topic: testing a packaged build)

Judge each new build by its first launch on a PC that has never run it, because that's what a judge gets. A second launch hides cold-start problems: the driver has already cached the shaders, as tonight's crash then clean run showed.

Update, 15 Nov 01:40: sound levels after listening in the packaged game (Andy's decisions)

  • Andy listened to the packaged audio build: "The machine gun is too loud" and "The missiles are too quiet".
  • Measured as each sound's loudest 400 ms:
    • The gun loop was at -9.5 dBFS and plays nonstop, about 8 dB above everything else: the missile launch at -16.3, the laser at -17.7, the engine stages at about -17.5.
    • The missile's peak is its thump, normalized to -1 dBFS, which left the roar at only -21.4.
  • Changes in build_sounds.js:
    • Gun: the firing loop's RMS goes from -10 to -16 dBFS (GUN_CUT.levelDb), and the spin-down moves with it. Both are 6 dB quieter.
    • Missile launch, all four variants: the roar after the thump is lifted 3 dB. A look-ahead limiter then takes 3 dB off the thump's sharpest peaks, so the launch normalizes 3 dB louder.
    • Result for the missile: the roar is up 6 dB (to -15.4), the thump 1.7 dB, and the loudest 400 ms 3 dB (to -13.3). The peak stays at -1 dBFS, and the variants are within 0.7 dB of each other.
    • Before, the gun was 6.8 dB louder than a missile launch. Now a launch is 2.2 dB louder than the gun.
  • Only those six sounds changed. Re-importing re-saves every asset, so the other 18, whose audio is byte-identical, were restored from git.
  • Soundboard round 4: Previous plays the round-3 levels.
  • Packaged build Builds\2026-11-15_0140_levels: 650 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings, all 25 sound assets present. The smoke test reports RESULT: PASS after 125s, with 0 error lines.
  • The launch crash happened again, and not only on the first launch:
    • First launch: a compute pipeline failed 1 s in, out of memory.
    • Second launch: a graphics pipeline failed right after "Player launch 1".
    • Third launch: it runs.
    • Each run compiles more shaders that the driver hasn't cached yet, and sometimes one of them fails.
  • Still open: Andy's verdict on the new levels. The level and devlog commits are local, not yet pushed to PR #38.

Coach tip (topic: distance fades)

Tune a distance fade at three distances you care about: point-blank (50 m), dogfight (300 m) and across the battle (1 km). Write down the dB at each, here -0, -7 and -26. Those three numbers are what you're really choosing. The curve type is only how you get there.

15 Nov QuestionWhat a Shipping build is, and whether it means going to mainAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: nothing is decided yet. Claude had proposed a first Shipping build while PR #39 waited for review, and Andrius asked what that is and whether it means merging to main.

Answer

  • They are two different things. A Shipping build is a kind of game program. main is a place in Git.
  • Shipping build: when Unreal packages the game it is told which configuration to use. Every build so far is "Development": it keeps the console, the test cheat (OrionDamageCarrier), the log file, the 30-second test lines and the dev picture tool. "Shipping" is the configuration meant for players and judges: all of those are left out, and the program is optimised further. Making one changes nothing in Git.
  • main: the branch that holds the released code. Work reaches it by a pull request from develop, which only Andrius approves.
  • How they meet: the build handed in for the jam should be a Shipping build made from the code on main, so the program and the released code match. A Shipping build can be made from any branch at any time before that, as a test.
  • Why make one early: a Shipping build can fail or behave differently where a Development build does not, because code that exists only for testing is removed and checks are compiled out. One difference is already known: the smoke test relies on the cheat and on the log file, and a Shipping build has neither, so a Shipping build has to be checked by playing it.

Still open

  1. Andrius: whether to make a first Shipping build now from develop. It would be a test of the build, not the one to hand in.
  2. PR #39 was merged into develop by Andrius at 01:58 UTC, while this question was being answered. develop now holds everything: the audio, the headlights and the ten fixes.

15 NovThe first Shipping buildAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius ("Make Shipping build", after asking what one is). Claude chose the two extra packaging options below, so the team should review them.

What was done

  • Packaged develop at d7ccf91, which holds everything merged so far: the audio (PR #38), the headlights and the ten fixes (PR #39). The only change to the packaging command is -clientconfig=Shipping.
  • First try (Builds\2026-11-15_0203_shipping): packaged in 1 minute with 0 errors and 0 warnings. It carried a 213 MB debug file that a player has no use for, and nothing to install Microsoft's Visual C++ runtime, which the game needs to start.
  • Second build, the one to play (Builds\2026-11-15_0207_shipping): the same, with two more options, both looked up in the engine's own help (RunUAT BuildCookRun -help):
    • -prereqs puts Microsoft's Visual C++ runtime installer in the package (Engine\Extras\Redist\en-us\vc_redist.x64.exe, 18 MB), so the game can start on a PC that lacks it;
    • -nodebuginfo leaves the debug file out.
  • It is 827 MB in 28 files. The game program is 158 MB, against 332 MB in the Development build.
  • The command, so anyone can repeat it: RunUAT.bat BuildCookRun -project=<the .uproject> -noP4 -platform=Win64 -clientconfig=Shipping -build -cook -stage -pak -prereqs -nodebuginfo -archive -archivedirectory=<folder> -utf8output -unattended -nosplash

Tested

  • Package: 651 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings. The game code compiled in Shipping at the first attempt, with the test cheat, the test log lines and the dev picture tool compiled out.
  • The smoke test cannot run on a Shipping build: it has no log file, no console and no cheat. A new check starts the game twice with no window and judges it from the process itself:
    • game logic only (-nullrhi): still running after 70 s, 325 MB of memory, 76 s of processor time in the last 58 s, no crash report;
    • real renderer with no window (-RenderOffscreen): still running after 70 s, 1,111 MB of memory, no crash report.
  • As expected for Shipping, no log file was written in either run.

Not verified

  • That it plays. These checks show the program starts and keeps running. They do not show a picture, take input, play sound or reach the win screen, and the dev picture tool does not exist in a Shipping build. Only a person playing it can check.
  • A PC without the Visual C++ runtime. This PC has it, so the installer in the package has not been tried.
  • How it opens: full screen or windowed, and at what resolution.
  • The jam's own rules for a submission (size limit, zip or installer, what must be in it) have not been read.

Still open

  1. Andrius: play Builds\2026-11-15_0207_shipping\Windows\orion_81_space_ctf.exe through to a win or a loss.
  2. The first try (Builds\2026-11-15_0203_shipping, 1 GB) is superseded and can be deleted.
  3. The build to hand in should be made the same way from main, after a release pull request from develop, which only Andrius approves. main lacks PRs #37, #38 and #39.
  4. This note and the note on what a Shipping build is are on branch docs/devlog-shipping-build-question, committed and not pushed.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: the two packaging runs, the check script and its runs, this entry

Coach tip (topic: the build you hand in)

Make the build you will hand in look like the one you tested: same configuration, same options, same command. The first Shipping build here differed from a hand-in build in two ways that only showed when its folder was listed, a debug file nobody needs and a missing runtime installer. Write the command down once and reuse it.

15 NovFrigates, four a side, firing from every hard point; missile trails that fade outAndriusZilvinasFable 5.2Opus 5.5Sonnet 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius ("take in the frigate ship type, have four of them from each team roaming the battlefield, it should shoot from all of its hard points, with appropriate weapon types", and "when exploded, the plumes that it generated should dissipate slowly, not immediately, kind of like fade out"). The model is Zilvinas's Fleet_Escort_Frigate.glb (PR #40). Claude chose everything listed under "Choices", so the team should review it.

The model

  • One mesh of 622,568 triangles with three textures. It has no separate parts and no named hard points, so nothing on it can turn: shots leave from the right places, but the barrels do not follow their targets.
  • The weapon mounts were found by rendering the model from six sides in Blender and measuring them: two twin-barrel main turrets, four small point defence turrets, two pads of six missile cells (20 hard points), six engines. There is nothing underneath.
  • Imported with Scripts/setup_model_assets.py as SM_Frigate: Nanite, collision from its own shape, 67 MB of assets in Git LFS.

What was built

  • The frigate (AOrionFrigate), 250 m long, 2,500 health, no shields. It flies a tilted circle of its own and fights with all 20 hard points:
    • Cannons, 4 barrels: shells of 15 damage, one every 0.75 s, at the nearest enemy frigate in reach (3 km), otherwise at an enemy capital ship. They can fire up to 100 degrees off the bow.
    • Point defence, 4 turrets: tracers at enemy fighters within 380 m on that turret's side of the ship.
    • Missiles, 12 cells: one homing missile every 5 s, from the cells in turn, at a random enemy fighter within 1.2 km. It leaves the cell upward and then homes.
  • Four a side. Two fly inside the capital ships' orbits (circles of 880 to 1,165 m, outside the station field) and two outside them (2,050 to 2,350 m). Every circle is 95 m or more from the next, and half the frigates go round each way, so the two sides keep meeting and no two can touch.
  • Loss and return. A destroyed frigate comes back on its path 45 s later with a jump flash. Frigates do not decide the match.
  • Telling the teams apart. The model has the same red markings for both teams, so each frigate has six engine plumes in its team's exhaust colour and four team-coloured lights.
  • Hits. Rounds hit enemy frigates and pass through frigates of their own team. The HUD marks enemy frigates that are on screen.
  • Missile trails fade out. When a cruise missile or a fighter rocket ends, its trail stays where it is, widens to 1.8 times and dims through six steps to nothing: over 4 s for a cruise missile, 1 s for a rocket. Its plume cone shrinks away in 0.3 s.

How the work was split

ItemWhatExecutorReview
JAM-0007The frigate ship class (845 lines)Sonnet 5.5Opus 5.5: no blocker, 4 notes
JAM-0008Trails that fade; rounds that hit frigatesSonnet 5.5Opus 5.5: accepted, 6 notes
main sessionModel renders and measurements, import, interface, spawning and return, HUD marks, picturesFable 5.2by picture and by match

What the reviews found

  • The hard-point formula in the plan mirrored left and right (Opus). The hull is turned half a turn to bring its bow forward, which flips both sideways and lengthways, and the plan's formula flipped only lengthways. Nothing showed, because every mount on this ship has a twin on the other side. Fixed in the code and in the plan.
  • A cannon could go quiet on a target at the edge of its arc, waiting on a barrel that could not reach it while another could (Opus). Fixed: the next barrel tries.
  • Nothing in the trail code. The reviewer's note that four brightness steps might read as flicker on the 1 s rocket fade led to six steps.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. Weapons by mount: cannons for ships, point defence for fighters near the hull, missiles for fighters further out. A "fleet escort" is read as a ship that protects the fleet from fighters.
  2. Size and health: 250 m (a capital ship is 1 km, a fighter 9 m) and 2,500 health (a capital ship has 20,000).
  3. Where they fly: two shells that nothing else uses, so frigates never cross the station field or a capital ship's path.
  4. They come back after 45 s, so each side keeps its four.
  5. Bots do not attack frigates. Enemy frigates and the player do.
  6. Cruise missiles and the capital ships' big guns still go only for capital ships, as Andrius asked earlier.

Tested

  • Build: editor target, 0 errors and 0 warnings, after each merge and fix.
  • Pictures (editor build, real renderer, no window):
    • A Blue frigate from the side and from above: bow forward, six plumes on the six nozzles, team lights, and a muzzle flash sitting on the forward turret's barrels.
    • Missile trails: a finished trail is left behind, wider and dimmer, where it used to vanish.
  • Headless, editor build, all 143 bots and 8 frigates, four matches:
    • Left alone for 180 s on an otherwise idle PC: game logic 5.5 to 6.3 ms a frame with about 138 bots, the same as before the frigates. The frigates fired 6,873 shots.
    • Left alone for 300 s: 5 frigates destroyed and all 5 back 45 s later; 9,432 frigate shots; 23 fighters lost to turrets and frigates. (No frame time from this run: a package was being made beside it.)
    • Red cut to 10%, twice: BLUE TEAM WINS.
    • 0 error lines beyond the engine's own plugin noise in all four.
  • Rebase onto develop at 0b6bb9e (PR #42, Andy's sound follow-up, merged while this was being built): no file in common, no conflict.
  • Packaged Development build (Builds\2026-11-15_0249_frigates): 659 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings. Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1 reports RESULT: PASS after 160s, with BLUE TEAM WINS, "Player launch 1, 0.8 s into the match" and 0 error lines. Game logic 3.5 to 4.0 ms a frame with about 138 bots (4.0 to 4.1 ms before the frigates).
  • Packaged Shipping build (Builds\2026-11-15_0252_frigates_shipping): 0 errors, 0 warnings, 862 MB. The Shipping check: both hidden runs still up after 60 s, no crash report. A first attempt failed at the link step because the test matches had the editor's copy of the game code open; the code was not at fault.

Not verified

  • Fighting a frigate. Nobody has flown at one. How dangerous its missiles and point defence are to the player is unknown.
  • Sound. Every test runs with no sound device. Each cannon shell has a muzzle blast that Andy's code turns into an explosion sound, so a frigate in a gun duel booms four times in three seconds. Whether that is too much is for a listen.
  • Each hard point. The forward cannon's flash was seen on its barrels. The other mounts come from the same measurements but were not each caught in a picture.
  • The fade in motion. The pictures are stills: whether six steps read as a smooth fade is for a flight.
  • Drawing cost. The smoke test measures game logic. Eight frigates of 622,568 triangles each are drawn with Nanite; their cost on screen is not measured.
  • Balance. In the bot-only matches a frigate lasted about two and a half minutes. Whether four a side is too many or too few to fly among is for a flight.
  • A fighter inside a returning frigate (review note): when a frigate comes back, a fighter that happens to be where its hull appears will crash into it. Rare; not handled.

Still open

  1. Andrius: play Builds\2026-11-15_0249_frigates (Development) or Builds\2026-11-15_0252_frigates_shipping (Shipping).
  2. Zilvinas: if the turrets were exported as separate parts, they could turn; and a version of the texture with Blue's markings would let the hull itself show the team.
  3. PR #41 (the Shipping build notes) is in conflict with develop since PR #42. Its four commits are included in this branch, rebased, so one pull request can carry everything. To decide: close PR #41 in favour of that, or push the rebased PR #41 over the old one.
  4. Branch feat/frigates-and-missile-trail-fade: committed, not pushed.
  5. main has the release (PR #36). It lacks PRs #37 to #42 and this branch.

Coach tip (topic: adding a model that came as one piece)

Before writing any code for a new model, render it from six sides with a straight-on camera and write down where things are. A straight-on picture is a ruler: one pixel is a fixed distance. Twenty hard points were measured that way in a few minutes here, and the first picture from the game showed the muzzle flash sitting on the barrels.

Coach tip (topic: two of your own branches at once)

When a second branch is started while the first one's pull request is still open, build the second on top of the first. Two branches that both add to the same file (here the devlog) will conflict with each other the moment one is merged. Stacked, they cannot.

Coach tip (topic: merge or rebase)

Rebase a branch nobody else has seen; merge into one that is already pushed. A rebase rewrites commits, so a pushed branch then needs a forced push and anyone who has it must start over. A merge only adds a commit. The team's rule, rebase before opening a pull request, fits that: before the push, not after.

15 NovFrigate_MK2 with turrets that turn, and a choice of ship at the start: fighter or frigateAndriusZilvinasFable 5.2Opus 5.5Sonnet 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius ("then lets incorporate the frigates_mk2" and "before the game launches, at game start allow the player to choose a ship, either the fighter or the frigate"). Frigate_MK2 is Zilvinas's rework of the frigate (PR #43). Claude chose everything listed under "Choices", so the team should review it.

What was built

  • Frigate_MK2 in the game. The model is imported as 35 parts by a new script, Scripts/setup_frigate_mk2.py. A frigate's two main turrets now turn onto their target and their barrels rise and fall; its four point defence turrets turn; a silo door opens before each missile leaves. Shells leave from the barrels' tips, along the barrels, and only once the turret has turned onto the target.
  • A choice of ship. At the start of a match a screen asks: [1] FIGHTER or [2] FRIGATE. The battle is already running behind it.
    • The fighter launches from the carrier's bay as before.
    • The frigate: the player takes over their team's first frigate. W/S set the speed, A/D turn, SPACE/CTRL lift and lower the nose, Q/E roll, SHIFT gives more speed. The mouse turns the view round the ship, and the crosshair is where the main guns aim; the left button fires them. Point defence and missiles keep firing by themselves.
    • The frigate cannot pass through things, and a hard knock costs health. When it is destroyed, the player waits 45 s for it to come back, still aboard.
  • A HUD for the frigate: a crosshair at the aim point (red where the guns cannot reach), a hull bar, the speed, its own help line, and "FRIGATE LOST" with the seconds until it returns.

How the work was split

ItemWhatExecutorReview
JAM-0009The MK2 rig: parts in place, turrets that turn, silo doorsSonnet 5.5Opus 5.5: accepted, 4 notes
JAM-0010The frigate flown by a player, and driving the rigSonnet 5.5Opus 5.5: accepted with 3 fixes
JAM-0011The ship choice in the game mode, the choice screen, the frigate's HUDSonnet 5.5Opus 5.5: no blocker, 2 fixes
main sessionReading the MK2 file, the import script, the interfaces, merging, fixes from the reviews, pictures, testsFable 5.2by picture and by match

What the reviews found

  • AI frigates could fall silent (Opus). A frigate picked targets in a wide cone, including straight overhead, but the MK2 barrels only lift to 60 degrees. On such a target the turret stopped at its limit and nothing fired, sometimes for minutes, while two frigates passed. Fixed: a target the barrels cannot reach is not picked.
  • The player's aim was traced from the camera, 400 m behind the ship, so anything passing between camera and ship became the aim (Opus). Fixed: it starts above the ship.
  • The view behind the choice screen was set once and then drifted off as the carrier moved (Opus). Fixed: it follows the carrier.
  • A dev picture run would have waited at the choice screen for ever (Opus). Fixed: such a run takes the fighter, unless -OrionShip=choose is given.
  • Turret aim at close range was a degree or two off, enough to stop the guns firing at something under 160 m (Opus). Fixed: the bearing is taken from the turret's own axis.

What the pictures showed

  • The first close picture of the foredeck: both main turrets turned about 90 degrees onto a target off the beam, with the muzzle flash at the barrel tips. The parts sit where they should.
  • The first picture from the flown frigate had the ship half off the bottom of the screen. The camera was lowered and brought a little closer; the whole ship is now in view.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. Keys steer, the mouse aims. A 250 m ship that turns at 14 degrees a second cannot aim by pointing its nose, and its turrets turn by themselves.
  2. The player takes one of the team's four frigates; no fifth is added.
  3. The battle runs behind the choice screen. Nothing waits for the player.
  4. A frigate pilot's carrier is one fighter short (23 bots): the count of bots is fixed before the choice is made.
  5. Speeds for a flown frigate: up to 90 m/s, 140 m/s with SHIFT held (an AI frigate cruises at 45 m/s).
  6. Tests still take the fighter. A run with nobody at the keyboard (-unattended) skips the screen. -OrionShip=fighter, frigate or choose sets it from the command line.

A mistake in the test tooling

  • Scripts/check-shipping.ps1 (written by Claude for the first Shipping build, in develop since PR #44) took "the game" to be the largest process with the game's name, and stopped every process with that name when a run finished.
  • At about 03:50 UTC it was run while Andrius was playing a build. It closed that game, and its first reading was of that game, not of the build under test.
  • Fixed by Claude without asking, in this branch: it is test tooling and the fault was Claude's. The script now keeps the process it started, finds that process's children by parent id, and measures and stops only those. Nothing is chosen or stopped by name.
  • Checked: on the re-run, a game started from Explorer at 03:52:55 UTC ran through both of the script's stop steps and was still running afterwards.
  • The packaged smoke test (Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1) was read as well: it already stops only the process it started.

Tested

  • Editor build: 0 errors, 0 warnings.
  • Matches with no window and nobody at the keyboard, run to the end:
    • player in the fighter: launched at 0.8 s, BLUE TEAM WINS, 0 error lines;
    • player in the frigate (-OrionShip=frigate): BLUE TEAM WINS, 0 error lines.

15 Nov Six featuresTurrets on frigates, weak points on every weapon, a ringed planet, torpedo volleys, two escorts, frigate zoomAndriusFable 5.2Opus 5.5Sonnet 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius asked for the six features ("For when tokens reset, but do push until you run into the 5h window"). How each was read, and every number, is Claude's and is listed under "Choices" for the team to review.

What was built

  1. Capital ship turrets shoot frigates. The six point defence turrets take an enemy frigate in reach when no fighter is in reach.
  2. Weak points on the engines and on every weapon. A carrier now has 18: the bridge, the four engines, the main gun, six point defence turrets, six missile cells. Destroy a weapon's weak point and that weapon is out for the rest of the match; a destroyed engine loses its plume. Within 2 km the HUD marks them, and names the one at the crosshair ("MAIN GUN", "ENGINE 2").
  3. The planet has a ring. One flat sheet with a new material: three broad zones as Saturn has, fine bands, two gaps, and the planet's shadow across the ring.
  4. Frigate torpedoes. The twelve cells launch volleys of four, 0.35 s apart, every 14 s. A torpedo climbs off the deck, turns and flies straight for its target. Volleys take turns between a weak point of the nearest enemy capital ship and the nearest enemy frigate. Every hard point (4 barrels, 4 point defence turrets, 12 cells) counts its shots in the log.
  5. Two escorts for the player. Two of the team's own bots fly either side of the player, slightly behind, on the screen. They attack what the player aims at, only while the player fires, with the weapon the player has selected. They escort a flown frigate too.
  6. Zoom for a flown frigate on the right mouse button (80 to 30 degrees of view).

How the work was split

ItemWhatExecutorReview
JAM-0012Capital ship: turrets on frigates, 18 weak pointsSonnet 5.5Opus 5.5: accepted with 1 fix
JAM-0013Frigate: torpedoes, hard point counts, zoomSonnet 5.5Opus 5.5: accepted with 3 fixes
JAM-0014The two escortsSonnet 5.5Opus 5.5: accepted with 3 fixes and 6 notes
JAM-0016The escort fixes from that review, and a test pilot that attacksSonnet 5.5Main session, by match and picture; no second Opus review
JAM-0015The ring; merging; the capital ship and frigate fixes; testsFable 5.2 (main session)by picture and by match

The usage limit stopped all three executors a few minutes in. They were resumed from where they stopped when it reset at 05:10 UTC; nothing was lost.

What the reviews found

  • Unseen walls (Opus, capital ship). The new weak points are invisible balls up to 54 m across. As built they were solid, so a fighter skimming the deck would have bounced off nothing and taken crash damage. Fixed: shots find them, ships fly through.
  • A torpedo through its own deck (Opus, frigate). It began turning in its first frame, so with the target below the ship it dived back through the hull it had just left. Fixed: it climbs for 0.4 s first.
  • Torpedoes at a dead weak point went for the middle of the ship and hit plain hull (Opus). Fixed: they go for the nearest weak point left.
  • Zoom stuck on for the 45 s a destroyed frigate is away (Opus). Fixed.
  • The escorts (Opus): at the slot first chosen their wings were drawn through the leader's; in a hard turn they had less sideways thrust than the leader's flight assist, so the inner escort would slide through the leader; after a respawn they settled about 1 km behind a fast leader and never caught up. All three fixed in JAM-0016, with six smaller notes.

What the tests showed that the reviews could not

  • Torpedoes nearly always chose frigates. A frigate was in reach most of the time, so weak points were seldom the target. Changed: volleys take turns.
  • Then torpedoes doubled the pace of the match. A hit on a weak point counts three times on the ship's health. Health each capital ship lost a second, over four minutes: 31 before; 63 at torpedo damage 60 (and 14 weak points destroyed); 34 at damage 20 (none destroyed). Settled on 35: 37 a second, so a match runs about a fifth faster than before.
  • Half the frigates' point defence turrets never fired in a four-minute match: no fighter came within their 380 m. Their reach is now 600 m. The turrets on the side away from the fight still stay quiet.
  • The escorts did not fire in a test where the player's fighter turned and fired. Two causes: their guns reach about 230 m and the target was further off, and their noses trailed a turning leader by more than the fire cone. Fixed: the nose leads the turn. A new test pilot (-OrionShotPilot=aim+fire) flies attack runs on a weak point; in that run both escorts were 3 m from their slots and firing.
  • 18 marks a ship on the HUD made a string of brackets along every enemy ship from 3 km away. They now show within 2 km.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. Which turrets shoot frigates: the six point defence turrets only. The main gun still shoots capital ships only, as Andrius ruled for the ten fixes. Their reach is 225 m, so this is mostly felt by a player who flies a frigate up to a carrier. Open question below.
  2. "The new weak points do not change the total": read as balance. Four weak points cost 8 % of the ship's health each, 32 % in all. The 18 still cost 32 %: bridge 8 %, each engine 2 %, each weapon 16/13 %.
  3. Health of a weak point: bridge 1,200 and main gun 1,200 (as before), engine 600, point defence turret 450, missile cell 450.
  4. Torpedoes replace the frigate's homing missiles at fighters. Damage 35 (56 on a hull), 180 m/s, 30 s of life.
  5. Volleys take turns between a weak point and a frigate, weak point first.
  6. The frigate's point defence reaches 600 m (was 380 m).
  7. The escorts come out of the team's own attackers, so a team stays 24.
  8. The escorts attack only while the player fires (within the last 3 s) and never leave their slots to chase.
  9. The ring's outer edge is 16 km from the planet's middle, about 7 km short of the battlefield, so nobody can fly through the sheet.

  • Final code, two matches with no window: a four-minute match with 0 error lines and 6.7 ms average frame; a match with Red weakened that ran to BLUE TEAM WINS with 0 error lines.
  • Weak points: every ship logs "18 weak points ... destroying all costs 32.0% of health". In the match at torpedo damage 60, 14 were destroyed and each logged its weapon as disabled. After the change that lets ships fly through them, hits still land.
  • Torpedoes: the log shows all twelve cells firing in turn (cells 7/7/7/7/6/6/6/6/6/6/6/6); one picture shows a volley of four leaving a frigate.
  • Escorts: flying straight, both 0 m from their slots within a minute, with a picture from the player's seat; on an attack run, both 3 m from their slots and firing; with the player in a frigate, both 0 m from their slots beside it.
  • The ring: two pictures.

  • Four pictures are kept in D:\Games\2026 Epic Jam\Builds\pictures_2026-11-15_sixfeatures.

15 Nov AudioExplosions in three sizes, built from three recordingsAndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy. The sound so far is approved ("were happy with the sound so far"). For the explosions, use three picks from the sound sources page: Deep Explosion, Explosion Large and Propane Explosion Designed. Asked how, Andy chose: "use the three sounds as context to synthesize new sounds that will simulate explosions of various sizes and intensity, with as much variety as reasonably possible", for example three small, three medium and three large. And yes to a bigger capital ship death built from them.

Licences, re-read on each sound's page today (all "Creative Commons 0", linking to CC0 1.0)

  • Deep Explosion (Kodack): recorded on a Sony HDR-PJ260V, edited in Audacity and FL Studio. The shortlist had flagged a comment asking "Is This Really CC0?". Read in order, Kodack answered it at the time: "yes it is :) all sounds are made by me".
  • Explosion large (SamsterBirdies): a firecracker set off in a garage, recorded on a Tascam DR-100mkIII, then pitched down and layered.
  • Propane Explosion Designed (modusmogulus): layers recorded on a Zoom H4n Pro.
  • Claude's browser isn't signed in to Freesound, so the build uses Freesound's public high-quality previews until the originals are downloaded.

What the recordings are (measured)

  • Deep Explosion: 2 s of rolling sub-bass with no sharp hit, 97-99% of it below 80 Hz.
  • Explosion large: a classic blast. It hits at 0.03 s, is down 40 dB by 1.9 s, and is strongest from 80 Hz to 1 kHz.
  • Propane: an instant "whumpf", 10 dB down within 30 ms, with a tail that sinks into the sub-bass.
  • None of them has much crack.

How they're built (blast() in build_sounds.js)

  • Each explosion layers:
    • a synthesized crack, plus Explosion large's attack pitched up;
    • a body led by one of the recordings;
    • Deep Explosion's sub-bass, under medium and large blasts only;
    • a tail from another recording, stretched in time for large blasts;
    • debris crackle.
  • Sizes:
    • Small: 1.4 s, pitched up (1.35-1.6x), the brightest.
    • Medium: 2.4 s.
    • Large: 4.2 s, pitched down (0.68-0.82x).
  • Three per size:
    • Soft: led by Explosion large.
    • Medium: led by Propane, with Deep Explosion's rumble as the tail.
    • Hard: both blasts together, with the sharpest crack and the most saturation.
  • Each variant draws its own pitch, timing and mix. Within each size, the variants' waveforms correlate 0.04-0.22, where 1 would mean identical.
  • Loudness rises with size and intensity, measured roughly the way LUFS weighs it.
    • A first pass measured loudness unweighted. That let Deep Explosion, pitched down to 20-55 Hz, dominate the hard variants, and laptop speakers can't play that.
    • Fixed: Deep Explosion is never pitched below 0.95x, everything below 35 Hz is cut, and "hard" now means more crack, not more sub-bass.
  • The capital ship's death: the three recordings stacked and pitched down (0.6x; Deep Explosion only to 0.85x), two large blasts at 1.4 s and 2.6 s, then the rumble and groan from before.

In the game (OrionProjectileManager)

  • Blasts under 12 m (missile hits, launch flashes, cannon fire) play a small explosion.
  • Under 35 m (torpedoes, fighters, salvos, shells) play a medium one.
  • From there up to a capital ship's death (weak points, frigates, cruise missiles) play a large one.
  • Each plays a random variant, never the same one twice in a row, and the pitch changes by only about 6% across a band.
  • All nine fade with distance. The capital ship's death plays to everyone.

Checked

  • 35 of 35 sounds imported. The editor build found all ten explosion sounds, and the assets read back with the distance fade and the voice cap.
  • Packaged build Builds\2026-11-15_1210_explosions: 712 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings, all 10 explosions in.
  • The smoke test reports RESULT: PASS after 95s, with 0 error lines. It ran first, then the build was launched for Andy to listen. It got through on the first launch.

Also

  • A teammate had numbered the sounds on the published soundboard (#01, #02 and so on). That change was only on the page, so Claude added it to the template so that rebuilds keep it. The soundboard is at version 11.

Still open

  • Andy's verdict on the explosions.
  • Pushing feat/audio-explosions and opening its pull request.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), max effort
  • Used for: the licence checks, fetching the previews, measuring the recordings, the explosion generator and its checks, the C++ banding, the import, build, package and smoke test, merging the soundboard numbering, this entry

Coach tip (topic: loudness)

Balance sounds with a meter weighted the way ears hear, not raw RMS. A raw meter counts 30 Hz rumble as loud, but laptop speakers and many headsets barely play it. So a sub-heavy explosion set to the "right" level can vanish for half your players.

Update, 15 Nov 12:32: the capital ship hum is in game (Andy's decision)

  • Andy is happy with the explosions ("ok thats great thanks") and asked for the capital ship proximity hum next, the "dull quiet humming sound when in proximity" from his first audio request.
  • The sound: the 45 Hz hum made in round 1, which nothing played yet. Measured, 82% of it was below 60 Hz, which laptop speakers can't play. It keeps its pitch, with harmonics up to 315 Hz added, so now about a quarter of it is above 100 Hz and the ear fills in the missing fundamental. It's still dull, with 1% above 400 Hz, and it's a quiet bed, about 6 dB under your engine at the hull.
  • In game:
    • Each carrier plays it from its middle. It fades in over 2.5 s when the carrier spawns, at a random point in its 8 s loop so carriers don't hum in step, and fades out over 3 s when the carrier explodes.
    • ATT_Capital_Hum: full volume inside a box around the 1 km hull (1.1 km x 360 m x 360 m), so it's as loud at the bow as at the middle. Then it falls evenly in dB to -30 dB 700 m out: about -9 dB at 200 m and -17 dB at 400 m.
    • It isn't panned. The sound sits at the ship's middle, 500 m from its bow, so panning would point the wrong way. A low hum is felt rather than placed anyway.
    • SCON_Hums keeps the nearest 2 carriers.
    • Frigates don't hum. Andy asked for capital ships only.
  • Checked:
    • The settings read back from the saved assets, and the editor build found the sound.
    • The hum, its attenuation and its voice cap are in the package. Before, the cooker left the hum out because nothing played it.
    • Packaged build Builds\2026-11-15_1229_hum: 715 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings. The smoke test reports RESULT: PASS after 105s, with 0 error lines. It ran first, then the build was launched for Andy.
  • Soundboard version 12: the hum card has the new version, and Previous plays the old one.
  • Still open: Andy's verdict on the hum, and pushing feat/audio-explosions (explosions plus hum) and opening its pull request.

15 Nov Andrius's play notesESC menu and settings, HUD bars and hit marks, a 10 s launch through opening doors, shields, frigate weak points, engines that slow a ship, plumes that do not add upAndriusFable 5.2Opus 5.5Sonnet 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius played Builds\2026-11-15_0549_sixfeatures_shipping from 11:32 UTC and sent his notes as one message, four times between 11:45 and 12:25 UTC, each time with more added. He asked for every item below. How each was read, and every number, is Claude's and is listed under "Choices" for the team to review.

His notes, and what was built for each

  1. "When you are in the selection menu ... you wouldn't be able to use the mouse to move the camera." The game mode set the view back on the bays every frame, but the mouse turned it in between, which showed as a wobble. Until a ship is chosen the mouse and the movement keys now do nothing.
  2. An acceleration bar, bottom right. For the fighter and for a flown frigate: the ship's acceleration in m/s2 and in g, against the most its engines give, with a tick at full thrust without boost.
  3. Enemy weak points: a health bar once damaged, a hit mark when a shot lands. A damaged weak point shows a small bar. For 0.25 s after the player's own shot takes health from a weak point, a white X shows on it and at the crosshair. A hit on plain hull shows nothing: the mark means "that was a weak point".
  4. Capital ship plumes half as long. One number scales every layer, the shock diamonds and their spacing.
  5. Plumes that overlap do not add up. The plume material was a glow added on top of what is behind it, so four plumes in a row were four times as bright. It is now a surface with holes (a stipple that changes every frame): where plumes overlap, the screen shows the densest one and never the sum.
  6. "The ships appear too dark", and a menu on ESC. Two new fill lights and a stronger one opposite the sun; the base exposure up by 0.4 stops. The ESC menu pauses the game: Resume, Difficulty (Easy, Normal, Hard), Settings (Controls: a list of every key; Graphics: quality Low to Epic, Brightness and Contrast sliders, Reset), Quit game. Keyboard, gamepad and mouse. Settings are saved.
  7. A 10 s straight launch, from deeper in the ship, with the doors. A fighter now appears 82 m inside the hangar (it was 18 m) behind closed bay doors. The doors open over 2 s, it passes them at 6 s and the pilot has the controls at 10 s. Bots launch the same way.
  8. Destroyed engines slow the ship. Each of a capital ship's four engines gives a quarter of its speed along its path; with none left it stops.
  9. Shields on the frigates and the capital ships; "the same weak point mentality" on frigates. One shield per ship takes every hit first. A frigate has ten weak points (two main turrets, four point defence turrets, two missile banks, two engine groups); destroying one stops that system.
  10. "Fix the capital ship animation, now it elongates." The ship was stretched to ten times its length while jumping in. It now keeps its own shape.

What the reviews found

  • The next fighter appeared inside the pilot's view (Opus, launch). Fighters left a bay 2 s apart, which put the follower 16 m behind the leader, where the leader's chase camera is. Now 3 s (27 m).
  • Ten seconds without the controls and without protection (Opus, launch). From the doors to the end of the run a fighter flew locked in the open, where enemy bombers attack the engines: a player could die before ever having the stick. A launching fighter now takes no damage, and no bot or turret picks it as a target.
  • A stopped ship is run into by the ship behind it (Opus, launch). A team's ships keep their spacing only because they travel at the same rate. A ship now holds 30 degrees of the circle behind a slower ship ahead of it.
  • That rule never fired as first built (Opus, shields). Claude's brief said "same radius"; each ship flies a circle of its own radius. The planner's mistake, not the executor's. Fixed.
  • The X at the crosshair missed a flown frigate's distant hits (Opus, HUD), the first click after Alt+Tab was swallowed once the menu had been used (Opus, menu), a destroyed frigate's panel kept its old shield value and point defence still aimed at launching fighters (Opus, shields). All fixed.

What the tests showed that the reviews could not

  • The plume material took three tries, by picture. As a solid surface the plume was a flat paper cone with its white-hot core hidden behind the outer layer. As a thin stipple it was a few bright dots. Kept: a denser stipple, where the outer layer covers about half its pixels and the core shows through the holes. It reads as a soft haze with a bright core. It is fainter and grainier than the old glow.
  • With a shield up, a fighter firing at a weak point sees nothing happen. The marks on a shielded ship are now in the shield's blue and the name reads "SHIELD UP".
  • The first test of "the ship behind holds back" could not show it. A ship that jumps in later starts where it would have got to by then, which is ahead of a ship that stopped at the start. The test cheat got a delay. Then: Red's first ship stopped 75 s in, the ship behind it went from 38 m/s to 2 and then 0, the ship ahead kept its 34 m/s.
  • Shields change the shape of a match, not whether it ends. For the first two to three minutes the shields soak everything and no ship loses health. Once they are down the fleets' fire keeps them down, and ships then lost health faster than before (about 60 a second for each ship over a minute, against 37 over a whole match before).

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. The menu pauses the game.
  2. Difficulty scales only the player's own ship: damage it takes x0.5, x1, x1.6; damage of its shots x1.4, x1, x0.8. Bots and capital ships are untouched.
  3. Controls is a list to read. No key rebinding today.
  4. A fourth menu entry, Quit game, with a second press to confirm. A packaged game had no other way out than Alt+F4.
  5. "Bring in the door animations" was read as: the pilot sees the bay doors open. The doors are the four leaves built in code; the model's own leaf meshes in Zilvinas's carrier source are not imported.
  6. The 10 s launch is for bots too, so nobody overtakes anybody in the tunnel. A death now costs about 16 s out of the fight.
  7. A launching fighter cannot be hurt or targeted.
  8. A shield is one pool per ship, over hull and weak points alike, with no spill-over. Capital ship 3,000 (15 % of its health), back after 12 s without a hit at 100 a second. Frigate 500 (20 %), back after 8 s at 50 a second.
  9. Frigate weak points: health 300 (main turret), 150 (point defence), 250 (missile bank, engine group); together they cost 32 % of the frigate's health, as the carrier's do. Bots and torpedoes still aim at the middle of a frigate.
  10. Engines: a quarter of the speed each, eased at 5 % of full speed a second. On a frigate, half for each of the two groups.
  11. Lighting: fill opposite the sun from 8 % to 28 % of the sun, two side fills at 18 %, exposure +0.4 stops.
  12. The plume look (see above): it answers "do not add up" at the cost of a softer, grainier plume.

Coach tip (topic: notes that keep arriving while the work runs)

Freeze a wave, do not stretch it. The notes came in four parts over forty minutes. The first three executors were already running when parts three and four arrived; the additions went to whoever owned the file (one section added to a running item) or into a second wave built on the merged first one. What was never done: hand a new note to an executor whose files another executor also touches. Every note found a single owner, so five executors and four reviews produced no merge conflict. The cost of the growing list is elsewhere: ten things changed between two packages, and no person has played any of them, so the next play test is the real review.

Coach tip (topic: the build you hand in)

Fetch before you package, not before you push. A package is a picture of one commit; if a teammate merged an hour ago, the build you are proud of is already missing their work, and nothing in the build tells you so. Two minutes of git fetch and a rebase before packaging cost less than explaining on hand-in day why the explosions are silent.

15 Nov Version numbersEvery build from v0.0.1 to v0.14.0 tagged; the play notes sent for review to develop and as a release to mainAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius ("Have a look at semantic versioning ... tag the very first build as v0.0.1 and assign either 0.x.y numbers to each build version based on how big or small the update was. Tag this build as the next logical build version and open the PR without my feedback ... also make a PR for this build to main too."). The rule for the numbers and each number are Claude's, for the team to review. Only Andrius approves the pull request into main.

The rule (from Semantic Versioning 2.0.0, semver.org)

  • A version is MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH. MAJOR stays 0: in that scheme 0 means "still in development, anything may change".
  • MINOR goes up when a build adds something a player can newly do, see or hear. PATCH goes up when a build only fixes or retunes what was there.
  • A Development and a Shipping package of the same code share one number.
  • Each number is a Git tag on the commit in develop that first holds that update, and every tag contains all the versions before it.
  • The list, with the reason for each number and the build folders that belong to it, is the new file CHANGELOG.md at the top of the repository.

The numbers

VersionUpdateKind
v0.0.1The first packaged build (14 Nov 11:47 UTC)first
v0.1.0Derelict station, ship models, lead reticleMINOR
v0.1.1Fix: start-up error in packagesPATCH

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. MINOR or PATCH by what a player gets, not by how many lines changed. A 1 km carrier touched many numbers and is a PATCH (the same game, resized); zoom on the right mouse button was a few lines and makes a MINOR.
  2. Sound that improves what was there is a PATCH (v0.13.1); the first sound at all is a MINOR (v0.8.0).
  3. Tags sit on develop's own history, not on commits that a rebase replaced. Three builds were packaged from a branch before it was rebased onto Andy's sounds (0028_headlights, 0125_tenfixes, 1256_playnotes); their tags hold those sounds too, and CHANGELOG.md says so beside each.
  4. v0.8.0 and v0.13.1 have no build folder on Andrius's PC: they are Andy's sound merges, built on his.
  5. One folder is matched by its time only: 2026-11-14_1852_develop is not named in the devlog; it was packaged two minutes after pull request #28 was merged.
  6. Art files that the game does not use yet (the .glb uploads) got no number.
  7. The release to main goes from its own branch, release/v0.14.0, at the same commit as the play notes branch, so that merging the pull request into develop cannot close the one into main.

What is sent for review

  • To develop: the branch feat/pause-menu-hud-bars-plumes: the play notes (two entries above), CHANGELOG.md and this entry.
  • To main: release/v0.14.0, the same commit. For main it brings v0.12.0, v0.13.0, v0.13.1 and v0.14.0 on top of v0.11.0.
  • The builds that match: Builds\2026-11-15_1311_playnotes (Development, smoke test PASS after 246s) and Builds\2026-11-15_1311_playnotes_shipping (Shipping, both hidden runs up with no crash report).

Not verified

  • Nobody has played v0.14.0. Andrius is playing it while these pull requests are open. What the play notes entry lists as not verified still stands.
  • The game does not show its own version. The number is in Git and in CHANGELOG.md only; the packages of 13:11 UTC carry no version text.

15 Nov QuestionCan Claude make explosion textures with PBRGen?AndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy: drop the PBRGen idea and improve the existing explosions in code instead (see the next entry).

Question

Can Claude generate explosion textures using PBRGen?

Answer

  • Claude has no PBRGen connection in this session. The Unreal MCP server is also down (connection refused), so Claude can't make or import assets in the editor right now.
  • PBR generators make surface maps (base colour, normal, roughness, metallic) for hulls and rocks. An explosion is light, not a surface. In Unreal it's an emissive, translucent flipbook (SubUV) or a Niagara system, so PBR maps are the wrong kind of texture for it.
  • Our explosions today are drawn in code: AOrionProjectileManager::SpawnBlast scales glowing instanced spheres (OrionProjectileManager.cpp:468). They use no textures and cost nothing to cook.
  • If we want texture-based fire, Claude can write a script that renders an animated noise fireball into a flipbook PNG (for example 8×8 frames, 2048²) for an emissive SubUV material. It's an original procedural texture with no licence questions.

Coach note

It's Sunday, and packaging was due to start this morning. New VFX textures add cook risk and LFS assets, and the gameplay loop doesn't need them. Ship the sphere blasts and only try a flipbook after a packaged build passes the smoke test.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: low
  • Used for: checking the repo for PBRGen and how explosions are drawn, answering, writing this note
15 Nov ExplosionsLumpy fireballs that cool, embers, and 3D shockwavesAndyFable 5.2Opus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy ("they are too spherical and the emissive is too uniform, could 3D shockwaves be added too?"). Andy also decided that packaging can wait until after this. Claude chose the look and all the numbers below, so the team should check them in game.

Question

Can we make the existing explosions more realistic: less spherical, uneven glow, and add 3D shockwaves?

What changed (branch feat/explosion-visuals, not committed yet)

  • New material M_Explosion, built by Scripts/setup_explosion_material.py, the same way as the plume material. It's additive and unlit, and reads four per-instance values: heat, fade, noise seed and lump size.
    • 3D noise that drifts over time breaks the glow into hot and cool patches, so the fire looks like it's boiling.
    • Each lobe is hotter in the middle than at its edge.
    • Colour goes from white-hot to orange to dull red as the lobe cools, and brightness drops faster than the colour.
    • World position offset pushes the surface in and out, so lobes are lumpy, not balls.
  • Each explosion is now built from parts (AOrionProjectileManager::AddExplosionVisual):
    • a white flash in the first eighth of its life;
    • 5 lobes (8 for blasts of 15 m and up), each squashed, turned and pushed outward its own way, starting a little apart in time;
    • 7 embers (14 at 40 m and up) that streak out, slow down and cool;
    • a thin blue-white shockwave shell, plus a flat ring at a random tilt for blasts of 18 m and up. Only their outlines glow.
  • Muzzle flashes (capital ship salvos and missile cells, frigate cannons and torpedo tubes) are a new kind, Muzzle. They get a short three-lobe fireball with no embers or shockwave. They still play explosion sounds, as before.
  • Explosions last a little longer, 0.35 s + 0.035 s per metre (was 0.25 s + 0.03 s), so you can see them cool. A capital ship's 120 m death blast lasts about 4.5 s. Nothing in gameplay reads this time.
  • If M_Explosion fails to load, explosions fall back to the old single glowing sphere.
  • New dev camera, -OrionShotCam=blast: it sets off a 120, 60, 20 and 9 m explosion and a 25 m muzzle flash in open space, one every 4 s from 10 s. Picture times can now have tenths (-OrionShots=10.3), and the picture name gets a _3 suffix.

Checked

  • The editor build compiles.
  • Two picture runs, 13 pictures each, with no crash. The first run showed milky bubbles: the shockwave was too wide and too bright. After tuning, the shell is a thin outline and the ring a halo, and the cooling fireball is ragged and mottled orange-brown.
  • M_Explosion.uasset shows filter: lfs.

Not checked yet

  • A packaged build, a cook manifest that includes M_Explosion, and the smoke test. /Game/Space is always cooked, so it should be in.
  • Frame rate during a capital ship's death, which sets off many big blasts at once: each blast is about 20 additive spheres now, against 1 before.
  • A person watching it in motion.
  • In the editor, the first explosion hitched for about 7 s while its shaders compiled. A packaged build compiles shaders ahead of time.

Coach tip (topic: VFX on a jam deadline)

Tune effects with a repeatable camera like -OrionShotCam=blast, not by flying around and waiting for a fight. Two picture runs of 45 s each were enough to see that the shockwave was wrong and to fix it.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app
  • Effort: low
  • Used for: the material script, the C++ changes, the test camera, reading the pictures, this note
