15 Nov Engine plumesThe glowing ball behind each fighter engineAndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy (what it should look like); Claude (the sizes and the brightness curve)

Question

What are the spherical objects behind the fighters' engines? Close the gap so each one sits half inside the engine cone, and make it fade at low throttle and brighten at high throttle.

Finding

  • They are the exhaust glows ExhaustR and ExhaustL in AOrionSpaceFighterPawn. They are not part of the ship model. Each one was a flat disc 60 cm across, placed for the old box-built ship at x = -362. It used the opaque surface material at glow 15 (idle) to 120 (full), which is far past white, so it bloomed into a ball and barely changed with the throttle.
  • On all four Meshy models (measured from the GLB vertices), the nozzle exit is at about x = -294, so the disc floated about 68 cm behind the engine. The plume cones and shock diamonds start at the disc, so they had the same gap.

Decision

  • The glow is now a ball 45 cm across, as wide as the nozzle's bore. It is centred on each model's measured nozzle exit, so its front half sits inside the engine. There is a table of nozzle positions per model, next to the muzzle table.
  • It uses the plume material: a soft outline, no near-camera dimming, and it fades to nothing. Brightness is 40 × (glow level / max)²: almost nothing at idle, about a quarter at half throttle, solid at full throttle, and up to 90 on boost.
  • The plumes, diamonds and engine lights moved forward with the glow. The trail origin stays where it was.

Still open

  • Not checked by eye in the game yet (the Unreal MCP server was down). The editor build compiles.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), low effort
  • Used for: finding the object, measuring the nozzles from the GLBs, the code change, this note

Update, 15 Nov: the nozzle glow passed Andy's look in PIE; engine trails no longer cut through the ship (Andy's request)

  • Andy checked the exhaust balls in PIE and they looked right.
  • The problem: an engine trail is a ribbon from the engine through 10 samples of where it was over the last 0.6 s. When a ship strafes or flies backwards, those samples are beside it or ahead of it, so the ribbon ran through the hull.
  • The fix, in GetEngineTrail: the trail now goes 1.5 m straight back from each nozzle, which clears the fins (they reach 76 cm behind the nozzles). After that it bends toward where the engine has been, and the bend eases in over the next 1.5 m. A sample that isn't far enough behind is pushed back. Each older sample is at least 15 cm further back than the one before, so the trail never folds over itself. In normal forward flight it looks the same as before.
  • The editor build compiles. Not yet checked in PIE.

AI usage (this update)

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), low effort
  • Used for: the trail change, this update

Update, 15 Nov: trails sweep in a curve when strafing (Andy: "close", but the turn should be a sweeping curve, not 90 degrees)

  • Andy liked how far behind the ship the trail starts to turn. In a hard strafe, though, it turned almost 90 degrees.
  • Change: each older trail point now goes back at least as far as it moves sideways (TrailSweep = 1). That caps the turn at 45 degrees off straight back, so a strafe sweeps the trail away in a curve. The straight 1.5 m and the 1.5 m ease-in are unchanged.
  • Compiles (game target). Andy will load it into the open editor with Live Coding and check it in PIE.

AI usage (this update)

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), low effort
  • Used for: the sweep change, this update

Update, 15 Nov: the trail curves out of the nozzle on a 1.5 m radius (Andy's decision: "close to what I'm looking for", replace the straight section with a 1.5 m-radius curve)

  • Andy tested the straight-stem version in PIE. It was close, but he wanted a curve with a 1.5 m radius instead of the straight 1.5 m section. The curve starts at the trail's origin and turns toward the direction of the trail.
  • How it works now: the trail leaves each nozzle heading straight back. It follows a circle of radius 1.5 m, turning toward the trail, until it points at the place 6 m along the trail (Claude's choice: that keeps the join smooth). Then it continues straight to that point and follows the rest of the trail. The curve starts where the trail leaves the nozzle. The hidden piece inside the engine stays straight, so the curve can't poke out through the engine casing.
  • The trail behind the curve is still pushed back behind the ship. Every sample is kept at least 1.65 m behind the nozzles, plus 2.5 m for each second of its age. This now goes by each sample's age instead of its place in the list, so the near part of the trail no longer jumps back 15 cm every 0.06 s.
  • The trail's width now also goes by age: full at the nozzle, nothing at the age a sample is dropped. Before, it went by point count, and the extra points on the curve would have made the trail thin out within its first 2 m. The trail's end no longer pops either.
  • Claude prototyped the shape in Python first. It drew six flight cases over the blue fighter's real outline from its GLB: forward, strafe, reverse, forward with strafe, a hard turn, and reverse with strafe. In every case the trail stays behind the nozzles, and the curve held the same angle across three sampling phases, so it should not flicker. Near the nozzles the curve stays within 2 cm of the engine axis, and the fins only reach about 24 cm behind the nozzles at nozzle height.
  • The game target compiles with no errors or warnings. Not yet checked in PIE.

AI usage (this update)

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), max effort
  • Used for: the curve geometry, the Python prototype and pictures, the C++ change, this update
15 Nov TrailsNo more bright squares where pieces meet, and hexagonal instead of squareAndyOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Decided by: Andy (the problem, and hexagonal trails); Claude (the fix: open tubes with no end faces)

Question

On every trail, engine and missile alike, the brightness doubles where two pieces of trail meet, which makes bright square spots. Andy proposed making one of the two overlapping pieces fully transparent, and asked for the trails to be hexagonal prisms instead of square ones.

Finding

  • Every trail is a chain of the engine's Cube, one box per stretch between two points, drawn with M_EngineTrail. That material is additive, unlit and one-sided.
  • A box chain doesn't overlap along a straight run: each box ends exactly where the next one starts. But each box had end faces. A line of sight through a joint passes through the next box's end face, which counts as a second front face, so the glow doubled over the end face's shape: a square at every joint. Looking along a trail, as the chase camera does, sight lines cross many joints, so the squares stood out.

Decision

  • Every trail (fighter engine trails, rocket, cruise-missile and torpedo trails, and the fading ghost trails) is now a chain of open hexagonal tubes with no end faces. A line of sight through a straight run of pieces now crosses exactly one front face, so one of the two overlapping pieces is effectively fully transparent at every joint, as Andy proposed.
  • The tube is 100 units long and 100 across its flats, with its corners pointing up and down, so from above (the chase camera's view) it is exactly as wide as the old box. It is built in code when the game starts, using Unreal's fast mesh build, which also works in a packaged game. That means there is no new .uasset to commit through LFS. If the build ever fails, trails fall back to the boxes and the log says so.
  • M_EngineTrail is unchanged, so trail brightness and the near-camera fade are as before.

Still open

  • At a sharp bend, two neighbouring pieces still overlap in a thin wedge on the inside of the bend. That's about 6 cm deep on a fighter trail at a 30° bend, and most joints bend much less. Two different trails crossing each other still add up, like two glows.
  • Looking straight into the open front end of a trail that has no missile in front of it (a fading ghost trail) shows it hollow.
  • The game target compiles. Not yet run in the editor or checked by eye.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), max effort
  • Used for: finding the cause, the tube mesh and its winding (checked against the engine's own box generator), the C++ change, this note

  • Log: one engine ensure at frame 4, GetStaticMaterials()[MaterialIndex].UVChannelData.bInitialized. It comes from the trail tube built at run time, whose material slot never had its UV data marked as set up. Texture streaming asks every mesh for it. All 26 error lines are that ensure's call stack, and the game code logged no warnings. Ensures don't stop the game. In a Development build it costs a one-time hitch and leaves a crash-report folder in the build's Saved\Crashes on each launch. Shipping compiles ensures out, which is why the Shipping check passed. Fix (not made yet): mark the slot's UV data with FMeshUVChannelInfo(1.f). That would be v0.15.2.
  • v0.15.1 is now a candidate for KNOWN-GOOD. That is Andy's call.

15 NovA playbook for other Claude sessions, and model and effort recorded on every version's pull requestAndriusFable 5.2

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius ("can you make an organizational artefact that guides other claude users llm sessions how to do it. Record which model and which effort level was used for each version iteration in the pull request descriptions."). What goes into the playbook, and which pull requests got the record, are Claude's choices, for the team to review.

What was made

  1. The LLM Build Playbook, a Claude Doc: https://claude.ai/artifact/2ayfYeJLf5YenN7Nuf5moo . It is written for a Claude session that is about to work on the game, and for the person directing it. Eleven sections:
    • how to use it, and the four things a session does before any change;
    • the path of a change, as a drawing of twelve steps with one loop (nothing is tagged until it is reviewed, tested and packaged);
    • where things live: the repository, the build folders, the tracker, the working copies, the tags, the devlog page;
    • models and effort: main session, executors (Sonnet 5.5, medium), reviewers (Opus 5.5, high), and where that is recorded;
    • splitting work between agents: interfaces first, one owner per file, compile-only copies, briefs in the tracker;
    • building and testing with no person: the build command, matches with no window, the picture tool's switches, the test cheats;
    • packaging: the two commands, the folder name, the two checks;
    • version numbers: the rule, the one setting, the list, the tag;
    • Git and pull requests, with what a description must hold;
    • the devlog: the parts of an entry;
    • twelve hard rules, each with the mistake behind it.
  2. Model and effort on the pull requests. The pull request that brought each version into develop now ends with a section "Version, models and effort": a table of version, work, model and effort, taken from the "AI usage" lines of this devlog. Fifteen merged pull requests got it (#10, #18, #21, #24, #26, #28, #33, #35, #37, #38, #39, #44, #46, #47, #48). #50 has the table for v0.14.0 and v0.14.1, and the release #51 has one row for every version from v0.0.1 to v0.14.1.
  3. CHANGELOG.md: v0.12.0 to v0.14.1 are marked as on main since pull request #51, and the steps for the next build say that the pull request states model and effort.

What the record shows

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. A Claude Doc, not a page. The app offers a document type for guides that people keep editing; the team can change the playbook in place and comment on it.
  2. The record went onto merged pull requests too, as an appended, dated section that says who added it. Nothing in any description was changed or removed. Three of the fifteen are Andy's pull requests (#28, #38, #48); their figures are the ones his sessions wrote into this devlog.
  3. The pull request that gets a version's record is the one that brought it into develop, not a release into main. PR #33 therefore carries five versions.
  4. "Session default" is written where the effort was never changed. The devlog does not say which level that default was, so none was guessed.
  5. No machine paths in the playbook: placeholders stand for the repository, the engine and the folders beside it.

Tested

  • The playbook's drawing was looked at as the page draws it: twelve boxes, no text over a border.
  • Every edited description was read back: it starts with its old text and holds the new section (15 of 15).
  • #50 and #51 hold their tables. Both had been merged by Andrius at 13:49 UTC, between two of Claude's steps; a merged pull request's description can still be edited.

Not verified

  • No other Claude session has used the playbook yet. Whether a session that reads it works the way it describes is untested.
  • The playbook is private until it is shared. Only its owner can open the link; Andy, Zilvinas and their sessions cannot until Andrius shares it from the page's Share menu.
  • Model and effort for the older versions are as the devlog recorded them at the time. They were not checked against anything else.

  3. A short CLAUDE.md in the repository that points at the playbook would make Claude Code sessions load the rules by themselves. Not done; for Andrius to decide.

Coach tip (topic: handing a way of working to someone else)

Write the rule and the mistake that made it, side by side. "Never stop a process by name" reads as fuss until the next line says a script once closed the build its owner was playing. A session that knows why a rule exists can tell when a new situation is the same mistake in another form; a session that only has the rule cannot.

15 Nov v0.16.0The O3 prompts of the v0.15.0 play testAndriusOpus 5.5Sonnet 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius ("Take prompts for O3 and update the game. Tag it as v 0.16.0. Feel free to fan out, use opus 5.5 to give tasks and review code work and sonnet 5.5 to write the code necessary."). The prompts come from the play test list v0.15.0_prompt-assignments.csv, where each prompt is assigned to one of three orchestrators; this build does the nine O3 rows. How each prompt is met (the planner decisions below) is Claude's reading, for the team to review.

What changed for a player

  • A-3, A-6 · weak point marks. A weak point shows nothing until your own shot hits it. Then its bracket, health bar and name show for 10 s after your last hit on it, fading over the last 2 s. A hit that the ship's shield stops still marks the weak point, in the shield colour with "SHIELD UP". The cause of A-6 ("one capital ship right, the others not") was the shield: a shielded ship threw a weak point hit away before it was recorded, so it gave no mark and no bar, while a ship with its shield down showed both. Every capital ship and frigate now behaves the same.
  • A-8 · escorts keep up. Your two escorts now leave the bays right after you (they used to launch about 25 s later, behind the defenders and patrols). They boost when you boost, catch up when they fall behind (speed limit 1.3 times the normal one while catching up), and settle back into their slots without overshooting.
  • B-2 · win condition. Only destroying all three enemy capital ships wins. The 15 minute time-out that judged a match by fleet health is off (-OrionTimeLimit=<s> brings it back for tests). The header says "DESTROY ALL 3 ENEMY CAPITAL SHIPS", the clock counts up, and the win screen says "ALL 3 <TEAM> CAPITAL SHIPS DESTROYED".
  • C-1 · no exhaust in the hangar. A fighter has no trail and no plume until its rearmost nozzle has passed the bay doors; then its trail starts fresh. For the player and every bot.
  • C-7 · frigate forcefield. A see-through shell round each frigate, about 280 m long round its 250 m hull: faint while the shield is up, a flare on each hit it takes, gone while the shield is down.
  • C-9 · frigate mouse setting. Settings > Controls has a "FRIGATE MOUSE" slider, 0.25x to 3.00x, saved with the other settings. The fighter's mouse is unchanged.
  • C-17 · frigate spawns. A frigate starts 300 to 600 m from a standing carrier of its own team (150 to 900 m if nothing fits), clear of the bay launch lanes and the debris, then flies its circle round the station as before.
  • C-19 · debris round the carriers. Each capital ship carries a cloud of 40 tumbling wreck pieces, 8 to 60 m, made from the station's parts, 150 to 600 m from its hull, solid to ships and kept out of the bay lanes and the path ahead. -OrionDebris=0 switches it off; -OrionDebrisSeed=<n> repeats a layout. The project has no rock model, so the cloud is wreckage only.

Planner decisions (Claude Opus 5.5), for review

  1. Weak point marks last 10 s after the player's last hit, with a 2 s fade.
  2. A-6 read as the shield case; the reviewer and the executor found no other cause in the code.
  3. B-2: the time-out is off, not lengthened.
  4. C-19: the debris travels with each carrier, rather than fixed rocks along the carriers' paths, so the obstacles are always where the fighting is.

How it was made

  • Plan: epic EP-006 and items JAM-0023 to JAM-0030 in the tracker, with each file owned by one item.
  • Seven executors coded in parallel, each in its own compile-only copy; their changes were merged with no conflicts.
  • An Opus review found one blocker: the first frigate spawn code moved frigate circles so they flew through the station and the carriers' lanes. It also found five should-fixes.
  • Four fix rounds followed, then an Opus check of the fixes (one more should-fix, applied).
  • Test matches with pictures showed three more problems, all fixed:
    • escorts too slow to catch a boosting player;
    • escorts launching late;
    • the player's fighter flying out of its bay into a frigate that had started in the launch lane.

Tested

  • Build: the editor target builds with exit code 0 and no compiler warnings (19 files, about 1,290 lines added).
  • Development package D:\Games\2026 Epic Jam\Builds\2026-11-15_1834_v0.16.0: 723 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings, 1.4 GB.
  • Smoke test: RESULT: PASS after 206s. The log reads Match over after 203 s: BLUE TEAM WINS once the third Red capital ship fell.
  • Shipping package D:\Games\2026 Epic Jam\Builds\2026-11-15_1834_v0.16.0_shipping:
    • The first try failed after a successful cook: the cook program returned exit code 777003 at shutdown.
    • The second try succeeded.
    • The Shipping check passed: both hidden runs stayed up for 70 s with no crash report, no debug files, and the C++ runtime installer included.
  • Pictures taken in test matches with no window:
    • No trail in the hangar, only the nozzle glow.
    • The frigate forcefield: one tight ellipsoid.
    • Debris clouds round the carriers.
    • The Controls page showing FRIGATE MOUSE 2.00x and v0.16.0.
    • At 51 s into a straight full boost (225 m/s), both escorts flying beside the player, on screen.
  • Frigate spawns: every spawn line in the test logs shows the frigate nearer its own capital ship (450 to 790 m) than the enemy's (2.1 to 3.5 km).
  • Debris cost: two copies of the package ran side by side, one with -OrionDebris=0.
    • With the same load (86 fighters): 6.3 against 6.0 ms per frame.
    • Later in the match: 8.0 against 7.2 ms and 13.3 against 11.9 ms, but the two battles had grown to different sizes by then.
    • Both runs logged one handled engine ensure about a mesh's texture data. It appears with the debris switched off too, and it is not in the v0.14.0 logs, so it came in between v0.14.0 and now. Shipping builds have no ensures.
  • A match still ends without the time-out: a bot-only match with no cheat, on a copy of the Development package, ended by itself after 510 s (8.5 minutes): Match over after 510 s: RED TEAM WINS. Red had lost one capital ship; the three Blue ones fell within the last 82 s.

Not verified

  • Nobody has played this build. Feel (the escorts' flying, the debris as an obstacle, the frigate mouse) needs a real flight.
  • The hull may shield a weak point ball from shots on some model, which only play shows (the A-6 risk the executor named).
  • O1 and O2 are changing nearby code at the same time: C-14 recolours the same weak point marks, C-11 changes the escorts' firing, C-2 and A-2 change the same Controls page. Whoever merges second will have small conflicts to resolve.
  • Zilvinas's branch v0.16.0_Zilvinas uses the same number. Following the version list's rule (one number for one state of the code), whichever build reaches develop second should become v0.17.0.

AI usage

  • Main session (planner, integrator, tests): Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, at the session's default effort. It also made one six-line change itself: escorts launch right after the player.
  • Executors: Claude Sonnet 5.5 (claude-sonnet-5-5) at medium effort, 11 runs: seven items, then fix parts A, B, C and D.
  • Reviewers: Claude Opus 5.5 at high effort, 2 runs: the review, then the check of the fixes.
  • Used for: the plan and briefs, all game code, the reviews, the test matches and pictures, the packages, this entry.

Coach tip (topic: testing AI teammates)

Test the case the player complains about, not the easy one. The escorts held formation in a calm test. Only a straight boost from the very first second showed the real cause: they launched 25 s after the player. A test that copies the complaint finds the cause; a test that copies the design only confirms it.

15 Nov v0.16.1Cinematic ModeAndriusOpus 5.5Sonnet 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius ("Could you mak a version of the build where when you launch the game you can click esc and go into Cinematic Mode where you are a ghost camera and you can move as fast as the fighter can in any direction. Iterate as v0.16.1"). The details below (controls, what happens to your ship, no HUD) are Claude's choices, for the team to review.

What changed for a player

  • Where it is: the ESC menu has a new row, CINEMATIC MODE, between Settings and Quit. It works at any time, including on the ship choice screen right after the game starts. While you are in the mode, the row reads LEAVE CINEMATIC MODE.
  • The ghost camera:
    • It flies through everything, and bots and turrets do not see it.
    • Controls: W/S/A/D move, SPACE/CTRL up and down, the mouse turns, Q/E roll, SHIFT boosts, the mouse wheel scales the speed down to 0.1x for slow shots, H hides the hint. Sticks and bumpers work on a gamepad.
    • It is as fast as a fighter: 50 m/s, and 225 m/s with SHIFT. The numbers are read from the fighter's code, so they follow any retune.
    • Movement eases in and out for smooth shots, and the camera stops when no key is held.
  • Your ship while you film:
    • A fighter is flown by a bot of your team, with flight assist on, so it keeps fighting. A frigate flies its circle by itself.
    • When you leave, you get your ship back. If it was lost meanwhile, you relaunch from the bay after the normal wait.
  • A clean picture: no HUD, marks or messages while filming, only a hint line that fades after 6 s. The ESC menu and the win screen still show.
  • Tracers are drawn relative to the moving camera, so they look right while you fly fast.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. The ship is handed to a bot rather than left drifting, so filming does not lose you the ship or change the match.
  2. "As fast as the fighter" was read as its normal top speed plus its boost.
  3. The number is v0.16.1 as Andrius asked. By the version list's rule, a new thing to do would be MINOR (v0.17.0). The list's row says so.

How it was made

  • One Sonnet executor built it from a brief (tracker JAM-0031).
  • An Opus review found no blockers and three should-fixes, all applied:
    • an instant relaunch that skipped the respawn wait;
    • a drifting ship if flight assist was off;
    • the objective message used up while hidden.
  • Two filming fixes were added after the review: tracers relative to the ghost, and a start view for the test switch.
  • The main session (Opus) delayed the test switch by 2 s, so a test starts where a player who pressed ESC would start.

Tested

  • Editor build: exit code 0, no compiler warnings.
  • Pictures from test matches with no window:
    • the ESC menu with CINEMATIC MODE selected;
    • cinematic mode entered from a fighter: it starts at the fighter's view in the bay and flies out through the doors;
    • cinematic mode entered from a frigate: it starts at the frigate's view, with a carrier, debris and a frigate's forcefield in frame;
    • no HUD in any of them, and the hint line gone after 6 s.
  • The log lines Cinematic mode on (was: fighter launching), Cinematic mode on (was: frigate) and Ghost camera: top speed 50 m/s, boost 225 m/s.
  • Development package D:\Games\2026 Epic Jam\Builds\2026-11-15_2020_v0.16.1: packaged smoke test RESULT: PASS after 241s; its log reads Game version: v0.16.1.
  • Shipping package D:\Games\2026 Epic Jam\Builds\2026-11-15_2020_v0.16.1_shipping: the Shipping check passed (both hidden runs stayed up 70 s, no crash report, no debug files, runtime installer included).

Not verified

  • Nobody has flown the ghost by hand, so mouse directions, Q/E roll, wheel steps and the feel need a real try.
  • Leaving cinematic mode was checked by reading the code and by the review, not in a run, because the test switches only enter.

AI usage

  • Main session: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. It planned and wrote the brief, ran the tests and packages, and made the 2 s delay change.
  • Executors: Claude Sonnet 5.5 (claude-sonnet-5-5) at medium effort, 2 runs: the feature, then the review fixes.
  • Reviewer: Claude Opus 5.5 at high effort, 1 run.

Coach tip (topic: test switches)

A test switch should reach the same state a player reaches, not just the same code. The first cinematic test entered on the very first frame, before the camera had moved to the player's ship, so it started in empty space and looked like a bug. Waiting 2 s made the test match what a player sees after pressing ESC.

15 Nov v0.17.0The O1 prompts of the v0.15.0 play testAndyAndriusFable 5.2Opus 5.5Sonnet 5.5

Asked by: Andy
Who decided: Andy ("please take prompts for orchestrator 1 and update the game. capture this as branch feat/for 0.16.0, feel free to fan out using opus 5.5 to give tasks and use sonnet 5.5 to write the necessary code"). The prompts come from the play test list v0.15.0_prompt-assignments.csv; this build does ten of the eleven O1 rows. Andy also chose the new project name for the eleventh ("orion-81-space-jam") and, when develop had moved, "Rebase now, rename later", which Claude had recommended. When develop moved a second time (v0.16.1, Cinematic Mode), Andy asked: "check with latest develop". How each prompt is met is Claude's reading, listed under "Choices Claude made, for review".

What changed for a player

  1. A-1 · d-pad in menus. The ESC menu already took the d-pad, A and B. The screen that ignored them was CHOOSE YOUR SHIP, which read only 1/2 and X/Y. It is now a list of two: d-pad or arrows select, A or Enter launches, 1/2 still pick directly. The ESC menu's footer names the pad buttons.
  2. C-2 · mouse too slow. The main cause was not the sensitivity number. The fighter's 9 g turn limit threw away whatever the mouse asked beyond it, every frame: at 150 m/s a 60 degree flick turned the ship 0.1 degrees. Now up to 30 degrees of mouse movement is kept and played out (a 90 degree flick turned 30 degrees in 1.5 s). The base sensitivity went from 0.12 to 0.18 degrees per count. Settings > Controls has a FIGHTER MOUSE SENSITIVITY slider, 0.20x to 2.20x.
  3. C-10 · a button for each weapon, and keys you can change. Left mouse fires the minigun, right the energy cannon, the rear side button (MB4) rockets; middle click zooms. Each weapon has its own cooldown. A gamepad's trigger still fires the selected weapon. Settings > Controls > FIGHTER KEYS lists the fighter's 15 keys: select a row and press a key; a key already in use swaps places; ESC, M, 1/2/3, the wheel, tilde and F11 are reserved; one row resets them all.
  4. C-4 · clunky frigate. The heavy feel was intended (v0.12.0: "keys steer, the mouse aims"), but nobody had flown the numbers. The clunk came from the turn taking 1.5 s to build and 1.5 s to stop. Now yaw 24, pitch 18 and roll 30 degrees a second (were 14, 10, 18), at full rate in 0.6 s and stopped in 0.4 s. AI frigates fly as before.
  5. C-6 · the main cannon "out of ammo". There is no ammo. Both main turrets of a frigate are weak points, and once both were destroyed the guns stayed silent until the frigate died. Now a main turret lost on the player's frigate comes back 15 s later. The HUD says "(BACK IN n s)" on its LOST line and "MAIN GUNS BACK IN n s" under the crosshair when both are out.
  6. C-16 · speed meter. The frigate's acceleration bar is now a speed bar: the fill is speed against the 140 m/s top, a white tick marks the throttle setting, the label reads "SPEED 45 m/s SET 45 m/s". The fighter keeps its acceleration bar.
  7. A-7 · capital ship plumes. 30% of their length (PlumeLengthScale 0.5 to 0.15). The width is unchanged, so each is a short glow on its nozzle.
  8. C-18 · carriers at 50% at the start. Confirmed: the whole battle ran behind the ship choice screen. Now the match starts once, when the player's ship exists. Behind the choice screen the first carriers and the frigates cruise and nobody fires, and the clock reads 0:00. At the start every capital ship's hull and shield are reset, the fighters launch, and ships 2 and 3 jump in 30 s and 60 s later.
  9. C-12 · blue paint. On Blue's carriers and frigates every red marking is team blue, and the frigates' light rows glow blue. Red's ships are unchanged.
  10. A-9 · the planet. A smooth sphere (16,128 triangles) with a Neptune skin, its equator in the ring's plane (it was 17.4 degrees off). The skin is NASA's "Neptune" texture map from NASA Science 3D Resources, a painting credited "Don Davis & JPL/Caltech": the only global map of a blue planet that NASA or JPL publish, and a small one (720 x 360). Source, terms, hash and credit line are in CREDITS.md.
  11. Also fixed: the engine check (an ensure) that the run-time trail mesh failed at the start of every Development match. Andy's v0.15.1 play test found it, and the v0.16.0 entry notes it too.

Held back: A-4, no more "ctf". The rename of the project to orion_81_space_jam is written as a script and was proven on its own (it moved 327 files and changed 35; the renamed project built, won a match with no renderer, packaged and passed the smoke test). It is not in this build. It goes in as its own pull request once O2 has merged, so that nobody has to rebase across 330 moved files.

How the work was split

PackagePromptsBrief and reviewCodeReview
Menu and controlsA-1, C-2, C-10Opus 5.5Sonnet 5.5approved; 2 optional follow-ups done
Flown frigateC-4, C-6, C-16Opus 5.5Sonnet 5.5approved; 3 optional follow-ups done

PlanetA-9Opus 5.5Sonnet 5.5approved; 2 optional follow-ups done
Merging, the rebase, the rename script, packagesFable 5.2 (main session)no second model

Each package ran in its own full copy of the project, so five were written at once. Each agent could build, run a match with no renderer and take pictures. The five packages merged with no conflicts between them.

What the reviews found

  • No review found anything that had to be fixed. They listed nine optional improvements, all done by Sonnet 5.5: a softer day and night edge on the planet; a log line that really measures the planet against the ring; no repair countdown on a wrecked frigate; a size check on the turret array; the "MAIN GUNS BACK IN" line under the crosshair; a paint log line that names each carrier; key bindings logged as plain pairs; a hint line that shrinks to fit; and the objective message shown again at the match start.
  • The frigate review noted that the tester had already misread the small LOST line as "out of ammo"; that is why the warning under the crosshair was added.

What the tests showed that the reviews could not

  • develop moved while this was being built. Andrius merged the O3 prompts and tagged them v0.16.0 at 18:54 UTC. Claude did not fetch again before packaging at 19:50, so its first two packages held the O1 work on top of v0.15.1, without the O3 work, under a number that was already taken. They are renamed 2026-11-15_1950_o1prompts_before-rebase and ..._shipping.
  • The rebase onto v0.16.0 (Andy: "Rebase now, rename later") had conflicts in five files:
    • The Controls page. O3's FRIGATE MOUSE slider was the page's first row by number; this work had turned the page into a table of rows. Both sliders are now rows of that table, with FIGHTER KEYS and BACK under them, and both show their value as "1.00x".
    • One line merged by itself and was wrong: the frigate slider's key steps were tied to "row 0", which had become the fighter's slider. Found by reading, fixed, and checked with key presses (frigate 1.05x, 1.10x; fighter 1.10x, 1.20x).
    • The settings (both sliders' values are kept), and one line each in the frigate's and the game mode's headers (both sides kept).
  • Dropped: the objective message at the match start. The O3 build already shows the objective once the player has a ship, with the new wording.
  • Added: the clock holds at 0:00 until the player has chosen. Since v0.16.0 the clock counts up; behind the choice screen it would have counted from the level load and jumped back.
  • A test of Claude's own was wrong. A match with no renderer cannot press buttons on the ship choice screen, because that screen reads its input while it draws. It timed out after 7 minutes; the same test with the renderer on passed.
  • develop moved a second time. Andrius merged Cinematic Mode as v0.16.1 at 20:34 UTC, twelve minutes after the v0.17.0 packages of 20:22 were made. Andy: "check with latest develop". The branch was rebased again and packaged again. The 20:22 packages are renamed 2026-11-15_2022_v0.17.0_before-v0.16.1 and 2026-11-15_2022_v0.17.0_before-v0.16.1_shipping.
  • The second rebase had three conflicts in code, each solved by keeping both sides:
    • the QUIT row's label, beside the new CINEMATIC MODE row;
    • one include line in the HUD;
    • the game mode, where both sides had added functions at the same place.
  • The version list, the version setting and this devlog conflicted too. v0.17.0 goes above v0.16.1, and both devlog entries are kept.
  • Where Cinematic Mode and this work meet, checked in runs with the renderer on:
    • The ESC menu has five rows now, and QUIT moved from row 3 to row 4. This time the row numbers merged right: all five rows and the key line are inside the panel.
    • From a fighter, switched on and off through the real menu with fed key presses: Cinematic mode on (was: fighter launching), then Cinematic mode off (back to: fighter). After leaving, the G key switched the flight assist off (the HUD reads ASSIST OFF), so the player's keys reach the fighter again. The v0.16.1 entry lists leaving as not run; the key feed built for A-1 made it possible to run.
    • From the ship choice screen: Cinematic mode on (was: choice screen). The d-pad and A, pressed while filming, did not choose a ship. After Cinematic mode off (back to: choice screen) the same two presses chose the frigate, and the match started.
  • Two things the team should know. Nothing was changed for them:
    1. From the ship choice screen, Cinematic Mode now films a calm world. Since C-18 the battle starts only when the player has chosen a ship. To film a battle, choose a ship first.
    2. The ghost camera reads its mouse turn from the fighter. C-2 raised that from 0.12 to 0.18 degrees per count, so the ghost turns 1.5 times faster than in v0.16.1. It does not follow the new FIGHTER MOUSE SENSITIVITY slider.

Choices Claude made, for review (Andy, Andrius, Zilvinas)

  1. C-2: the 30 degree mouse backlog (MouseBacklogMaxDegrees; 0 restores the old behaviour). The turn rate at speed is unchanged; if the fighter still feels slow at speed, the number to change is the 9 g limit.
  2. C-10: rockets on the rear side button; only the fighter's keys can be changed; the reset has no confirmation; the frigate also zooms with middle click; bots get the per-weapon cooldowns too.
  3. C-4: the new turn rates and timings. "Still clunky" or "too twitchy" is a one-line change.
  4. C-6: 15 s, and the player's frigate only. The hull lost with the turret is not given back.
  5. C-18: a calm world behind the choice screen, not a frozen one; the 10 s hangar launch counts as match time.
  6. A-7: the plume keeps its width, so it is short and round.
  7. C-12: the blue is subtle on a carrier's shadowed side; the frigates' rocket noses stay red; fighters were out of scope.
  8. A-9: a painted, low-resolution Neptune. The game has no credits screen for it or for the sky map.
  9. The Controls page after the merge: O3's label "FRIGATE MOUSE" is kept as it was, beside "FIGHTER MOUSE SENSITIVITY"; the fighter's value now reads "1.00x" like the frigate's (it read "x1.00").
  10. The number: v0.17.0, because v0.16.0 was taken and a tag is never moved. The branch keeps the name Andy gave it, feat/for-0.16.0.
  11. After the second rebase the number stays v0.17.0, because it was never tagged, pushed or played. The two packages of 20:22 are renamed, not deleted.

Coach tip (topic: rebasing onto a teammate's work)

A merge with no conflict is not a correct merge. Git compares lines, not meaning: O3's slider was "row 0" of a page, this branch turned the page into a table, and the line that said "row 0" merged cleanly onto the wrong slider. After a rebase, search for every number or order that both sides rely on (row numbers, list orders, array sizes) and test each one by hand. Then fetch once more before you package: a package is a claim about develop plus your work, and it is only true for the develop you last fetched. It came true twelve minutes later: develop moved again right after the packages were made.

15 Nov The O2 prompts of the v0.15.0 play testWeak points, frigate guns, escorts, trails, controls and audio settingsZilvinasFable 5.2

Asked by: Zilvinas
Who decided: Zilvinas (do the eleven prompts marked O2 in v0.15.0_prompt-assignments.csv on a new branch v0.16.0_Zilvinas, write only there, and leave the O1 and O3 prompts alone). How each prompt was met, and every number below, are Claude's choices, for the team to review.

What was built

  1. A-2: Menu, Settings, Controls now has two pages. MOUSE & KEYBOARD is the key list (with the FRIGATE MOUSE slider of v0.16.0 under it). CONTROLLER is a drawing of a pad with every control named beside it, and a SHIP entry that switches the names between fighter and frigate.
  2. A-5: During a hangar launch the engine trail rides the capital ship. Fighters leave through the stern while the ship flies on bow first, so early in the launch a fighter is still moving backwards along its own nose, and a trail left behind in the world lay in front of it.
  3. A-10: A frigate has a weak point on each of its six engines, ENGINE 1 to ENGINE 6 (it had two, one for each group of three). Losing one puts out that engine's plume and takes a sixth of the drive.
  4. B-1: Missile trails are about half as wide (cruise missile 3.2 m for 6 m, torpedo 1.1 m for 2 m, rocket 20 cm for 30 cm). A stretch of trail that bends is drawn as two or four short pieces along a smooth curve, so each joint turns only a little.
  5. C-5: A frigate with a pilot: the main turrets turn 2.5 times as fast (87.5 degrees a second round, 62.5 up and down), fire within 5 degrees of the aim (3 before), and turn 140 degrees either side of the bow (115 before). A magazine of 16 shells: empty, it reloads in 3 s; not empty, shells come back one every 0.4 s after 1 s without a shot. The HUD has a MAIN GUNS bar (shells left, or RELOADING with the seconds), and a small square for each turret that shows where it points and turns green when it may fire.
  6. C-8: Aim assist on a flown frigate's main guns. An enemy ship under the crosshair, or an enemy fighter or frigate within 1.5 degrees of it, is led: the guns aim where it will be when the shell arrives. The HUD draws a ring where the shells are sent, a line to it from the crosshair, and LEAD with the shell's flight time (or LEAD OUT OF RANGE).
  7. C-11: The player's two escorts fire on their own. With the leader quiet, each picks the nearest enemy fighter within reach of its guns, else an enemy frigate, else a weak point of an enemy capital ship, no more than 70 degrees off the leader's heading, and shoots while it holds its place in the formation.
  8. C-13: Menu, Settings, AUDIO: master, effects and music volume, each 0 to 100 %, saved with the other settings.
  9. C-14: Weak point marks are in the colour of the ship's own side: red on a Red ship. A hit the shield stopped is drawn dimmer and thinner (it was the shield's light blue).
  10. C-15: A frigate shell fired at a weak point of an enemy capital ship flies twice as far (6 km for 3 km). The shells of a frigate with a pilot also count four times on such a weak point and scatter less (0.1 degrees for 0.25).
  11. C-20: A fighter's gun round fired at a weak point of an enemy capital ship flies three times as far (768 m for 256 m with the kinetic gun, 759 m with the energy gun). Rounds fired at anything else keep their range, so dogfights do not change. The lead ring on the fighter's HUD knows the longer reach.

How it was made

  • One session wrote all of it; no agents. 17 source files, about 1,340 lines added, no asset and no setting changed, so nothing new is in Git LFS.
  • The branch was cut from develop at 04b46f0 (v0.15.1). While the work went on, the O3 prompts were merged as v0.16.0 (pull requests #54 and #55), and the branch was rebased onto d1abb41 with the team's rebase skill. Seven files conflicted. Both sides were kept in each: the FRIGATE MOUSE slider of v0.16.0 moved from the Controls page to the new Mouse & Keyboard page; the weak point marks keep v0.16.0's rule (shown only after the player's own hit) with the team colour of C-14; the settings file holds the frigate mouse scale and the three volumes.
  • git range-diff shows the one commit before and after the rebase; the backup is the branch backup/v0.16.0_Zilvinas-20261115-211448.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. The longer reach belongs to the round, not the gun (C-15, C-20). A round is given the longer life when the line it is fired on passes within 15 m, plus 3 cm for each metre of the way, of a living weak point of an enemy capital ship. Raising the guns' range outright would have changed every dogfight and every bot.
  2. Only a frigate with a pilot gets the fourfold damage, the tighter scatter, the magazine and the faster turrets. Frigates flown by the game keep their shells and their turrets, so the pace of the fleet battle stays as the team measured it. Fourfold makes a pilot's shell worth 60 on a weak point, 80 a second, against 69 a second for a fighter's kinetic gun.
  3. The "stutter" of C-5 was read as the turrets lagging the mouse. The guns fired only within 3 degrees of the aim and turned at 35 degrees a second, so they fell silent whenever the view moved. Nobody played it to confirm that this is what was felt.
  4. The magazine: 16 shells, 3 s reload, top-up after 1 s. C-6 (an O1 prompt) asks that the main guns can always fight; this design never leaves them empty for more than 3 s. If O1 builds its own ammunition, one of the two has to go.
  5. Aim assist leads, it does not steer. It adds the target's travel to the point under the crosshair; it never moves the aim sideways onto a target, except for a fighter or frigate within 1.5 degrees.
  6. Escorts turn up to 70 degrees off the leader's heading for an enemy of their own (25 for the leader's target, as before) and never leave their slot to close in.
  7. Six engine weak points share what the two had: 6 x 85 health for 2 x 250, 6 x 1 % of the ship's health for 2 x 3 %, each a ball of 4.2 m radius on its nozzle.
  8. Effects volume is every sound the game has today; the music slider changes nothing that can be heard yet, because the game has no music. The volumes go through the engine's own mixing with two objects made at run time, so there is no new asset.
  9. The Controller page draws a generic two-stick pad with Xbox names (A, B, X, Y, LT, RT, VIEW, START), taken from the two ships' own input tables.
  10. No version number was set. The branch is named v0.16.0, and v0.16.0 is already tagged on develop. ProjectVersion, CHANGELOG.md and the tags are untouched; the test packages are named by topic and commit, as the team did for builds made before a number was given.

Tested

  • Development package Builds/2026-11-15_1940_o2-fixes_c76e4bb: 214 s, 723 packages cooked, 0 errors, 0 warnings, 1,399 MB. Smoke test (Scripts/smoke-packaged.ps1, 480 s limit): RESULT: PASS after 221s, Match over after 215 s: BLUE TEAM WINS, 0 lines with Error: in the game's own log (kept in BuildLogs/smoke_test_game.log). Frames 10.2 to 10.7 ms on average, worst 20.8 ms, with 134 to 139 bot fighters and no renderer; not compared with a v0.16.0 package on this PC.
  • Shipping package Builds/2026-11-15_1940_o2-fixes_c76e4bb_shipping: 251 s, 723 packages, 0 errors, 0 warnings, 870 MB in 28 files, no debug files, the Visual C++ installer included. Scripts/check-shipping.ps1 -RunSeconds 60: both runs (no renderer, and the real renderer with no window) were up after 60 s with no crash report.
  • C-11, in the packaged game's log: nine lines such as "Escort Blue-17 opens fire on its own target: a weak point of a capital ship at 631 m, leader quiet" and "Escort Blue-19 opens fire on its own target: a fighter at 130 m, leader quiet". Before the change the escorts' 30-second log line read "0 rounds, no target".
  • C-20, same log: "Weak point reach: 4579 rounds fired at a weak point of a capital ship with the longer reach; 2807 of them hit the ship past their ordinary range".
  • A-2, C-13, C-5 (the bar): pictures in Builds/pictures_2026-11-15_o2-fixes, each one looked at: the Controller page for fighter and for frigate, the Audio page with effects at 60 %, the Mouse & Keyboard page with FRIGATE MOUSE, and a flown frigate's HUD with MAIN GUNS 16 / 16 and the turret mark on the aim. Pictures 01, 02, 03, 06 and 07 were taken before the rebase.

Every other run had -nosound, where the log says "no audio device: not applied".
  • The player's hits on weak points after the rebase: with the test pilot firing (-OrionShotPilot=aim+fire) the log has "Player hit Red capital ship 3 weak point 1 (ENGINE 1), shield down" and three more.

Not verified

  • The frigate's guns were never fired by a pilot (C-5, C-8, C-15). The test pilot flies only a fighter. The magazine, the reload, the faster turrets, the lead and the fourfold damage compile and are drawn at rest; none was seen working. This is the largest gap.
  • C-14 after the rebase was not seen. Since v0.16.0 a weak point is marked only for 10 s after the player's own hit. 15 NovAn objectives list on the right of the screenZilvinasFable 5.2

Asked by: Zilvinas
Who decided: Zilvinas (the feature, its three lines, 3 seconds of attention and then a small list that stays, and its own branch v0.16.0_Zilvinas_add_feat). The sizes, the place on the screen, the pulsing frame and the highlight are Claude's choices, listed below for review.

What was built

  1. When the player's game starts, a list headed OBJECTIVES stands on the right of the screen: large, on a dark panel, with an orange frame that pulses twice a second.
  2. After 3 seconds it shrinks over 0.6 s to a small list under the kill feed and above the weapon list, and it stays there for the whole match: in a fighter, in a frigate, while waiting to launch again and on the end screen.
  3. It has three lines for this version: "Destroy all enemy Carrier ships", "Enemy Carrier ships remaining: 3" and "Target ship weak points for additional damage".
  4. The second line follows the match. When an enemy carrier is destroyed the number goes down and the line blinks orange and white for 2.5 s.

How it was made

  • One new function, AOrionHUD::DrawObjectives, drawn on the HUD's canvas after the kill feed. No new asset, no new file, 119 lines in OrionHUD.cpp and OrionHUD.h.
  • The number is GetCapitalShipsLeft for the enemy team, the same figure the scoreboard shows as SHIPS 2/3: carriers not yet destroyed, counting those still to jump in.
  • The branch was cut from develop at d1abb41 (v0.16.0), not from the O2 branch, so the two can be merged in either order.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. The 3 seconds run from when the player has chosen a ship, not from when the program starts. On the fighter-or-frigate screen the list is large the whole time; the 3 seconds start with the launch. A player who reads the choice screen for 20 s would otherwise never see the large list.
  2. Sizes at 1080p. Large: medium font at 1.3, top edge 300 px down, panel 85 % dark. Small: medium font at 1.0, top edge 250 px down, panel 40 % dark, text at 85 %. A first try at 0.78 was too small to read in the picture and was changed.
  3. The wording is Zilvinas's, with "Carrier ships". The scoreboard at the top still says "DESTROY ALL 3 ENEMY CAPITAL SHIPS", and the kill feed opens with "Objective: destroy all 3 enemy capital ships. Hit their glowing weak points." (from v0.16.0). The game now uses both words for the same ship.
  4. The count line shows the number only ("remaining: 2"), not "2 of 3".
  5. A thin orange bar stays down the left edge of the small list, so the eye finds it again.
  6. The list is also drawn on the end screen, where it reads "remaining: 0" after a win.

Tested

  • Editor target compiled: Result: Succeeded, no error or warning lines.
  • Pictures of the player's view, each one looked at, in Builds/pictures_2026-11-15_objectives-list: 1.5 s (large, framed), 3.3 s (shrinking), 8 s (small, readable), 200 s into a match ("remaining: 2" with SHIPS 2/3 on the scoreboard), and the end of the match ("remaining: 0" in orange beside VICTORY).
  • The log of that match (Red weakened by the test cheat OrionDamageCarrier 1 0.9): "Objectives list shown: enemy carriers remaining 3", then "3 -> 2", "2 -> 1" and "1 -> 0", and Match over after 214 s: BLUE TEAM WINS.
  • A match with no window on this branch: RESULT: PASS after 231s, Match over after 222 s: BLUE TEAM WINS. (With no renderer no HUD is drawn, so this shows only that the match still runs.)

Not verified

  • Not packaged. Neither a Development nor a Shipping package of this branch was made, so the smoke test and the Shipping check were not run on it.
  • Nobody has played it. The pulse and the shrink were seen as three still pictures, not as movement.
  • Not checked at other screen sizes than 1920 x 1080, and not together with the O2 branch's new HUD parts (the frigate's ammo bar is in the opposite corner; the two were never drawn in one build).
  • No second model reviewed the code: the main session wrote it alone.

Still open

  1. Zilvinas: which version number this gets. v0.16.0 is already tagged on develop (the O3 prompts), so this would be v0.17.0 or v0.18.0 depending on whether it or the O2 branch is merged first. No number was set, nothing was tagged.
  2. Zilvinas: whether to open a pull request into develop. The branch is pushed (cbe5949); no pull request was opened.
  3. Whether the scoreboard and the feed should say "Carrier ships" too (choice 3).

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Fable 5.2 (claude-fable-5-2), session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: the design, the C++, the picture runs and the match with no window, this entry.

Coach tip

An objective the player can read at any moment is worth more than one shown once. Keep the list to what the player can act on now, and when a fourth line is wanted, ask which of the three it replaces.

15 Nov The game shows its official nameOrion-81 Space JamAndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius: the game's official name is "Orion-81 Space Jam" ("it's not Orion-81"; Orion-81 is the studio). The trailer already uses it, and this change makes the game match. The Unreal project, module, .uproject, program file and repo keep their names (orion_81_space_ctf), so packaging and the save folder stay the same a few hours before the hand-in.

What changed for a player

  • The game window's title bar reads Orion-81 Space Jam. Before, with no title set, it showed the Unreal project name orion_81_space_ctf.
  • The program's Windows details read Orion-81 Space Jam. They show in Task Manager's list and on the program's Properties > Details tab (Product name and File description). In the v0.16.1 Shipping package they read UnrealGame.

The HUD, the ship choice screen, the ESC menu and the win and lose screens are drawn in C++, and none of their text names the game (every string was searched for "Orion", "Orion-81", "Orion81", "CTF" and "Jam"). The ESC menu's corner still shows only the version, v0.17.0.

Strings changed

WhereSettingBeforeAfter
orion_81_space_ctf/Config/DefaultGame.ini, section [/Script/EngineSettings.GeneralProjectSettings]ProjectDisplayedTitle: the window's title barnot set, so orion_81_space_ctfOrion-81 Space Jam
the same sectionProjectName: the program's Product name and File descriptionnot set, so UnrealGameOrion-81 Space Jam

Tested (branch feat/official-name, on develop at 4cfe91a)

  • Editor target: built with exit code 0, no errors and no warnings (38 s).
  • Development game target: built with exit code 0 (34 s). The built program's version information shows Product name and File description Orion-81 Space Jam, while its internal name and file name are still orion_81_space_ctf.
  • Read back in a headless editor run: ProjectDisplayedTitle is the text Orion-81 Space Jam (read as text, not as a raw string), ProjectName is the same, and the game name that sets the save folder is still orion_81_space_ctf.

Not verified

  • Nobody has seen the title bar on screen yet. A check would have opened a window on Andrius's PC while he might be playing. Epic's 5.8 documentation says the displayed title is the window's title bar text, and that non-Shipping builds may add debug information after it. Look at the title bar on the next package.
  • No package was made for this change.

Still open (for Andrius)

  • The version number. By the rule in CHANGELOG.md this is a PATCH (v0.17.1). It is not set here, so it can't collide with the other branches in flight. Set it when this is packaged.
  • The program's Details tab still shows Unreal's defaults: company Epic Games, Inc. and copyright "Fill out your copyright notice in the Description page of Project Settings.". Two lines in the same section (CompanyName, CopyrightNotice) would fix them. The wording is Andrius's to choose.
  • Outside the game, the itch.io page, the submission zip and the packaged program file (orion_81_space_ctf.exe) still use the old names. Renaming the program file means renaming the project (A-4), which is not part of this change.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. One session and no subagents did the search, the change, the builds and checks, this note and the pull request.

Coach tip (topic: naming a packaged game)

In Unreal a game's name lives in two layers: the project name (folders, module, program file, save path) and the displayed name (window title, the program's Windows details). Close to a deadline, change only the displayed layer. That's two lines of config, and every script that finds the program or the save folder keeps working. Then check the program's Properties > Details after a game-target build, which takes under a minute, instead of waiting for a full package.

15 Nov SubmissionItch.io pricing for the jam downloadAndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Decided by: not decided yet (recommendation below, awaiting Andrius)

Question

How should the itch.io project be set up for paying for a download?

Recommendation

Set Pricing to No payments. Judges and players download straight away, and nobody needs to set up payouts. "$0 or donate" is also free, but it shows a donation pop-up before the download. Don't use Paid: judges would hit a paywall. The full rules PDF hasn't been checked for an explicit pricing rule.

Where it is on itch.io

Dashboard, then Create new project (or Edit game). Kind of project: Downloadable. Pricing: No payments. Upload TeamName_GameName.zip (under 1 GB) and tick Windows. Keep it as a Draft while testing, set it to Public, then use Submit on the jam page before Mon 16 Nov, 01:59 GMT.

Coach tip (topic: itch.io submission)

Once it's uploaded, download the ZIP from your own itch page into a fresh folder and run it. That's the exact file the judges get.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Cowork, low effort
  • Used for: checking the itch.io pricing docs, drafting this entry
15 Nov TrailerFootage and trailer trial 1AndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (record the trailer footage with ScreenForge; clean footage with no watermark; a 30 s trailer with the action packed into the first 10 s, one second per scene)

Question

Record gameplay for the jam's trailer with our own recorder, ScreenForge, at a quality that suits the entry, then cut a first 30-second trailer from the takes.

What the jam asks for

  • A link to a 30 to 60 second gameplay video. The jam page sets no resolution or host.
  • Entries close 15 Nov at 5:59 PM PST (01:59 UTC, 16 Nov). Late entries are disqualified.

Recording

  • ScreenForge records the whole 4K screen with no clicks (--record-start video) and saves it at 1920x1080, 60 fps, H.264, about 25 Mbit/s: twice YouTube's upload advice, so the footage survives one more encode.
  • No sound: ScreenForge's unattended mode never records audio, and the game has no sound files yet.
  • Andrius chose clean footage over ScreenForge's burned-in "Produced with AI" watermark, for the trailer takes only. The AI credit is on the trailer's end card instead.
  • Take 1: 3:36, game HUD on. Take 2: 3:16, filmed without the HUD. Both 0 dropped frames.
  • Shift+PrintScreen did not stop a take while the game had focus, so takes are stopped from the Claude app.

Trailer trial 1 (submission\trailer video\trailer trial 1.mp4, 30.0 s, 1080p60, 20 Mbit/s, no sound)

  • 0 to 10 s: ten one-second hits, each a different moment: the ringed planet and debris field, a nebula flare, a shield pass, a beam volley over the rings, turret fire, a swoop over the rings, the ring station, beams hitting a capital ship, two capital ships against the nebula, an orange beam slash.
  • 10 to 14 s: title "ORION-81" and "A 6DOF TEAM SPACE BATTLE" over a capital ship firing a beam volley.
  • 14 to 26 s: three longer shots, ending on explosions bursting on an enemy capital ship.
  • 26 to 30 s: end card: "EPIC AI MEGAJAM 2026", "Andy · Andrius · Zilvinas", "Made in Unreal Engine 5 · Produced with AI".
  • Cut with Windows' built-in video editing API from PowerShell, so no new software was installed. The scripts are in submission\trailer video\edit, and a re-cut takes about 10 s.

Choices Claude made, for review

  1. Which ten moments open the trailer, and the three longer shots. Three of the ten hits, and the climax, come from take 1 and show the HUD.
  2. The title "ORION-81": the project has no displayed game title.
  3. The subtitle "A 6DOF TEAM SPACE BATTLE" and the end card wording.
  4. No music. The trailer needs a track with a licence that allows it. The jam page notes that the WeLoveIndies free licence covers non-commercial use only.

Tested

  • The file: 1920x1080, 60 fps, 30.0 s, 20.1 Mbit/s.
  • Stills every 0.5 s through the whole trailer and every 0.25 s through the climax: every cut is a different scene, both cards show over moving footage, and no desktop appears.
  • The first cut of the climax ran into the in-game Esc menu (take 1 at 3:12.7). The shot was moved 1.5 s earlier, so it ends on the fireball just before the menu opens.

Not verified

  • Nobody has watched it at full speed with sound off on a phone and a monitor.
  • Not uploaded anywhere yet.

Not captured yet

  • A capital ship being destroyed in one continuous shot, and the win screen. The trailer should end on that loop: launch, fight, kill, win.

AI usage

  • Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: reading the jam rules, setting up and checking the recordings, choosing the shots from stills, the title and end cards, the cut and its checks, and this entry.

Coach tip (topic: trailers)

A trailer's first three seconds decide whether a judge keeps watching, so open on your strongest unique image, not on setup. Cut each one-second hit on motion that is already under way: a beam already crossing the frame or an explosion already blooming. A cut that lands on a still frame reads as a pause.

Coach tip (topic: feature headings in a trailer)

A heading only works if the shot under it proves it. "Fly as a squadron" lands because three shields line up on screen while it is up. If a feature has no shot that shows it, cut the heading rather than leave a claim the footage doesn't back. Judges notice the gap.

15 Nov Trailer musicFour CC0 funk tracks and a listening pageAndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius (find licence-free space funk music, download it next to the trailer videos, and make a page with a play button for each track). Claude chose the sources, the four tracks and the licence bar, so the team should review the shortlist. Which track goes on the trailer is still Andrius's call.

Licence bar

  • CC0 only: the one music licence on the project's allowed list. It allows commercial use with no credit.
  • Left out on purpose: CC-BY (credit required, not on the allowed list), any non-commercial licence (the jam page notes that the WeLoveIndies free licence is non-commercial only), and paid stock libraries.

The shortlist (all from OpenGameArt, licence read on each track's page)

TrackArtistLengthWhy it's on the list
Rhythm Gardencongusbongus3:27Funky electronic dance; the most synth-driven, so the closest to space funk
ConvertGame_assets_664:56Funky and groovy with a sci-fi edge
Midnight CruiserZane Little Music2:24Energetic jazz-funk fusion; another game used it for its Steam launch trailer
Funked UpJoth1:0670s funk loop, just over the trailer's 60 s
  • Left out: Xenocity - Sidekick (labelled CC0, but its author asks commercial users to make contact, so the licence is unclear) and Holizna's Funk collection (CC0, but one 86.5 MB zip). FreePD.com, a large CC0 library, closed in 2025.
  • Files: submission\trailer video\music\, one folder per track, with LICENCES.md (source pages, licences, SHA-256 hashes). Each download matched the size its link announced. Convert came as a zip holding one WAV, which was listed before it was extracted.

Listening page: https://claude.ai/artifact/4oAB9D9FbMFkAZmvK1drHG (private until shared)

  • A play button and a clickable waveform per track, with an amber line at 60 s, the trailer's length. One track plays at a time.
  • Convert plays from an AAC copy of its 50 MB WAV, made with Windows' own encoder. Rhythm Garden stays .ogg, because Windows has no Ogg decoder to convert it; it may not play on an iPhone.

Tested

  • In a local copy of the page in the built-in browser: all four waveforms drew from the real audio, Rhythm Garden played, and starting Convert paused it.
  • Not tested: the published page itself (the built-in browser isn't signed in to claude.ai), and playback on a phone.

AI usage

  • Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: the source search and licence checks, the downloads and hashes, the AAC copy, the listening page and this entry.

Coach tip (topic: trailer music)

Pick the track by its first ten seconds, not its best minute. A judge decides in the opening shots, so the music has to start with energy under your cold open. If a track's best part comes later, start the music from there and let the trailer begin on it.

15 Nov Trailer trial 3Rhythm Garden on the beat, "Orion-81 Space Jam Director's Cut" and the development spanAndriusOpus 5.5

Asked by: Andrius
Who decided: Andrius, four decisions: Rhythm Garden is the trailer music; the music starts at the beginning of the song; the game's official name is Orion-81 Space Jam ("it's not Orion-81"), and the trailer title reads "Orion-81 Space Jam DIRECTOR'S CUT" throughout; the end screen shows when development started and ended, from the game repo's GitHub commits. Claude chose how each looks and where the cuts fall, so the team should review them.

Trailer trial 3 (submission\trailer video\trailer trial 3.mp4, 59.05 s, 1080p60, 20 Mbit/s video, AAC 192 kbit/s stereo)

  • Same shots and feature headings as trial 2, re-timed to the music. Rhythm Garden plays from 0:00. The first shot runs to the song's first downbeat (1.115 s), then seven two-beat hits, then every section is a whole number of half bars, so every cut lands on a beat. Each cut is snapped to the nearest frame, at most 8 ms from its beat.
  • The music fades out over the last 3 s, under the end card.
  • Title and end card: ORION-81 SPACE JAM, then DIRECTOR'S CUT in amber. Trials 1 and 2 were re-rendered with the new name too.
  • End card: "DEVELOPMENT 13 Nov 2026 17:48 UTC – 15 Nov 2026 21:24 UTC · first and latest commit on GitHub · 51 h 35 min". It comes from orion-eighty-one/orion-81-space-jam after a fetch: the first commit (Initial commit, Andrius) and the latest (merge of pull request #58 into develop), 272 commits in all. The repo's name is not on the card, since the trailer will be public.

Mistake caught in testing

  • The first tempo reading was 154.4 BPM, and the first cut of trial 3 used it. Measuring the rendered trailer's own audio gave 115.9 BPM. The two are in a 4:3 ratio. Checking both on the full mix, the kick drum and the hi-hats showed the beat repeats every bar at 116 BPM (2.069 s), and the 0.39 s pulse is a syncopated kick, three-quarters of a beat. The trailer was re-cut on the 116 BPM grid.

Tested

  • The file: 59.05 s, 1920x1080, about 60 fps, AAC stereo at 192 kbit/s.
  • The trailer's own audio, decoded and measured: 116.0 BPM, first downbeat at 1.1153 s; the matching cut is at 1.117 s.
  • 59 stills, one a second, and full-size frames of the title and end cards: every card readable, no desktop or Esc menu frame.

Not verified

  • Nobody has watched or listened at full speed, on a phone or a monitor.
  • Not uploaded. The end time is the latest commit at 21:24 UTC; it goes stale if the team commits again before the upload. Re-run edit\make-cards.ps1 (after git fetch) and edit\render-trial-3.ps1 to bring it up to date (about 20 s).
  • The new name is on the trailer, the cards and the music page only. The game itself (window title, menus, the packaged program's name), the itch.io page and the zip name still need it.

AI usage

  • Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort. No subagents.
  • Used for: decoding the song (Blender's audio library, headless), the tempo, downbeat and section analysis (numpy), the music bed, the re-timed cut, the cards, the commit timestamps, the checks and this entry.

Coach tip (topic: checking your own measurement)

Measure the output, not just the input. The tempo looked right on the source track and was wrong. It only showed up when the finished trailer's audio was measured with the same tool. When a step can't be checked by eye or ear, run the same check on the final file.

How it was merged (Claude's choices, for review)

  • The base is the page as published, so nothing on it can disappear.
  • An entry is the same entry when its heading is the same.
  • A longer version on a branch replaces the page's version only if it starts with the page's text and its added lines are not already elsewhere on the page.
  • New entries go in the order of the branch that holds the most of them (v0.16.2_Zilvinas). The page's own last entries follow them: itch.io and the trailer.
  • Every remote branch (git show <branch>:update-for-devlog.md, after a fetch) and the trailer entry files were compared. No branch was checked out or changed.

About orion-81.io/epic-jam-2026

  • That page is a separate, hand-built website, not this devlog page. Its source is D:\Games\orion-81\orion-81-publish (GitHub Andrius-Bubliauskas/orion-81, served on GitHub Pages as orion-81.io).
  • It is at version 3.4.1 of 12 Nov and shows Day 1 only.
  • Its security policy loads nothing from other sites, so it can't show this page inside it.
  • To bring it up to date, someone has to write Days 2 to 4 into epic-jam-2026/index.html and push. The quickest step is a link from it to this page. Not done in this update.

Still open

  • This page is private. The org wants the devlog shared with the organisation; the owner sets that in the page's Share menu.
  • The merged Markdown is not in the repo. develop's update-for-devlog.md still lacks the itch.io and trailer entries, and Zilvinas's and #60's entries arrive when those branches merge.

AI usage

  • Model: Claude Opus 5.5 (claude-opus-5-5), Claude Code desktop app, session default effort, in a session forked from the packaging session. No subagents.
  • Used for: reading every branch's devlog, the merge script and its checks, the page build, the publish, the look at orion-81.io, this entry.

Coach tip (topic: one devlog, many branches)

A devlog kept in a file on every branch splits as the branches do. Five entries existed only on the published page, and six only on branches. Before the hand-in, merge the devlog into develop once, from the published page plus every open branch, and from then on add entries only on develop.
